# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The project uses Semantic Versioning.

## Unreleased

### Security

- **Recovery staging copies files symlink-safe, closing the ARCH-030 predictable-path symlink escape (SEC-007/#325, integrations).** `backend/slskd.stage_selected_audio_files` — the shared staging sink reached by every recovery caller (`import_service._stage_selected_audio_files`, `acquisition_service`) — previously created each per-file destination with `dest.parent.mkdir` + `shutil.copy2`, both of which FOLLOW symlinks, while the post-`mkdir` containment re-check validated only the top-level `stage` dir, not each per-file dest or the `CD NN` disc subdirs. Because ARCH-030 made the staged folder name PREDICTABLE (`rec-<16hex>`) and added reuse-overwrite, a symlink pre-planted at a dest file or a `CD NN` subdir — by a local writer to the app-private staging dir, or via a race between recovery retries — was followed by `copy2`, letting downloaded bytes overwrite an arbitrary file the app user can write (config, Beets DB, media). The copy loop now mirrors the SEC-007 barrier discipline from `composite_workflows`: every directory below the validated `stage` folder is opened or created one component at a time with `O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW` (a component swapped for a symlink is refused, never followed), and each destination is written to an `O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` temp then atomically `rename`d into place — the rename replaces a dest planted on a deterministic reuse (symlink or file) WITHOUT following it. Staging fails closed on hosts without `os.supports_dir_fd`/`O_NOFOLLOW` (no symlink-following path fallback), consistent with the rest of the barrier. The stale comment that claimed the directory re-check "closes the predictable-path/symlink TOCTOU" is corrected — it validates only the top-level dir; the per-file fd-relative copy is what closes it. No new credentials, env vars, or logged secrets. Tests: `tests/test_sec_arch030_slskd_nofollow.py` (POSIX; symlink pre-planted at a dest file and at a `CD NN` disc subdir is refused/not-followed and its target is untouched), existing `tests/test_slskd_helpers.py` staging coverage unchanged.
- **Recovery staging now descends the intermediate `_beets_missing_import` component fd-relative instead of following it (SEC-007/#325 Finding F1/C5, integrations).** `backend/slskd._staged_copy_nofollow` opened the FULL validated `stage` path (`<downloads>/_beets_missing_import/<prefix>-album`) in a single `os.open(..., O_NOFOLLOW)`; `O_NOFOLLOW` guards only a path's FINAL component, so the intermediate `_beets_missing_import` directory was still followed if it were a symlink — and the `stage.resolve()` containment check in `stage_selected_audio_files` is a separate syscall (TOCTOU). Escaping containment required winning a tight race on the intermediate component with a co-located local writer (outside the single-container threat model), and `resolve()` still blocked an outside target — hence non-blocking — but it did not match the fd discipline already used for the deeper components. The copy now opens the trusted downloads root once (without `O_NOFOLLOW`, as the configured mount, mirroring `_unlink_proven_under`) and descends EVERY component below it — the intermediate `_beets_missing_import` staging dir, the `<prefix>-album` folder and each `CD NN` disc subdir — one at a time with `O_DIRECTORY|O_NOFOLLOW`, failing closed (`RuntimeError`) on any symlinked or non-directory component, and still fails closed on hosts without `os.supports_dir_fd`/`O_NOFOLLOW`. The Finding-A per-file `O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` temp + atomic `rename` plant is unchanged. No credentials, env vars, or logged secrets. Tests: `tests/test_sec_arch030_slskd_nofollow.py` (POSIX; a symlinked intermediate `_beets_missing_import` pointing at an in-root decoy — so `resolve()` passes and only the fd-walk can refuse — is refused and no bytes are written through it; the existing dest-file and `CD NN` cases still pass).
- **Orphan staging cleanup fails closed when the recovery staging root itself is a symlink (SEC-007/#325 Finding B, ARCH-030 Step 4).** `recovery_service._staging_root` resolved `<downloads>/_beets_missing_import` with `os.path.realpath`, which silently FOLLOWS a symlinked root: if `_beets_missing_import` were a symlink (attacker pre-plant or a benign relocation symlink) pointing at the whole `<downloads>` root, `root` became that target and the per-child `realpath`/`commonpath` containment check in `reconcile_orphan_staging_folders` then accepted the target's aged, unowned top-level entries — so the sweep could `delete_file` not-yet-imported completed downloads at the downloads root, contradicting the function's own guarantee never to delete real media outside the staging root. (The live music library stayed protected by `delete_file`'s own MUSIC_ROOT/protected-data refusal.) `_staging_root` now `lstat`s that path (`os.path.islink`, which never follows the final component) and returns `""` — no cleanup — when it is a symlink, so `root` is only ever the genuine staging directory and the existing per-child containment check can only ever match entries truly inside it. The `composite_workflows.delete_file` barrier is unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_step4_gate_and_orphans.py` (`SymlinkedStagingRootTests`).
- **Orphan staging cleanup fails closed on carrier-store uncertainty instead of treating a read error as "unowned" (SEC-007/#325 Finding F8, ARCH-030 Step 4).** `recovery_service.reconcile_orphan_staging_folders` decided a staging folder was deletable from two carrier-store reads — the per-folder `recovery_carrier.load_for_folder` ownership lookup and the `_live_carrier_prefixes()` live-carrier map. Both swallowed errors into a negative result (`bound=None`, empty prefix map), so a transient carrier-store read failure made an aged folder that actually belongs to a LIVE carrier look unowned, and the sweep could `delete_file` it. (The deletion stayed contained to the staging root and recoverable, but it was still an unintended removal of a live recovery's staging folder.) A deletion now requires that BOTH ownership reads positively SUCCEEDED and showed no owner: `_live_carrier_prefixes()` no longer swallows a store-read error (it raises, so an empty return is a trustworthy "no live carriers" rather than an error-empty), the caller records a per-run prefix-read failure, and the per-folder lookup records its own failure; if either read failed, ownership is UNKNOWN and the folder is skipped (never deleted), logging only the exception type and folder name. Adoption and the normal unowned-and-aged cleanup are unchanged. No credentials, env vars, or secrets are involved. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_step4_gate_and_orphans.py` (`test_per_folder_lookup_failure_fails_closed_no_delete`, `test_prefix_store_read_failure_fails_closed_no_delete`).
- **Orphan staging cleanup now protects a queued Review path that is an ancestor or descendant of a scanned folder, not just an exact match (ARCH-030 reconcile-overlap, library-transaction).** `recovery_service.reconcile_orphan_staging_folders` scans TOP-LEVEL staging folders but checked the Review queue with `_pending_review_has_path`, which is exact-path membership only. A queued Review row at a DESCENDANT of a scanned orphan (e.g. a row at `<prefix>-album/CD 02` while the sweep deletes `<prefix>-album`) was unprotected, so an aged unowned parent folder could be deleted out from under a not-yet-imported download. The sweep and `import_service._delete_staged_import_folder` now use a new `pending_review_store._pending_review_has_path_or_overlap`, which keeps the folder when any queued Review path equals, is a descendant of, or is an ancestor of it — boundary-aware (keys are resolved/normalised, so `.../album` never matches `.../album-2`) and still fail-closed (`unknown=True`) on an unreadable queue. Tests: `tests/test_arch030_merge_completion.py` (`PendingReviewOverlapTests`).
- **Recovery staging-root resolution fails closed when the `/proc/self/fd` read-back is unavailable, instead of re-resolving by name (SEC-007/#325, ARCH-030, library-transaction).** `recovery_service._staging_root` resolves the staging dir through a single `O_NOFOLLOW` fd-open and reads its canonical path back from `/proc/self/fd/<fd>`; on an fd-capable host where that read-back failed it fell back to `os.path.realpath(os.path.join(base, name))`, which drops the pinned fd and re-resolves the whole path by name — reopening the TOCTOU window the fd-open had just closed (an intermediate component swapped to a symlink between the open and the realpath would be followed). It now returns `""` (no cleanup) on a read-back failure, keeping `root` only ever the genuine fd-pinned staging directory. Tests: `tests/test_arch030_merge_completion.py` (`StagingRootReadbackFailClosedTests`).

### Fixed

- **The download-time identity gate now DEFERS a genuine AcoustID `NO_RESULT` recovery file to the slot tier instead of deleting it before it can get there (ARCH-030 C1, integrations).** `acquisition_service._validate_wanted_download_identity_before_import` set `match["ok"]=False` for a file whose AcoustID lookup answered empty (`NO_RESULT`) exactly as for wrong audio, so `best_idx<0` and `composite_workflows.delete_file` removed it — and, when the whole download was `NO_RESULT` (the 112 "Forever" intro/outro anchor case), the staged folder became empty, `_first_staged_folder_with_audio` returned `""`, and the `DETERMINISTIC_METADATA_SLOT` tier shipped in Step 6 was UNREACHABLE in the real flow: the feature could not complete end to end for its own anchor case. The gate now classifies a non-accepted file from its AcoustID identity status: a genuine `NO_RESULT` (answered-empty — the file may be the correct intro/outro, and `NO_RESULT` is exactly what the slot gate requires) or an `UNAVAILABLE` lookup (not configured / rejected key / outage / rate limit / timeout / fpcalc missing — not negative evidence) is LEFT STAGED, not deleted; only a `CONFLICT` (AcoustID returned a candidate that contradicts the wanted recording/RG) or a format-rejected file is still deleted. When no file fingerprint-accepts inline but one or more were deferred, `_import_downloaded_album` does NOT run the inline Beets import on unverified audio — it leaves the carrier STAGED (an advancer state, file on disk) so the scheduled advancer's `DETERMINISTIC_METADATA_SLOT` gate — which remains the sole auto-insert authority and still must pass — decides accept-vs-review downstream. No gate is weakened: the fail-closed "a file we decided to delete that cannot be removed → nothing imports" guard is preserved, and the validator still raises when nothing verified AND nothing was deferred. Tests: `tests/test_arch030_noresult_download_defer.py` (drives the real download → validate → stage → advance path: a `NO_RESULT` file survives staging and the slot-tier folder resolver finds it; a `CONFLICT` file is still deleted).
- **Bare-release-ID missing-track recoveries no longer mint duplicate carriers/folders (ARCH-030 C9, integrations).** `acquisition_service.start_album_download` computed the recovery dedup/resume identity key from the requested rgid, but `create_carrier` keys on the RESOLVED canonical rgid. A client that supplied only a release id (`mb_albumid`, no `mb_releasegroupid`) is indistinguishable by UUID shape from a release-group id, so the key used the release id while the carrier was created under the real Release Group — `get_by_identity` missed it and a retry minted a DUPLICATE carrier/folder. A recovery with no explicit `mb_releasegroupid` now resolves the release's canonical Release Group up front (`helpers_mb._fetch_mb_release_group_strict`) so the dedup key, the identity-match guard and `create_carrier` all agree; it fails open to the bare id when MusicBrainz cannot answer (worst case: a lost resume optimisation). An explicit `mb_releasegroupid` and every non-recovery keep the existing network-free path. Tests: `tests/test_arch030_noresult_download_defer.py`.
- **(C10, documented)** The pre-lock `get_by_identity` dedup read in `start_album_download` runs before the guarded job's identity workflow lock, so two concurrent duplicate requests can both read "no carrier" and mint a fresh uuid; this is benign — the job workflow is keyed on the same stable identity slug and `fail_fast_in_process` refuses the second job, so only one `_do` runs and only one carrier is ever created. Marked in code with a `ponytail:` comment naming the residual race; moving the read under the job-engine guard is not a small, safe change and is unnecessary given the lock already serialises creation.
- **An authorized (or human-confirmed) `NO_RESULT` missing-track recovery now completes the merge into the existing album instead of stranding a duplicate album row (ARCH-030 B4, library-transaction).** The post-import AcoustID gate `import_service._validate_wanted_album_items_with_acoustid` refused on `no_result` unconditionally, so even the headline 112 `NO_RESULT` "Forever" anchor case — a slot the matching gate had already authorized (`matching_decision.action_allowed=True`, `VERIFIED_SUBSET` / `DETERMINISTIC_METADATA_SLOT` under the Option C double-corroboration policy, docs/adr/0007) — was imported by Beets and then REFUSED the merge, leaving the downloaded files as a separate, stranded album row next to the existing album. The gate now HONORS that already-set authorization for exactly the authorized wanted subset: when the folder-bound carrier is `action_allowed=True` (read, never recomputed, via the new consume-only `_recovery_slot_authorized`), an AcoustID `no_result` for the authorized tracks no longer blocks the merge, so the fill lands on the existing album (fs move + DB merge) on both the unattended advancer path and the human-confirmed manual-review path (where, under Option C, most recovery tracks go). Everything stronger still refuses even when authorized — a different-song `mismatch`, an infra `not_checked` failure, and any file that maps to no requested track (`unverified`, i.e. outside the authorized subset) — and an unauthorized/unverified recovery still goes to Review unchanged. Completion is still proven fs+db (the existing read-back on the existing album), so nothing is marked imported until the files are actually merged. Tests: `tests/test_arch030_merge_completion.py` (an integration test reaching the REAL merge: an authorized `NO_RESULT` slot merges with no stranded row, while an unauthorized one still goes to Review; plus the validator subset-boundary and `_recovery_slot_authorized` cases).

### Changed

- **The missing-track recovery advancer now runs automatically on the job scheduler, and its keep-in-review fallback is a compare-and-set (ARCH-030 F6/F9, backend).** F6: the lifecycle advancer (`recovery_service.advance_stuck_carriers` / `start_recovery_advance_job`) previously ran ONLY when something POSTed `/api/import/recovery/advance` — there was no scheduler, no acquisition handoff and no frontend caller — so a carrier a download left stuck in `DOWNLOADED`/`verifying`/`review_required` never advanced on its own and requirement 8 ("download → evaluate → auto-import when safe, else retain target identity and report why") was not met end-to-end. A new `recovery_service.start_recovery_advance_schedule()` registers the SAME bounded, guarded advancer on the canonical job scheduler (`jobs.every`, the hook maintenance's auto-scan already uses — not an ad-hoc thread), registered once at startup from `app.py`; each tick starts the one advancer job, `jobs.every` skips a tick while the returned job still runs, and the job stays workflow-locked + `dedupe_key`-guarded on `recovery-advance`, so the sweep never overlaps itself across threads, processes or restarts and never creates a duplicate import / retry storm. The trigger ONLY re-evaluates carriers against the matching contract; the `action_allowed=True` gate in `start_folder_import_with_id` (Step 4) remains the one and only auto-insert authority, and a carrier the contract does not authorize keeps its target identity and stays in review with the contract's reason. Cadence is `RECOVERY_ADVANCE_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default 120s; `<= 0` disables the schedule, the manual POST endpoint still works). F9 (CAS race): the fallback that keeps a non-advanceable carrier in review used a blind `advance_state(cid, "review_required")` with no `expected_revision`, so a concurrent CLAIM (`-> importing`) that landed during the slow preflight was clobbered back to review; it now re-reads the carrier, only transitions one that is STILL advanceable (never dragging an already-claimed/terminal carrier back), and compare-and-sets on the re-read revision — on a CAS miss it skips rather than overwrites, leaving the next sweep to re-evaluate. No new job type, mutation sink, credentials or env secrets. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_arch030_advancer_scheduling.py`.
- **The recovery insert authority now consumes the `DETERMINISTIC_METADATA_SLOT` tier, so a genuine AcoustID `NO_RESULT` missing-track recovery is proven instead of stalling in review (ARCH-030 Step 6, music-identity).** `backend/matching_service._missing_track_recording_proof` previously required a per-file *confirmed* AcoustID recording, so the anchor case — 112 "Forever" (RG `37cb4a72`) missing #1 "Forever (Intro)" and #9 "For Us (Outro)", both fingerprinting to `NO_RESULT` (genuinely absent, not an outage) — never satisfied the proof and stayed in review forever even though the matching contract already authorized it (F2). The proof now accepts a `NO_RESULT` file that the shared contract's own alignment proves is a deterministic metadata slot (exact normalized title + exact release-track position + measured duration within the ADR-0007 7.0s tolerance + validated Release Group + matching artist), resolving the filled slot from the contract's alignment rather than inventing a second, looser definition, and it agrees with the contract's `action_allowed` (VERIFIED_SUBSET) — it never authorizes a `FULL_RELEASE`. The fingerprint gate is NOT weakened: `UNAVAILABLE`, `CONFLICT` and `AMBIGUOUS` are still refused (only answered-empty `NO_RESULT` can qualify), and a `NO_RESULT` file with no measured duration fails closed to review. The slot's measured duration is fpcalc-derived (surfaced from the AcoustID answer's evidence and preserved across the file-level cache), never a local media-tag read. Separately (F7, identity robustness), `backend/matching/evidence.evaluate_release_group_candidate` now treats a downloaded file whose OWN embedded Release-Group/artist identity CONTRADICTS the target as a conflict (`file_release_group_conflict` / `file_artist_conflict`) routed to review — so a file self-identifying as an unrelated release (e.g. FLEUR RG `2da91738`) proposed into a 112 destination (RG `37cb4a72`) can never return `action_allowed=True`; the embedded tag is used only to DETECT the contradiction and fail closed, never to reconstruct identity. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_arch030_slot_wiring.py`.
- **The matching contract's `action_allowed` is the only authorization to auto-insert a recovery, and orphaned staging folders are safely adopted or cleaned up (ARCH-030 Step 4, library-transaction).** `import_service.start_folder_import_with_id` now treats an UNATTENDED recovery auto-insert — marked by `recovery_carrier_id`, which the recovery advancer sets and a human Review confirm never does — as authorized ONLY when the matching contract attached to the folder's carrier has `action_allowed=True`. It consumes that server-side boolean from the carrier's stored `matching_decision` (it never recomputes a more permissive one) and resolves the carrier from the FOLDER binding, not the client id, so a client cannot aim an authorized carrier at an arbitrary folder. On `action_allowed!=True` (including a missing/empty decision — fail-closed) it refuses synchronously, before any job starts or any file is touched, returns `recovery_action_not_allowed` (409), and keeps the carrier in manual review with the contract's reason. A `VERIFIED_SUBSET` authorization still routes only the carrier's wanted subset through the existing existing-album missing-track merge, never a whole-release import. Human-confirmed Review imports (no `recovery_carrier_id`) are unchanged — the human is the authorization. Separately, a new bounded, best-effort sweep (`recovery_service.reconcile_orphan_staging_folders`, folded into the advancer job) reconciles orphaned non-terminal staging folders left under `_beets_missing_import` by the pre-Step-3 per-attempt scheme: a folder still bound to — or whose deterministic `rec-<16hex>` prefix matches — a live carrier is ADOPTED (re-bound, never re-minted), and a folder owned by no carrier and old enough is CLEANED UP through the controlled staging-delete path (`composite_workflows.delete_file`, LT-13). **Security (SEC-007/#325):** every path is pre-verified fail-closed (realpath + `commonpath` containment inside the validated staging root, symlinked children skipped) and `delete_file` independently re-enforces containment (inside a staging root, never a staging root itself, never `MUSIC_ROOT` or protected data, no symlink component, fd-relative, lstat-rechecked) — so the live music library is never touched and no real media outside the staging root is ever deleted. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_step4_gate_and_orphans.py`.
- **Import Review renders a redesigned recovery card that shows which tracks are missing, keeps the carrier's canonical album as the one primary identity, and reports per-dimension confidence + honest provider status (ARCH-030 Step 5, frontend).** Carrier-backed recovery items (`evidence.recovery_carrier`) now render a dedicated `RecoveryReviewCard` with a WANTED / DOWNLOADED / WHAT-NEEDS-CHECKING / NEXT information architecture and exactly ONE primary action, replacing the old identity panel + stage pair. **Which tracks are missing (req 3)** is shown explicitly: expected-track titles with track/disc numbers, clickable MusicBrainz recording links and the canonical Release Group link, expected-vs-present counts, and a per-track status chip (imported / verified / downloaded / pending) read straight from the backend packet — never guessed from a folder name. **Expected vs suggested (req 6)** are separated: the intended destination album (the carrier's canonical `mb_releasegroupid` + `expected_tracks`) is the primary identity and the single primary action's target, taken directly from the carrier and never chosen by candidate ranking; AI/alternative candidates render in a clearly-separated, non-primary "Alternative suggestions" section, so an unrelated title-collision (e.g. FLEUR "FOREVER FOREVER" when the target is 112 "Forever") can never be auto-selected or shown as the primary action. **Per-dimension confidence + provider chips (req 7):** the five `matching_decision` dimensions (identifier syntax, MusicBrainz lookup, album identity, downloaded-recording verification, import eligibility) render separately rather than as one blob; provider-status chips read AcoustID "no match in database" differently from "not configured", and the AI chip shows not-run / rejected and NEVER surfaces AI confidence when the AI provider did not actually contribute (`ai_status.contributed=false`, or a rejected/unauthenticated key). The single primary action follows the product direction "auto when deterministic evidence is strong; ELSE manual": when the matching contract allows auto-import (`action_allowed=true`) it is the automatic "Import recovered track" action; when it does not, the same single slot becomes a visibly-distinct human-review "Review & import manually" confirm (F5) that routes through the EXISTING human Review import path (page `startApply` → confirm dialog → `runApply`, carrying no `recovery_carrier_id`, so it is human-authorized and still fully server-side validated) — it never flips the auto-gate and never targets an alternative/title-collision candidate (the carrier's canonical Release Group stays the only confirmed target). Provenance and the destination path are collapsed into an expandable details block. `frontend/src/api/types.ts` widens `matching_decision` to the contract-v3 shape (top-level `identity_proof`, the five `dimensions`, `action_allowed`) and adds the `recovery_carrier` packet fields (`tracks`, `provenance`, `matching_decision`, `confidence`, `review_reason`, `recovery_identity_key`), an `AiStatus` type on the ai-suggest response, and `provider_health` on the health-detail response. Read-only: the frontend consumes backend fields present since Step 3 and changes no backend shape. Tests: `frontend/tests/RecoveryReviewCard.test.tsx`.
- **Missing-track recovery reuses one staged folder per recovery, records durable download provenance, and reports provider validity honestly (ARCH-030 Step 3, integrations).** The staging layer now derives a **deterministic** folder prefix from the stable recovery-identity key (`rec-<16 hex>`), so a retry of the same recovery REUSES one `_beets_missing_import/<prefix>-<album>` folder (slskd path) and one `<artist>/<album> - <source> missing <prefix>` folder (direct/yt-dlp path) instead of minting a fresh uuid/timestamp family each attempt — the duplicate-folder storm behind the 38-item "Pending AI" queue. The prefix is threaded through `acquisition_service` and `backend/import_service._stage_selected_audio_files` (which self-derives it from a carrier bound to the source folder when not passed, so every staging call site gets the reuse behaviour), and a deterministic re-stage REPLACES the prior copy rather than accumulating `name.1` siblings. **Security (SEC-007/#325):** the now-predictable staged name is sanitised to a bounded `[0-9A-Za-z._-]` token (a fixed-length hex-of-a-hash, never raw identity text) and, after `mkdir`, the real staged path is re-checked to be a non-symlink directory still contained under the validated downloads root — closing the predictable-path/symlink TOCTOU a random uuid name previously masked (enforced for both callers; the direct path keeps the existing `_download_dest_under` root-containment). The carrier `source` provenance is extended additively to a durable record: provider/method and the **discovery query** at create, plus the **chosen result reference** (resolved MB release + staged folder) and **per-file source** once the download lands (folded onto the existing carrier transitions — no extra lifecycle write). `GET /api/health/detail` now surfaces `provider_boundary.provider_health()` as a structured `provider_health` field (last classified outcome per provider, already redacted), kept separate from the env-presence `checks` booleans — so a present-but-rejected AI/Lidarr/Discogs/slskd/Plex key no longer reads as healthy. Item AI-suggest (`POST /api/items/<id>/ai-suggest`) now emits a structured `ai_status` (configured vs validly reachable, with the boundary's last outcome, which knows a 401-rejected key) and a `confidence_source`, and never attributes the MusicBrainz/AcoustID fallback confidence to AI when the AI provider did not actually respond — so the UI can show a provider-status chip instead of a misleading "AI low". `matching_decision.ai` is unchanged (its boundary default is correct by design). No new credentials or env vars; no secret/token is logged or persisted. Tests: `tests/test_mtr3_integration.py`, `tests/test_mtr_acquisition_carrier.py`, `tests/test_missing_import_single_staging.py`.
- **Carrier-bound recovery seeds MusicBrainz candidates from the canonical Release Group, not folder/tag/filename title text (ARCH-030 Step 2.5).** When a recovery/repair pins the carrier's canonical `mb_releasegroupid`, the AI suggestion path (`ai_service._ai_suggest_album_internal`) now starts its candidate pool from that Release Group's own releases — a new `rgid:` binding seed on `helpers_mb._mb_release_search` — instead of a free `release:"<title>"` text search. This closes the remaining vector behind requirement 4 ("do not allow generic MusicBrainz title matching to replace a known target album"): a title-collision release on an unrelated Release Group (e.g. FLEUR "FOREVER FOREVER" vs 112 "Forever", RG 2da91738 vs 37cb4a72) is now **neither seeded nor** ranked primary, composing with the existing Step-1 relatedness veto (which the seed makes a no-op since no unrelated RG is in the pool). On an empty rgid search (search-index lag) the fallback stays RG-bound: `musicbrainz_service._mb_release_search_by_folder_tracks` gains an optional `release_group_id` that scopes every recording query with `rgid:` and stamps the resulting candidates with that RG (binding, not reconstruction), rather than a free title match that could land on another group. The canonical RG is only ever taken from the carrier — never reconstructed from the folder/job/filename. A genuine new-album import (no carrier RG) is unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_recovery_rg_seeding.py`.

### Fixed

- **The deterministic-metadata-slot auto-accept gate now uses a STRICT, version-preserving canonical-title identity check, so a live take / remaster / alt-take / edit can no longer silently fill a bare-title slot (ARCH-030 F4, music-identity).** `backend/matching/models.TrackAssignment.is_deterministic_metadata_slot` gated the title on `title_similarity >= 0.999`, but `title_similarity` is the variant-MAXIMIZED ranking score from `track_alignment._title_similarity`, which deliberately strips `(Live)`/remaster/alt-take/`feat.`/parenthetical text (`normalize.title_variants`). So a wrong recording like "Song (Live 2019)" normalized to the bare target "Song" and scored 1.0 >= 0.999; combined with exact position + duration within the 7.0s tolerance + AcoustID `NO_RESULT` it reached `can_auto_accept(VERIFIED_SUBSET)` -> `action_allowed=True` -> an UNATTENDED import of the wrong audio, looser than docs/adr/0007 guarantees. The slot precondition now compares the version-PRESERVING canonical target title against the local title's version-preserving canonical forms (new `normalize.canonical_title_identity_variants`, which keeps every version/recording marker and removes only non-identity cruft — a trailing source-ID hash and an optional leading track-number prefix) and requires an exact match; a version/recording distinction that is present on the file but absent on the target now fails closed to review. `_title_similarity`/`title_similarity` are unchanged for ordinary candidate ranking. The genuine exact-title intro/outro recovery (112 "Forever (Intro)" / "For Us (Outro)") still slots. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_deterministic_slot.py` (`VersionStrippedTitleCannotAutoPassTests`).
- **The F7 embedded-contradiction guard is now effective on the recovery preflight path, not just the contract (ARCH-030 F5/C2, music-identity).** The preflight candidate rows built in `backend/matching_service._folder_release_preflight` (`_add_candidate`) and `_missing_track_recording_proof` dropped each file's embedded Release-Group/artist identity, so the F7 guard in `backend/matching/evidence.evaluate_release_group_candidate` (which reads `embedded_release_group_id`/`embedded_artist_id` on each local track) never fired on the real advancer path and a mistagged file self-identifying as a different Release Group could reach the slot's `not conflicts` precondition unseen. Engine/tag-supplied embedded RG/artist IDs are now threaded into both row constructions, so an inspected staged file whose embedded RG contradicts the carrier target is a `file_release_group_conflict` and the preflight refuses to auto-accept (identity_conflict). Absence of embedded identity (the common fingerprint-only fresh-download recovery case, where no tag is read) stays neutral — it is never a positive and cannot by itself unlock auto-accept; that path is held by the now-strict F4 title gate + exact position + duration + genuine `NO_RESULT` + fingerprint-conflict detection. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_arch030_slot_wiring.py` (`EmbeddedContradictionBlocksPreflightSlot`).
- **Carrier-bound MusicBrainz folder-track search no longer fabricates target-Release-Group membership for foreign releases (ARCH-030 C6, music-identity).** `backend/musicbrainz_service._mb_release_search_by_folder_tracks` scoped each recording query with `rgid:<RG>` and then stamped `mb_releasegroupid=<RG>` on EVERY release of every matched recording. The `rgid:` filter scopes which RECORDINGS match, but a matched recording's `releases` array lists every release it appears on — including releases in OTHER Release Groups (compilations, alternate editions) — so foreign releases were falsely labeled as the target RG, directly producing the "incorrect album suggestions" the recovery redesign exists to fix. Each release is now kept only when its OWN `release-group` id equals the target RG; a release whose release-group id is absent/unverifiable is dropped (fail closed), never fabricated into the target RG. Unscoped (no carrier RG) generic matching is unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_recovery_rg_seeding.py` (`test_foreign_compilation_release_is_not_surfaced_as_target_rg`, `test_release_without_verifiable_rg_is_dropped_when_scoped`).

## v0.14.0 - 2026-10-26

### Upgrade Notes

- **AI review/history state moves to the data volume (ARCH-034).** `ai_match_history.json`, `recent_imports.json` and `ai_review_decisions.json` now live under `WEB_MANAGER_DATA_DIR` (default `/web-manager-data`), next to `ai_batch_jobs/`, instead of under `/config` (the Beets config mount). On the first start after upgrade each file is moved from its old `/config` location to the new one automatically and exactly once — existing import/match history is preserved, a file already present at the new location is never overwritten, and on any move error the old file is left in place rather than lost. No action is required; keep `WEB_MANAGER_DATA_DIR` on the data volume. A fresh install starts with these files empty, as before.
- **Restart or recreate Web Manager after upgrading** to reload its embedded Beets plugin. The webmanager plugin moves from `1.18.5` to `1.19.0`: it adds the durable import/album-row rollback operations and the exact-path item restore used by artist-folder rollback. No config or library migration is required. Older transactions that lack before-state refuse rollback with `rollback_unavailable` instead of being reported as restored.

### Security

- **Pillow upgraded 12.3.0 → 13.0.0, clearing all 82 open Dependabot alerts.** Every open Dependabot alert on the repository (10 critical, 46 high, 26 moderate) was a Pillow advisory with no fix in the 12.x line; 13.0.0 is the first patched version for all of them. Pillow is only ever used to validate/measure uploaded or provider-fetched artwork bytes (`backend/artwork_service.py`, `beetsplug/webmanager/art_ops.py`, `backend/transaction_engine.py` format sniffing) via `Image.open(formats=...)`, `MAX_IMAGE_PIXELS`, `verify()`, `init()`/`OPEN`, `n_frames`/`is_animated` and `DecompressionBombWarning` — all unchanged in 13.0.0. The only breaking change that applies is the drop of Python 3.10; the runtime image is Python 3.12 and the test image Python 3.13, so no code change was required. The single pin lives in `requirements.txt`; `requirements-dev.txt` inherits it via `-r requirements.txt`, which is why Dependabot attributed the alerts to both manifests.

- **Rollback and health-detail responses no longer echo raw exception text (SEC-007; CodeQL py/stack-trace-exposure #1433/#1443/#1444/#1445).** Four sinks returned stringified caught exceptions to the client: the recover-import rollback route (`routes_maintenance.api_transaction_rollback`, the `(KeyError, ValueError)` branch), the recover-import rollback executor's lookup-failed and execution-failed `error` fields (`backend/transaction_engine.rollback_recover_import`, surfaced to the client through `_rollback_dispatch_body`), and the embedded-Beets config/library self-test messages returned by `GET /api/health/detail` (`backend/beets_embedded.py`). Each now logs the full detail server-side (and keeps it in the transaction's persisted operator-only logs where one exists) and returns only a bounded message plus the exception's type name — never its text, which could carry internal paths or config contents. The 11 `py/path-injection` alerts in the webmanager rollback ops (`restore_ops`/`replace_ops`/`remove_ops`/`art_ops`) were reviewed as false positives: every request-derived path flows through `engine_common._manifest_dir`, which accepts only a `[0-9a-f]{32}` id and re-verifies containment with `realpath`+`commonpath`, so request-driven traversal is impossible. A Codex second-opinion review additionally prompted defense-in-depth hardening of the same ops (exploiting the remaining gaps needs write access to the engine-private quarantine dir under `/config`): the manifest writers now open their `.tmp` with `O_NOFOLLOW`, and `remove_ops`/`replace_ops` validate manifest-derived move sources with resolved containment instead of a lexical prefix check. Tests: `tests/test_sec007_recover_rollback_route.py`, `tests/test_recover_uploads.py`, `tests/test_beets_embedded.py`, `tests/test_sec007_manifest_write_nofollow.py`, `tests/test_webmanager_quarantine_remove.py`.
- **Missing-track recovery resume cannot be steered by a client-supplied carrier id (ARCH-030).** `acquisition_service.start_album_download` validated the incoming `recovery_carrier_id` only as 32 hex characters and otherwise trusted it: a crafted/stale id belonging to a *different* in-flight recovery flowed into `recovery_carrier.create_carrier` (an unconditional upsert) and reset that victim carrier's `checkpoint`→`CREATED` and `verification_evidence`→`{}` while replacing its `existing_album_id`/`mb_releasegroupid`/folder, and the IMPORTED/PLEX_SYNCED resume short-circuit returned the victim's completed state as a false `imported` success for the attacker's unrelated request. Cross-album/cross-recovery imports stayed blocked by the merge album-equality gate, the CAS claim and the live-album precheck; this closes the carrier-row integrity gap underneath them. The resume path now cross-checks the stored carrier's identity against the request — same `existing_album_id`, and same canonical `mb_releasegroupid` when the request names one (ADR-0002) — before honoring the id: on any mismatch (or a carrier that cannot be loaded) the incoming id is ignored and a fresh uuid is minted, so a stolen id can neither clobber another carrier nor drive the short-circuit or the victim's guarded-workflow slug. A legitimately-matching resume now reuses its carrier through the non-resetting `advance_state` CAS instead of the resetting upsert, so its durable checkpoint and verification evidence survive the resume. The short-circuit additionally requires the completed carrier to actually cover the requested track(s) before reporting already-imported, and a transient carrier-read failure on a genuine resume surfaces a retryable error instead of silently re-downloading/re-importing a possibly-complete recovery. The `auto_import=false` Review hand-off is now fail-closed: when the enqueue fails, the carrier (excluded from the AI batch) is marked a recoverable `failed` and the job returns a non-success `review_handoff_failed` result rather than a bare success that would strand the folder. Separately, `recovery_carrier.advance_state` now ignores (with a logged warning) the reserved `payload_extra` keys `checkpoint`/`verification_evidence`/`recovery_workflow_id`, so those durable fields can only be set through its dedicated validated kwargs (the checkpoint allow-list / evidence slot) and never smuggled in unchecked by a future caller. No credentials, tokens or secrets are involved; the warning logs only key names and the carrier id. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_acquisition_carrier.py`, `tests/test_mtr_backend_complete.py`, `tests/test_recovery_carrier.py`.

- **Missing-track recovery resume closes its remaining fail-open integrity edges (ARCH-030, round 3).** Five residual edges in the security-sensitive resume path of `acquisition_service.start_album_download` are now fail-closed: (1) a transient carrier-read error inside the create step no longer falls through to `recovery_carrier.create_carrier`'s resetting upsert — it logs only the exception type and skips creation, so a read blip can never reset a possibly-existing carrier's `checkpoint`/`verification_evidence` (re-opening the Finding-A clobber under a read error); (2) the `auto_import=false` Review hand-off now also fails closed when the carrier cannot be reloaded (not only when the enqueue returns falsy) — the carrier-bound folder is excluded from the AI batch, so a `None`/raised reload is marked recoverable `failed` and the job returns `review_handoff_failed` instead of a bare success that strands the folder; (3) a track with neither a recording id nor a known position is treated as UNPROVEN and never compares as COVERED, so a title-only/unknown-position request can no longer be reported a false already-imported for the same album + Release Group; (4) the requested canonical Release Group is resolved from the same field(s) the handler already accepts (a release-group reference folded into `mb_albumid`, not only `mb_releasegroupid`), so the identity cross-check is never silently downgraded to album-only (the Release Group stays canonical — a release id is never substituted, ADR-0002); and (5) a same-album + same-Release-Group resume whose requested track scope differs from the stored carrier's now mints a fresh uuid instead of reusing a sibling carrier's stale `expected_tracks`. Track matching now bridges recording-id and (disc, position) signatures so a legitimate resume still reuses its carrier. No credentials, tokens or secrets are involved; logs carry only exception type names and the carrier id. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_resume_coverage.py`.
- **Import Review keeps only the identity a client proposes, never its evidence.** `POST /api/folders/import-with-id` stored the request's whole `ai_suggestion`: when the import was refused, its `preflight`, `track_mapping`, `mb_valid`, `review_evidence` and `folder_signature` went into the Review queue as if the server had computed them, bypassing the rule `POST /api/ai-pending-review` already enforced. Both routes now keep only a proposed identity (Release, Release Group, artist, album, year, label, country, reason) and bounded display-only origin labels, whose folder is always the validated one; revalidation recomputes everything else. An `ai_suggestion` that is not an object is ignored instead of failing the job.

### Changed

- **AI review/history state files live under `WEB_MANAGER_DATA_DIR`, not `/config` (ARCH-034).** `ai_match_history.json`, `recent_imports.json` and `ai_review_decisions.json` now default to `WEB_MANAGER_DATA_DIR/<name>` (like `ai_batch_jobs/` and `recovery_carrier`), with a one-time, no-data-loss migration from the old `/config` location on first start (`ai_service._migrate_legacy_config_state`; see Upgrade Notes). Tests: `tests/test_arch034_state_migration.py`.

### Added

- **Import Review marks a stored AI suggestion stale when its folder changed (ARCH-034).** `GET /api/import/review-queue` now sets `suggestion_stale` on each pending row from the same folder-signature check the unattended import uses (`import_review_service._import_review_suggestion_is_stale`), and the Review page shows a "Suggestion stale" badge on such an item (hidden once a fresh candidate is selected). It is display-only — Apply still requires a fresh Find Match or a validated ID with a fresh target preview, unchanged. Tests: `frontend/tests/ImportReviewSuggestionStale.test.tsx`.
- **Import Review shows a 7-stage Download-Missing recovery status indicator (ARCH-030 Phase-6, frontend).** A carrier-backed Review item now renders the recovery workflow's position as the 7 linear stages — Missing → Searching → Downloading → Verifying → Importing → Plex Sync → Complete — as an ordered list with prior stages marked done, the current one highlighted, and later ones upcoming, positioned by the backend's `stage_index` (never guessed). The two off-path terminals (`needs_review`, `failed`) render as a distinct attention/error badge rather than a linear step. It consumes the backend status contract read-only from `evidence.recovery_carrier.recovery_stage` (`backend/recovery_carrier.py::workflow_stage`): a best-effort Plex failure stays at the Plex Sync step as an in-progress/attention state (the import is already durable — not a red error), Plex skipped-by-config or not-configured reads as Complete with the reason in the `note`, and the indicator never looks falsely "successful"/green until the workflow is genuinely Complete. The `note` qualifier is surfaced compactly. The indicator is screen-reader intelligible — an ordered list with `aria-current="step"` and a `role="status"` summary announcing "step N of 7: <label>", with color always paired with a text label and marker (not color-only) — and resilient to a missing/partial `recovery_stage` (older carriers), rendering nothing instead of crashing. New `RecoveryStageIndicator` in `frontend/src/features/importReview/MbidLink.tsx`; `RecoveryStage` type added to `frontend/src/api/types.ts` mirroring the contract exactly. Tests: `frontend/tests/RecoveryStageIndicator.test.tsx`.
- **Import Review renders missing-track recovery truthfully (ARCH-030, frontend).** A carrier-backed Review item now shows a visually distinct "Missing-track recovery" panel — "Recovery of &lt;album&gt; — track N", the existing target album (with its library `album_id`) and its canonical Release-Group MBID, the supporting Release and each expected track's Recording ID — instead of looking like a brand-new full-album import. Every MusicBrainz ID on the card goes through a shared `MbidLink` component (`frontend/src/features/importReview/MbidLink.tsx`) that shows the full UUID, links to the correct MB entity (`release-group`/`release`/`recording`/`artist`) in a new tab only after validating the UUID and entity, offers a one-click Copy button, labels the id by type, and carries a Verified / Suggested / Conflicting status. Recovery identities are shown as **Suggested** (unverified) — an unverified suggestion is never labelled as a confirmed match — because the carrier reaches Review precisely because it could not auto-resolve. The item reads `evidence.recovery_carrier` (falling back to the flattened `recovery_*` fields on the suggestion); it consumes the backend contract read-only. The Delete Folder action is grouped in a visually separated destructive zone and still requires the existing explicit confirmation. Tests: `frontend/tests/MbidLink.test.tsx`.
- **Every MusicBrainz ID on the Import Review page now routes through `MbidLink`, not just the recovery panel (ARCH-030, frontend).** The remaining non-recovery MBID displays in `frontend/src/features/importReview/ImportReviewPage.tsx` — the item-level canonical Release-Group ID, the current Release ID shown on a "needs release group" row, the candidate-carousel release group / representative release, the recording-evidence panel's Suggested Recording / Release / Release Group IDs, each linked-release id, and the recording-attach confirmation id — previously rendered as bare `<a>`/mono text with no UUID validation, Copy button, type label or status. They now use the shared `MbidLink`, so the addendum ("every MBID shown is the full UUID, clickable to the correct MB entity in a new tab with `rel="noreferrer noopener"`, has a Copy button, is typed, carries a Verified / Suggested / Conflicting status, and is validated before linking") holds across the whole Review page. The canonical `mb_releasegroupid` stays labelled as the Release Group and is never swapped for a Release id (ADR-0002). Status defaults to the neutral **Suggested** and is only **Conflicting** where the attach candidate carries conflicts; no **Verified** state is invented from syntax validity (see the truthfulness fix under Fixed). MusicBrainz navigation buttons that show a label (not a UUID) and the "submit a new release at musicbrainz.org/release/add" instructional link are unchanged, as they display no MBID. Tests: `frontend/tests/ImportReviewMbidMigration.test.tsx` (correct per-entity URL, neutral-vs-verified status, an invalid id never linked, and the `handleSuggest` reachable-"no match" warning-not-success path).
- **Missing-track recovery identity carrier (Phase-2 spine, ARCH-030).** New `backend/recovery_carrier.py` is a durable, schema-versioned, compare-and-swap SQLite store (under `<WEB_MANAGER_DATA_DIR>/recovery_carriers`, `RECOVERY_CARRIER_STATE_DIR`, modelled on `ai_batch_state_store`) that binds a staged missing-track download back to the album it is recovering a track for, so the identity survives process restarts and job splits and is read back as structured data — never parsed from a folder name. Phase-1 proved the pipeline carried that identity only in the live download job's closure/metadata and reduced it to the `allow_existing` boolean + a free-text suggestion at the Review hand-off, so a single downloaded track was treated as a brand-new full-album import and failed full-tracklist preflight. Each carrier holds `existing_album_id`, the **canonical** `mb_releasegroupid` (ADR-0002; the supporting Release is stored separately in `release_id` and is never substituted for the Release Group), the expected recording/release-track (`recording_id`/`track`/`disc`/`duration`), `destination_album_path`, `wanted_tracks`, the download `source`/method + provenance, `origin_job_id`, a `verification_evidence` slot and the recovery/checkpoint state for one guarded, resumable recovery workflow (`recovery_workflow_id`, reusing `job_engine` + `transaction_recovery`/`rollback_guard`). The spine creates the carrier at download (`acquisition_service.start_album_download`), binds it to the staged folder, and threads the structured identity into the Review hand-off (`pending_review_store._queue_folder_for_manual_review`) in place of the lossy boolean; the AI-batch scan, pending-review and preflight can resolve the album identity by `recovery_carrier.load_for_folder`. Published accessors for the downstream specialist phases: `create_carrier(...)`, `load_for_folder(path)` / `load_for_job(job_id)`, and `advance_state(carrier_id, state, checkpoint=…, evidence=…)` (checkpointed transitions for the recovery workflow). Specialist depth (preflight single-track mode, verified insertion/merge via the transaction engine, provider carrier population, Review UI) plugs into the `# HOOK(<owner>)` points left in `matching_service`, `import_service`, `ai_service` and `acquisition_service`; the spine lowers no AcoustID/confidence/identity-proof threshold. Tests: `tests/test_recovery_carrier.py`.
- **Single-track-recovery preflight keyed off the carrier (Phase-2 identity depth, ARCH-030).** When a recovery carrier is bound to a staged folder, `matching_service._folder_release_preflight` now runs a first-class single-track-recovery mode: the existing album, canonical `mb_releasegroupid` and the expected missing track(s) come from the carrier store (`recovery_carrier.load_for_folder`) — never re-derived from the folder name (ADR-0002) — so a legitimate one-track download for a known multi-track album is verified against the intended album by the deterministic per-recording proof (MusicBrainz recording/release-track evidence, AcoustID fingerprint, requested-track + duplicate checks, artist/track-number consistency) instead of being rejected for lacking all album tracks or accepted by the weaker tracklist-ratio relaxation. A supplemental duration-tolerance check (the carrier's expected duration is milliseconds, the measured audio length seconds — converted, never compared raw; canonical tolerance) sends an AcoustID-proven but wrong-length copy to review. No AcoustID/confidence/identity-proof threshold is lowered (ADR-0003); an uncertain file stays staged for review, and the preflight records what it proved into the carrier via `advance_state(..., evidence=…)`. The AI-batch scan (`ai_service._ai_batch_find_audio_dirs`) now consumes the carrier for an app-managed staging folder and leaves a carrier-bound recovery out of the generic new-album batch (its own guarded recovery workflow owns it) rather than re-deriving identity from the folder name and importing it as a new album. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_carrier_preflight.py`.
- **Missing-track recovery: verified single-track insertion into the existing album (Phase-2, ARCH-030, HOOK library-transaction).** `backend/import_service._merge_imported_album_into_existing` now recognises a recovery carrier bound to the staged folder (looked up by path through `recovery_carrier.load_for_folder`, never parsed from the folder name) that names the album being merged into, and drives it through the carrier's recovery state instead of treating the download as an ordinary merge. The insertion reuses the existing, already transaction-backed reconcile (`composite_workflows.{plan,apply}_existing_album_reconcile` → the transaction engine's preview/apply/audit/recovery and the durable rollback in `transaction_recovery`/`rollback_guard`) — no new mutation path. Before any library change it claims the carrier into `importing` with a compare-and-swap (a carrier already `importing`/`imported`, or a lost CAS, means a second recovery job — or a crash that left it mid-import — and is refused so the same track is never imported twice; album resource locks the reconcile apply already takes serialise same-album concurrency) and runs fail-closed preconditions: the target album must still exist and every expected (missing) track must still be absent, else the folder is routed to Review with its structured identity and nothing is imported or deleted. On success the carrier advances to `imported` + checkpoint `IMPORTED`; a merge that moved nothing (different Release Group, or slots held for review) goes to `review_required`; a merge that raised leaves a recoverable `failed` state with the imported album row untouched. The merge never lowers an identity/AcoustID threshold and never deletes the download, existing music or a conflicting file. A folder with no carrier is a strict no-op — the ordinary merge is unchanged. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_recovery_merge.py`.
- **Missing-track recovery carrier enrichment + single guarded workflow (Phase-2, ARCH-030).** `acquisition_service.start_album_download` now populates the carrier with the recording-level `expected_tracks` (`recording_id`/`track`/`disc`/`duration`/`title`, taken from the missing-track queue already reconciled against MusicBrainz completeness) and the `source` {method, provenance}, keeping the canonical `mb_releasegroupid` distinct from the supporting `release_id` (never substituted). The auto-import path drives the carrier's durable checkpoints as the download progresses (`CREATED` → `DOWNLOADED` when the staged folder is bound → `STAGED` when verified audio is ready for Beets; `VERIFIED`/`IMPORTED`/`PLEX_SYNCED` remain the downstream owners'). A missing-track recovery now runs as **one** guarded, resumable job whose `job_contract.guarded(workflow=…)` slug is `recovery_carrier.recovery_workflow_id(carrier_id)`, so a restart resumes the same recovery instead of splitting into decoupled jobs (a plain full-album download keeps the per-album `download-import-…` workflow). Carrier population and checkpointing are best-effort: a carrier-store failure never breaks the download/import. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_acquisition_carrier.py`.
- **Missing-track recovery is now one continuous download → verify → verified-insert → Plex-sync workflow (Phase-2 backend tail, ARCH-030).** The recovery pipeline's remaining robustness gaps are closed and the workflow runs end to end:
  - **Plex-sync leg (F9).** Once the verified track reaches the existing album (carrier `imported` + checkpoint `IMPORTED`), `import_service._recovery_plex_sync_leg` triggers one best-effort Plex library refresh (`plex_service._trigger_plex_refresh`, new allow-listed `recovery` workflow) and advances the carrier to `PLEX_SYNCED`. It is best-effort and auditable: a Plex failure never fails or rolls back the completed import — it is recorded on the carrier (`plex_sync.status`) and the import stays intact at `IMPORTED` (retriable). The operator's opt-in `PLEX_SYNC_AUTO_REFRESH` is respected — when it is off (or Plex is not configured) no refresh is forced and the carrier records a skip and stays terminal `imported`. The leg is idempotent (a carrier already `PLEX_SYNCED` is a no-op).
  - **Checkpoint resume (F7).** A recovery re-triggered with its existing `recovery_carrier_id` reuses the one guarded workflow (`recovery_workflow_id`) and resumes from the last durable checkpoint instead of restarting from scratch or splitting into decoupled jobs. A carrier already at `IMPORTED`/`PLEX_SYNCED` short-circuits the job: no second download and no second import (and the carrier CAS claim + merge precheck still refuse a double import on any path). `recovery_carrier.load_for_job` is now wired into the resume lookup. Durability does not depend on the UI.
  - **Replacement is not a recovery (F8).** `acquisition_service` no longer classifies a format replacement (`replace_existing`) carrying `wanted_tracks` for a PRESENT track as a recovery, so it is not minted a carrier and mis-routed to Review by the fail-closed precheck; the Preview-until-approved replacement path owns it.
  - **Review re-entry rehydration + restage rebind (F5/F6).** When an import re-enters from the Review queue (not the auto path), the carrier's structured identity (existing album + still-missing tracks) is rehydrated from the store and the single verified track is routed through the existing-album missing-track merge instead of importing a second album row. When the still-missing subset is restaged to a new path before the merge's `load_for_folder` lookup, `import_service._recovery_rebind_restaged` rebinds the carrier to the staged path so the merge still runs under carrier ownership/audit (reaching `IMPORTED`/`PLEX_SYNCED`).
  - **Non-auto hand-off (F3).** A carrier-bound folder downloaded with `auto_import=false` — deliberately excluded from the generic new-album AI batch — is handed to Review with its structured carrier identity (and the carrier marked `review_required`) instead of being stranded in `/downloads`.
  - **User-facing workflow status contract.** `recovery_carrier.workflow_stage(carrier)` is a pure, secret-free mapping from the carrier's durable state + checkpoint onto the seven Download-Missing UI stages (Missing → Searching → Downloading → Verifying → Importing → Plex Sync → Complete, plus `needs_review`/`failed` terminals): `{stage, stage_index, label, terminal, note}`. It is surfaced on the Review item via `review_identity(...)['recovery_stage']` for the Phase-6 status UI. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_backend_complete.py`, `tests/test_mtr_recovery_merge.py`.
- **Plex library-sync audit and opt-in auto-heal (`POST /api/plex/sync/audit`, `POST /api/plex/sync/heal`).** New `backend/plex_sync_service.py` (bound by `routes_plex_sync.py`) answers why a reachable Plex library still misses music the Web Manager can see. It reconciles three views of the same library: **filesystem** — every artist/album folder under `MUSIC_ROOT` — folder naming (literal `{Album MbId}`-style residue, unresolved template tokens, malformed release-group stamps, plus the real `{rgid}` suffix style canonical cleanup emits) and per-file readability judged exactly as the kernel would for the group the Plex server runs as (`PLEX_FS_GID`, numeric, default `568` — the linuxserver.io PUID/PGID convention), POSIX ACL masks decoded from `system.posix_acl_access`; **beets** — items joined by album directory through `beets_adapter` (canonical identity: `mb_releasegroupid`); **Plex** — the music section's track index joined by file path only, never by text identity (a failure or absence of Plex leaves the filesystem findings usable). The audit is read-only: per-album verdicts separate `in_plex` / `missing_from_plex` / `not_in_beets` / `plex_unavailable`, `beets_only_artists` names artists whose folders Plex has never indexed, naming/readability totals and per-artist examples are capped (`50`) so the payload stays bounded. The heal runs three **individually opt-in** repairs (off by default; the request body's `allow_chmod` / `allow_touch` / `allow_refresh` / `max_paths` override the environment for that single run; a heal with every action disabled is refused): **chmod** adds group read (and directory traverse) bits — and, only where no ACL entry covers Plex's group and a plain-mode fix suffices, chgrps the file (bits are only ever added, never removed — the slskd-shape file, an ACL named entry with the bits under a zero mask, is repaired by chmod raising the mask, while an ACL entry or maskless group-owner shape `chmod` cannot fix is counted as blocked with the exact `setfacl` command in the error text) — and repairs unreadable *directories* independently of file states, since a folder Plex cannot traverse hides every file inside it; **touch** bumps repaired album directories and their parents, because Plex's incremental scanner decides what to reopen from mtimes — a permission fix alone stays invisible to it (seen live 2026-10-23); **refresh** triggers one Plex library scan through `_trigger_plex_refresh`, which may re-send the idempotent refresh POST once on a transient failure (BA-5 protects only non-idempotent resends). `PLEX_SYNC_MAX_REPAIR_PATHS` (default `20000`, minimum `100`) caps permission edits per run and reports the remainder as `paths_remaining` rather than silently skipping. Nothing here deletes, moves or rewrites content; duplicate removal stays behind the unattended-deletion authorization (`backend/dedup_authorization`), which this feature does not touch. New environment variables `PLEX_FS_GID`, `PLEX_SYNC_AUTO_CHMOD`, `PLEX_SYNC_AUTO_TOUCH`, `PLEX_SYNC_AUTO_REFRESH`, `PLEX_SYNC_MAX_REPAIR_PATHS` (documented in `docs/CONFIGURATION.md` and `.env.example`). Tests: `tests/test_plex_sync_service.py` (POSIX ACL decode, readability/repair classification, naming verdicts, walk aggregation, beets join, Plex path presence, opt-in heal incl. the disabled/blocked/capped paths and the gated refresh) and `tests/test_plex_sync_routes.py` (both endpoints start the right job, reuse a running one, refuse an all-disabled heal, clamp `max_paths`, forward body flags to the service).
- **Exact-path item restore for trusted rollbacks.** New plugin operation `POST /webmanager/item-path/restore` (and `BeetsAdapter.restore_item_paths`) moves explicitly named tracks back to their recorded paths inside the allowed roots. It is hash-resumable, never overwrites an existing target, refuses allowed-root escapes and updates the Beets row only after the file is in place. `undo_import_rollback` remains the operator/recovery primitive for reversing a completed import rollback; it is not a UI transaction-state transition.
- **Real-library rollback regression coverage (#486 a–j).** `tests/test_arch004_023_real_restore.py` drives disposable Beets libraries and real files through album-row/import rollback and undo (bytes, rows, membership, quarantine), artwork fetch/rollback (cover and embedded bytes, changed-art refusal), item/album/genre restore truth, generic-route reachability, crash/restart recovery and durable manifests, concurrent rollback/apply, idempotent replay, exact-path restore (including containment and resume), artist-folder rollback and legacy `rollback_unavailable`. The frontend contract tests cover multipart key scoping, 5xx key retention and 4xx settlement.

### Fixed

- **Rolling back a Missing IDs Release Group fill no longer blanks an item whose file tag already carried that Release Group (ARCH-032, QA #401 N-d).** The Missing IDs batch fills `mb_releasegroupid` on an album that has a Release ID but no Release Group through a Metadata Update with tag writes (`routes_library._batch_write_album` → `composite_workflows.{plan,apply}_album_metadata`). The plan's before-state captured only the album-level value (empty) and, for items, only those named in an explicit per-item update — so an album-level tag write captured no per-item values. A Beets album write with tags rewrites every item's tags, so the rollback (`rollback_album_metadata` → `_restore_rows`) stamped the album's captured empty value onto every item, blanking the embedded Release Group of an item whose own file tag already carried it. `plan_album_metadata` now captures each album item's **own** value of every album-written field in the before-state (explicit per-item updates still add their own fields on top), so the rollback restores the album's value to the album and each item's own value to that item — the item keeps the Release Group it had. The apply/audit/recovery transaction contract is unchanged (only the before-state capture is widened; apply still writes only the album and any explicit per-item updates), and no threshold is lowered. This is the root-cause fix for the whole class: any album-level metadata write (name, genre, albumartist) now rolls back to each item's own prior value rather than the album's. Tests: `tests/test_arch032_release_group_fill_rollback.py` (fails before the fix: the item is blanked; passes after).
- **Review Queue follow-ups: lock-free prune, idempotent batch hand-off, preserved evidence (ARCH-033, QA #410 N1-N3).** Three Review Queue edges are closed. **N3** — a list-only Review Queue load pruned stale rows while holding the global queue lock, and the prune made Beets calls, so a slow Beets serialized every queue reader and writer. `_load_pending_reviews` now computes the Beets-backed resolution checks before the lock (`_compute_prune_resolutions(_read_pending_snapshot())`) and the locked pass only re-reads and applies them, dropping a row only when its resolution-relevant fields still match the snapshot; no Beets HTTP call happens while the queue lock is held. `_add_to_pending` loads with `prune_resolved=False` (it already makes its own library check outside the lock), keeping its locked section Beets-free. **N1** — a partial-import batch folder whose Review enqueue failed was marked `import_failed`, and a retry re-ran its Stage 1 import against a folder Beets had already partly imported. The inline enqueue failure now records the imported album id(s) and stashes the Review payload; on retry `_ai_batch_process_folder` re-creates the Review row from the stash and never re-runs Stage 1 for an already-imported folder. **N2** — the final-pass review for such a folder built a minimal row, dropping the original `conflict` / `skipped_paths` / `kept_album_ids` / `deterministic_decision`. The stash now feeds both the final pass and the N1 retry, so that evidence survives on the Review row. No queue/lock safety invariant is weakened. Tests: `tests/test_arch033_review_followups.py`.
- **Missing-track recovery is marked IMPORTED only when every expected/wanted track was actually delivered, so a partially-delivered recovery is never falsely reported imported or stranded (ARCH-030, QA round-2 Finding F-2 / QA-2-r).** `import_service._merge_imported_album_into_existing` advanced the carrier to `imported` + checkpoint `IMPORTED` whenever `attempted = bool(plan.move_ids or plan.duplicate_rows)` — i.e. *any* move/dup marked the whole recovery imported, even when the merge delivered only a strict subset of the recovery's expected/wanted tracks. Downstream, the acquisition resume short-circuit judges "already-done" by coverage of the carrier's intended tracks (`expected_tracks ∪ wanted_tracks`) against a carrier that reached `IMPORTED`, so a resubmission of an *intended-but-undelivered* track returned a false `{"imported": True}` and was stranded — the exact "don't lose the track / don't falsely report success" failure this recovery path exists to prevent. The outcome is now proven from the reconcile plan: IMPORTED is reached only when the merge landed on the existing album **and** every intended track (`expected_tracks ∪ wanted_tracks`, the same set the resume short-circuit treats as covered) is present in the album as it stands after the merge — the existing rows plus the imported rows this merge moves onto them — checked by reusing the existing `_recovery_slot_present` by recording id or (disc, track). A strict-subset (partial) delivery leaves the carrier in the existing recoverable `review_required` state (no fabricated state value, no IMPORTED checkpoint), so a resubmission of an undelivered track is not short-circuited as done. This makes IMPORTED mean "fully delivered", which makes `acquisition_service._requested_tracks_covered` correct without changing it. No identity/AcoustID/threshold is weakened and no mutation path is added (the delivery check is read-only classification; the CAS claim and fail-closed precheck are unchanged and reused). Tests: `tests/test_mtr_recovery_merge.py` (`test_partial_delivery_is_not_marked_imported`, `test_full_multi_track_delivery_reaches_imported`).
- **Import Review apply-confirm dialog links an album id to the correct MusicBrainz entity, not a dead `/release/{release-group-id}` (ARCH-030, QA round-3 Finding 6).** The dialog hardcoded the `MbidLink` entity from `item.target_kind` (`item` → `recording`, otherwise `release`), but the id it shows (`mbids[item.id]`) is populated from the **release-GROUP** id (`release_group_id`) on every album apply path. So the common album confirmation rendered a release-group UUID as entity `release`, linking to `https://musicbrainz.org/release/{release-group-id}` (wrong entity / dead link) and mislabelling it "Release ID". The apply intent now carries the id's actual MB entity, derived (`applyMbidEntity`) from the known source fields rather than guessed: a per-item target is a `recording`; an album target is the canonical `release-group` (ADR-0002 — a release-group id is never relabelled a release) unless the id specifically matches the known release (edition) id and differs from the release-group id (the `mb_albumid` fallback), in which case it is a `release`. The dialog links/labels from that entity (`/release-group/…` "Release Group ID", `/release/…` "Release ID", `/recording/…` "Recording ID"); the invalid-not-linked behaviour is unchanged. Tests: `frontend/tests/ImportReviewMbidMigration.test.tsx` (the release-group apply case now asserts `/release-group/`; added a genuine-release `/release/` case and a per-item `/recording/` case, plus `applyMbidEntity` unit cases).
- **Missing-track recovery no longer strands the carrier on the automatic import path when the staged subset is narrowed (ARCH-030, QA round-2 Finding 1).** `import_service.start_reimport_disk`'s auto path restages the still-missing subset to a new folder and reassigns `aldir`, after which the verified-insert merge and the Plex-sync leg look the carrier up by that reassigned path. The restage rebind (`_recovery_rebind_restaged`) was wired only into the Review re-entry path, so when the second source scan narrowed the subset (a new staged folder is created, not the #427 in-place branch), the carrier — still bound to the pre-restage folder — was orphaned: the merge ran carrier-less, the Plex-sync leg no-oped, and the Download-Missing status stalled at "Verifying". The auto path now rebinds the carrier to the restaged path (same album-equality gate; never parsed from the folder name; a genuinely absent carrier stays a strict no-op leaving the non-recovery merge unchanged) so the verified single-track insertion runs under carrier ownership/audit and the carrier advances normally to `IMPORTED` → `PLEX_SYNCED`. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_autopath_rebind.py` (end-to-end auto progression over the real `start_reimport_disk`, failing before this fix).
- **Import Review shows the missing-track recovery stage live, not a stale snapshot (ARCH-030, QA Finding 7).** The Download-Missing status indicator read `recovery_stage` from the Review item's stored evidence snapshot, which the non-auto hand-off captures *before* the carrier advances to `review_required` and which the read path never refreshed — so the queue could show e.g. "Verifying" while the carrier was actually `review_required`/`failed`. The Review-queue serializer (`GET /api/import/review-queue`) now recomputes `recovery_stage` fresh from the live carrier at read time (`recovery_carrier.get_carrier` → `workflow_stage`) via `pending_review_store._refresh_recovery_stage`, so the UI always reflects the carrier's current state + checkpoint. The stage object keeps its exact `{stage, stage_index, label, terminal, note}` shape and carries no secrets/paths; a missing or unreadable carrier degrades to the stored snapshot rather than crashing or fabricating a stage. This also makes the "snapshot taken before the advance" timing moot without changing the acquisition hand-off. (A still-in-flight download that is not yet a Review item has no queue row and is out of scope.) Tests: `tests/test_mtr_backend_complete.py::RecoveryStageFreshnessTests`.
- **Import Review never labels a MusicBrainz ID "Verified" from UUID syntax alone, and every MBID shown routes through the validated `MbidLink` (ARCH-030, frontend).** The item-level canonical Release-Group chip derived its status from `item.mb_valid`, which the backend sets by UUID *syntax* only (`re.match` on the UUID shape) — so a syntactically-valid-but-unverified id (e.g. one lifted from `evidence.preflight.release_group`) rendered a green **Verified** chip, contradicting the addendum rule that an unverified id is never shown as a confirmed match. The chip now defaults to the neutral **Suggested** (no genuine verified signal exists on the item today; a valid UUID still links and is copyable — validity governs linking, not verification). Follow-up for music-identity: if `mb_valid` should ever carry a real verified signal, surface it as a distinct field. The apply-confirm dialog, which showed a bare truncated `{intent.mbid}` with no validation/Copy/type/status (the `attach_recording_confirm` kind had been migrated but `apply` had not), now renders its id through `MbidLink` with the correct entity (`recording` for a per-item attach, otherwise `release`) and the invalid-not-linked behavior. The frontend-built `musicbrainz.org` navigation-button URLs (`musicBrainzUrl`/`musicBrainzReleaseGroupUrl`/`musicBrainzRecordingUrl` and the inline release-group href) now validate the UUID with `MbidLink`'s `isValidMbid` before building an href, so a malformed id can no longer produce a dead outbound Button link. Tests: `frontend/tests/ImportReviewMbidMigration.test.tsx`.
- **Import Review surfaces a live recovery-stage change in the queue, migrates the last raw MBID renderer, and corrects the apply-entity derivation under a contradictory selected match (ARCH-030 Phase-6 frontend, NF-4).** Three frontend follow-ups on the already-shipped Download-Missing indicator: (1) the 60s Review-queue auto-refresh compared items with `reviewItemStateKey`, which omitted the recovery stage, so the live-recomputed `recovery_stage` (backend `_refresh_recovery_stage`) never changed the snapshot and the "updates available" banner never fired for a recovery in progress — the state key now includes the stage, so a Download-Missing transition (Searching → Downloading → …) is surfaced for refresh (no new backend endpoint; the indicator is wired where the contract is served — the Review item). (2) The one remaining raw non-recovery MBID renderer — the "Representative Release ID" panel, previously bare truncated mono text — now routes through the validated `MbidLink` (full UUID, `/release/` link in a new tab with `rel="noreferrer noopener"`, Copy, typed, neutral Suggested status). (3) `applyMbidEntity` read the release (edition) id as `sm.representative_release_id || item.mb_albumid`, so with a selected match present `item.mb_albumid` was never consulted and an applied id equal to it (but differing from the match's representative release) was mislabelled/linked as a release-group; it now checks the applied id against **both** known release ids, keeping the ADR-0002-safe release-group default. Tests: `frontend/tests/ImportReviewMbidMigration.test.tsx` (NF-4 contradictory-match cases, `reviewItemStateKey` stage-transition cases).
- **Missing-track recovery carrier CAS no longer reports a false success to the loser of a concurrent claim (ARCH-030).** `recovery_carrier.RecoveryCarrierStore.cas_update` did a `SELECT`, a Python-level revision pre-check, then an `UPDATE ... WHERE carrier_id=? AND revision=?`, but never inspected the UPDATE's affected-row count and unconditionally returned `get(carrier_id)`. Under two WAL connections that both snapshot the same revision before either commits, both pass the pre-check, one UPDATE matches 1 row (the winner) and the loser's matches 0 rows without raising — so `cas_update` handed the loser the winner's record as a false success. That defeated `import_service._recovery_claim_importing`'s compare-and-swap claim into `importing` — the only serializer on the manual Review `apply_confirmed_import` path — letting two confirmers import the same track into the existing album (a duplicate audio merge). `cas_update` now raises `RecoveryCarrierConflictError` whenever a genuine 0-row UPDATE of an existing row occurs (mirroring `ai_batch_state_store`), including on an `expected_revision=None` blind write that loses a race; an uncontended blind write on a real row still succeeds. Relatedly, a delayed/concurrent folder preflight (`matching_service._record_carrier_preflight_evidence`) can no longer downgrade a carrier already in `importing`/`imported` or a terminal state back to `verifying`/`review_required` (which would have reopened the claim gate for a second claimer): it re-reads state, skips a claimed/terminal carrier, and otherwise advances with a compare-and-swap on the revision it just read. Tests: `tests/test_mtr_carrier_cas_race.py`.
- **Import Review "Find Match" no longer shows a green success banner when nothing matched.** A 200 response from the album/folder or recording suggest endpoints that selected no candidate (no release-group match, no valid MusicBrainz id, no recording id) now renders a neutral "no candidate" warning with the real outcome text, instead of a green success alert reading "Find Match completed" / "No confident recording match found". The success state is reserved for a response that actually selected a match.
- **ARCH-004 / ARCH-023 transaction and rollback reliability (#486).** Composite rollback is now real, reachable and truthful for the covered families. `item_metadata_repair_v1` and `genre_repair_v1` are registered with the generic rollback route, and rollback eligibility mirrors the executors. The rollback claim and recovery marker are one durable write. An interrupted rollback becomes `Recovery Required` before the restart sweep can mistake the earlier apply's success for the rollback's outcome; resumable folder-cleanup rollbacks instead remain `Running` for an explicit retry from their durable steps. The redundant synchronous startup sweep is gone. Album-row removal/rollback and confirmed-import rollback/undo write a durable `applying` manifest before mutation, restore rows in the right order, prove live album membership under `mutation_lock`, preflight every quarantine source, refuse missing sources rather than re-add rows pointing at absent media, and replay completed retries from stored idempotency results. Quarantine/undo moves verify bytes and never overwrite an occupied destination. Failed album-row removal compensates a completed removal before reporting it as restored.
