- **Artwork rollback verifies bytes, not pathnames (#486 H7/H8).** Artwork set and fetch record the cover's sha256 and each item's embedded-image hashes after the apply, and rollback refuses when either changed under the same name. Fetch-and-embed now requires `embedart` up front, snapshots every cover filename/extension fetchart may overwrite (not just `cover.jpg`), compensates any post-mutation failure, and restores the previous cover and embedded images byte-for-byte. The operator-requested fetch is forced so an existing cover is actually replaced instead of the request reporting success while doing nothing.
- **Metadata, genre and artist-folder rollback restore the library and the files (#486 C3/H10–H12).** The covered families now take the same album/item resource locks across snapshot, apply and verification, and validate the engine's result and counts instead of assuming success. An empty DB diff no longer hides changed file tags: DB values, file tags and paths are verified independently, and only a genuine no-op is reported as `Rolled Back`. Artist-folder rollback restores each track to its exact original path (through the new plugin operation), its original audio payload and its tags; Beets' path-format move alone is not accepted as a restore. Legacy or incomplete records report `rollback_unavailable`. A confirmed import that wrote tags in place reports `Partially Rolled Back` with `file_tags_restored: false` rather than claiming a full restore, and never removes the operator's files.
- **Multipart job starts no longer collide, and a 5xx is not treated as a definitive answer (#486 H9).** The frontend scopes a multipart upload's random idempotency key to the form fields and each file's name, size, modification time and type, so a different artwork upload to the same endpoint starts its own job. A 5xx or lost response keeps the key so a retry follows the job the server may already have started; a 2xx/4xx settles it so the next deliberate action is new. The two fetch helpers now share that behaviour.
- **A concurrent artist-discography request follows the running job instead of returning 409.** The route already promises a `job_id` while the lookup runs; a duplicate start now returns the existing job, matching the UI's polling contract.
- **AI match history records only a suggestion that was imported.** After every Review import, history recorded the submitted suggestion, including imports of a different release the operator chose and manual imports with no suggestion at all. It now records an entry only when the suggestion's Release Group (or, without one, its Release) is what Beets imported, stores the server-verified IDs, and writes the file atomically.
- **The unattended Review import no longer acts on a suggestion computed for different files.** `auto-enqueue-ready` imports from the stored track mapping and evidence, and the folder signature meant to detect a folder that changed after Find Match was never checked. It is now: a changed folder is skipped with "The folder changed after its match was computed" in the job log until it is revalidated. Find Match and revalidation take the signature before they gather evidence, so files arriving during a check also make the stored result stale. Suggestions stored before signatures existed are not treated as stale.
- **Find Match can no longer occupy every server worker.** Each Find Match runs for seconds (fingerprints, rate-limited MusicBrainz, the AI call) inside a web request, and the Review page started one for every item the cursor landed on, so paging through the queue could stall the whole UI. The folder, album and item Find Match routes now run at most two at once (one when `WEBCONTROL_THREADS` is below 8) and answer `503 suggest_busy` straight away beyond that; the page runs one automatic Find Match at a time, gives the item under the cursor its turn when it finishes, and shows a refusal as a warning to retry.
- **Find Match and import-with-id answer a malformed request body with an error instead of a 500.** A non-object JSON body or a non-text `path` no longer raises inside the route.

## v0.13.5 - 2026-10-22

### Performance

- **The folder-cleanup DB-reference scan no longer resolves `realpath(MUSIC_ROOT)` once per Beets item.** `composite_workflows.norm_library_path` runs once per item during a full-library scan that repeats on every rename; on a ~5,000-folder live run that was a `realpath` syscall storm that could make the run crawl or look hung. The root's `realpath` is now resolved once per distinct MUSIC_ROOT string (`functools.lru_cache`) and reused. The normalization result is byte-for-byte identical; only the trusted root is `realpath`-resolved, still never a caller path.

### Fixed

- **A tracked album folder whose Beets DB path is a DANGLING symlink is now HELD (`needs_db_resync`), not relocated into a loud partial move.** `_album_db_paths_stale` used `os.path.lexists`, which reports a dangling symlink (link present, target gone) as present, so the relocation ran and Beets skipped every track (a rollback-able `partial_move`). It now uses `os.path.exists`, so a DB path with no real file behind it is treated as stale and held. Regression test: `tests/test_folder_mbid_names_apply.py::TrackedRelocationTests::test_dangling_symlink_db_path_held_needs_db_resync`.

- **Renaming a Beets-TRACKED album folder to `Album (Year) {release-group-mbid}` no longer fails with `PATH_IS_TRACKED` when the DB and disk paths are desynced.** The folder-MBID apply (`_apply_folder_mbid_names`) decided tracked-vs-disk-only from its own item-count map (`_placeholder_folder_db`), which keyed item paths with a raw `os.path.dirname` — no realpath rewrite and no relative-path resolution. On a symlinked/realpath-diverging `MUSIC_ROOT` (and after an earlier placeholder-strip left the DB naming the pre-rename path) that map reported `db_item_count=0` for a folder the engine still considered tracked, so the apply picked the disk-only `folder_rename` and the engine's descendant-aware, realpath-aware DB-reference refusal (`_library_refs_under`) rejected it (`folder_cleanup_rename_failed` / `PATH_IS_TRACKED`). Both detectors now normalize through one shared `composite_workflows.norm_library_path`, so a folder the engine treats as tracked is classified tracked by the apply and routed to the DB-aware relocation (`relocate_album` / Beets `Album.move`) — folder and DB item/album paths move together. When Beets names paths that are no longer on disk (the strip-desync), the album is HELD (`needs_db_resync`) rather than relocated off stale paths, so no file is lost and DB and disk never diverge. The existing holds (`tracked_rgid_mismatch`, `tracked_multiple_albums`, `target_exists`), disk-only renames, idempotent `already_correct`, dry-run, the confirmed gate, `batch_limit`, resume and rollback-by-job are unchanged. Regression tests: `tests/test_folder_mbid_names_apply.py::TrackedRelocationTests` (realpath-desynced tracked relocation; stale-DB `needs_db_resync` hold).

## v0.13.4 - 2026-10-22

### Changed

- **A refused cross-origin redirect from a configured service URL now names the redacted redirect target.** `SafeRedirectHandler` already refuses a redirect that leaves a configured service's own origin (the implicit trust pins scheme+host+port); its `OutboundPolicyError` now includes the redacted target origin (`scheme://host:port`, path hashed — never a secret), so an operator diagnostic can say exactly where the service tried to redirect. Behaviour is otherwise unchanged.

- **Temporary Lidarr boot-trust diagnostic (v0.13.3) extended to log the RAW outbound-policy error.** The live v0.13.3 diagnostic confirmed the folder-MBID resolver's Lidarr call is rejected with a VALID configured-URL trust rule (`configured_url` correct, `would_trust_rule` valid) — i.e. not the empty-resolver case and, per direct instrumentation, not a thread/contextvar-inheritance case (the trusted rule IS visible to `secure_urlopen` on the worker thread). The one path that rejects a correctly-configured call is a cross-origin redirect from the service endpoint. The diagnostic now also logs `type(exc)` and the raw policy message (fixed sentences + redacted origins, no secret), so the next deploy names the exact redirect target (e.g. `http -> https` on the same host:port, or a different port). Still fires only on `ProviderOutcome.REJECTED`, once per process, and remains temporary pending removal once the live target is confirmed and the redirect-trust policy decision is made. New regression tests: `tests/test_provider_trust_redirect_diagnostic.py`.

## v0.13.3 - 2026-10-22

### Fixed

- **Starting a fresh AI-batch source scan (e.g. of `/downloads`) no longer fails immediately with "ERROR: expected_revision is required to update existing batch …".** `_start_ai_batch_job` creates — and bumps the revision of — the batch's durable store row before it unblocks the worker thread, but on a fresh (non-recover) run the worker's `_run_ai_batch_import` built a brand-new `_ai_batch_initial_state`, which carries no `revision`. Its first `_ai_batch_commit` therefore attempted an *un-revisioned* update of the already-existing row, which the ARCH-010 optimistic-concurrency (CAS) contract correctly rejects. The worker now loads the persisted row first (for the non-recover path too) and threads its current revision through every commit, so the scan proceeds and the stale-writer guard stays fully intact. The CAS contract itself is unchanged — an un-revisioned update of an existing batch is still refused; the fix is in the caller, which now passes the revision the state was last read at. Regression test: `tests/test_ai_batch_retry_race.py::FreshBatchSourceScanRevisionTests`.
### Added

- **TEMPORARY boot-trust diagnostic (to be removed once the live cause is confirmed).** When the folder-MBID resolver's Lidarr call is REJECTED by the outbound URL policy (`ProviderOutcome.REJECTED`), `backend.lidarr_rgid_resolver` now emits a single WARNING (once per process) capturing, from the live serving process's own job thread, why the configured-URL trust was not active there: `configured_url("lidarr")`, whether a resolver is registered and its value, `backend.app_runtime.LIDARR_URL`, the recomputed `implicit_rule_for_url(configured_url)` (`would_trust_rule`), the live `_TRUSTED_RULE`, and the `id()`s of the `backend.app_runtime` / `backend.security` / `backend.provider_boundary` module objects. It logs only non-secret base URLs (userinfo stripped) and rule/module ids — never the Lidarr API key — fires only on the rejection, and changes no behavior otherwise. Diagnoses whether the live cause is the empty-resolver shadow (fixed below) or a duplicate module instance; remove after the v0.13.3 deploy confirms it.

### Fixed

- **A provider's configured-URL trust can no longer be lost in the long-lived serving process when its registered resolver comes back empty.** The outbound boundary grants each operator-hosted provider implicit trust of its own configured URL (`provider_boundary.configured_url`), resolved either from a registered resolver (Plex, Lidarr) or, as a robust fallback, from the `backend.app_runtime` constant the service actually calls (`_RUNTIME_URL_CONSTANTS`: Lidarr, slskd, qBittorrent, yt-dlp PO). A registered resolver that returned `""` — e.g. a module-level closure in a route module that loaded before the setting was present, which is exactly how the running server could differ from a fresh `import app` — previously **shadowed** that fallback, so the configured URL earned no trust and the provider's own call to a private-LAN address (the Lidarr folder-MBID resolver's `192.168.0.250:8686`, `ProviderOutcome.REJECTED`, "blocked by outbound URL policy") was rejected. `configured_url` now falls through to the `_RUNTIME_URL_CONSTANTS` / `sys.modules["backend.app_runtime"]` value whenever the resolver yields nothing, so registering a resolver can only ADD a trust source, never remove the one the provider already had. Trust stays scoped to exactly the one configured scheme/host/port; the SSRF guard is unchanged for every other address and provider. New real-app-context regression tests (`tests/test_lidarr_trust_boot_activation.py`) boot the app the way the server does and assert the Lidarr configured URL is admitted through `provider_boundary.opened` end to end, including on a worker thread (the job engine runs jobs on threads), plus the resolver-fallthrough guard.

## v0.13.2 - 2026-10-22

### Security

- **The lazy `JobStore.get()` id lookup now validates the job id with a positive, anchored allowlist before building a filesystem path, clearing two HIGH CodeQL `py/path-injection` alerts.** Job ids are server-generated (`uuid.uuid4().hex`), so the lookup already rejected path-separator/dot/`..` input, but CodeQL's taint tracker does not recognise a negative traversal check as sanitization. Both path-construction sites now route through a single `JobStore._record_path()` barrier that admits an id only via `_SAFE_JOB_ID = re.compile(r"\A[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,128}\Z").fullmatch(...)` — a strict superset of every real id, with no separator/dot/traversal possible — before the id is joined onto the jobs directory; the `path.parent == root` check stays as defense in depth. Behaviour is unchanged: every valid id (server uuids, resumed/rollback ids, on-disk record stems) still resolves and only malformed/traversal input is refused.

### Fixed

- **Lidarr now registers its operator-configured URL with the provider boundary, so a private LAN `LIDARR_URL` is trusted for Lidarr's own outbound calls (like Plex already is).** The folder MBID-name resolver's Lidarr call was rejected by the outbound URL policy (`ProviderOutcome.REJECTED`, "blocked by outbound URL policy") whenever `LIDARR_URL` resolved to a private/RFC-1918 address (e.g. `192.168.0.250:8686`) and the configured URL was not otherwise resolvable at call time: the boundary grants a provider implicit trust of its own configured URL, but Lidarr (unlike Plex) never registered an explicit resolver, so trust relied on a fragile fallback. `routes_lidarr` (a startup route module) now calls `provider_boundary.register_configured_url("lidarr", ...)` at import time, reading the same live `backend.app_runtime.LIDARR_URL` the resolver uses (env, then the `LIDARR_URL_FILE` fallback and default). Trust stays scoped to exactly that one configured scheme/host/port for Lidarr's own calls; the SSRF guard is unchanged for every other address and provider.
- **Durable-job cold start is now fast and bounded regardless of accumulated job history.** On a long-lived instance the jobs directory (`WEB_MANAGER_DATA_DIR/jobs`) had grown to ~2,796 records (~34 MB), and `JobStore` read and parsed **every** file synchronously at startup (`_recover` globbed and `json.loads`'d all of them before the import that constructs the store returned). Over ZFS/NFS this made cold start take ~3 minutes with the process stuck in disk-sleep, so the container stayed `unhealthy` the whole time — which looked like a hang and triggered an unnecessary deploy rollback. Startup now reads only the most recent `JOB_STARTUP_LOAD_LIMIT` records (default `200`, newest by mtime) on the critical path; the long tail is handled off-path by a background thread that recovers any interrupted job and prunes terminal records older than `JOB_RETENTION_DAYS` (default `7`) from disk. `JobStore.get()` lazily loads any record by id, so the Jobs API, rollback-by-job and resume still resolve any non-pruned job even when it is outside the startup window. `recovery_required` records and running jobs are never auto-pruned, and the lazy id lookup rejects path-traversal input. New env knobs `JOB_STARTUP_LOAD_LIMIT` and `JOB_RETENTION_DAYS` (see `docs/CONFIGURATION.md` / `.env.example`); `0` or less for the load limit restores the old read-everything behaviour.
### Added

- **Regression guard: `POST /api/library/folder-mbid-names/apply` starts its heavy work in the job, not the request thread.** A new route-level test (`tests/test_folder_mbid_async_start.py`) asserts the endpoint returns `{ok, job_id}` promptly (sub-second) while the album-folder walk under `MUSIC_ROOT` and the Lidarr resolver snapshot — the ~9,022-dir-over-NFS cost that could block a request for ~173s and time out a client — run inside the job body (on the job thread), never synchronously in the request thread. The confirmed-gate (non-dry-run without `confirmed:true` → 400) is checked without touching the walk/resolver. Test-only; the handler already delegated the walk/resolver to the job via `jobs.start_python`, and this locks that structure against regression.

## v0.13.1 - 2026-10-22

### Security

- **Frontend `next` bumped `16.4.0 → 16.4.2` to clear a CRITICAL RCE and a HIGH DoS advisory** flagged by the `security` frontend-audit step. Dependency bump only; no application behaviour or configuration change.

### Fixed

- **Lidarr release-group-MBID resolver scopes its fetch to the library's artists (no longer pulls Lidarr's entire album DB).** The folder MBID-name dry-run fetched the full `GET /api/v1/album` (all albums) in one call; on a large Lidarr (37,749 albums ≈ 315 MB JSON) that times out at ~30 s and aborts the whole run, even though the library only spans ~149 distinct artists. `LidarrRgidResolver.from_lidarr` now takes the set of artist MBIDs actually present on disk (collected from the selected album folders) and fetches only those: one `GET /api/v1/artist` maps each needed `foreignArtistId` to Lidarr's internal `artist.id`, then one `GET /api/v1/album?artistId=<id>` per needed artist pulls just that artist's albums (small payloads). The in-memory artist-MBID→albums index and all matching/tie-break/strict-RGID logic are unchanged (each per-artist album still carries `artist.foreignArtistId`). Per-run Lidarr calls drop from one 315 MB request to `1 + (#distinct artists)` small requests (~150 on the reported library). Artist-MBID lookups are deduped and cached for the run; an artist MBID Lidarr does not know is skipped (no album call) and still resolves to `artist_not_in_lidarr`. Every call still goes through the provider boundary (`lidarr`) and any provider failure (timeout, auth, rate limit, unavailable, malformed) still **raises and aborts the run** — never downgraded to "unresolved".

## v0.13.0 - 2026-10-22

### Fixed

- **Lidarr release-group-MBID resolver never guesses across distinct release groups (QA finding-7).** Writing an `{release-group-mbid}` into an album folder establishes canonical identity, so it must not be a guess. The resolver's tie-breakers (exact year, albumType rank, monitored, trackFileCount) previously picked **one** candidate even when the same-title candidates belonged to **different** release groups, baking a wrong MBID into the folder. The resolver now collapses matched candidates by distinct `foreignAlbumId` (release-group MBID) **before** tie-breaking: tie-breakers only choose *which edition's album id to report* among candidates that all share **one** release-group MBID (editions/releases of a single release group — the emitted MBID is identical whichever wins). If an artist+title(+year) match spans more than one distinct release-group MBID, the folder is **held for review** (`ambiguous_multiple_albums`) rather than resolved. This applies to every tier: Tier B (title+year) and Tier C (title-only) resolve only to a single distinct RGID and otherwise hold; Tier A (path-exact) with distinct RGIDs falls through to the title tiers rather than guessing. A match whose candidates carry a mix of one valid RGID and a missing one is also held. Provider failures still abort the run, the MBID is still taken verbatim from `foreignAlbumId` and `_is_valid_mb_uuid`-validated, and the four unresolved reasons and one-snapshot-per-run behaviour are unchanged. This only moves borderline multi-release-group matches from "resolved (guessed)" to "unresolved: ambiguous_multiple_albums"; it never loosens anything.

### Added

- **Folder MBID names — name every album folder `Album (Year) {release-group-mbid}` from the Lidarr resolver, through the controlled mutation engine.** New `POST /api/library/folder-mbid-names/apply` (dry-run by default; `confirmed:true` required to mutate; optional `batch_limit`, `resume_from_job`) and `POST /api/library/folder-mbid-names/rollback` (by `job_id` or explicit `operations`). This is the correct replacement for the earlier strip-based placeholder apply, which wrongly *removed* the `{...}` token instead of *filling* the release-group MBID. One bulk Lidarr snapshot is taken per run (`LidarrRgidResolver.from_lidarr`); a provider failure **aborts the run touching nothing** and surfaces the provider error (never downgraded to "unresolved"). Per album folder: an already-correctly-named folder is an idempotent **skip** (`already_correct`); a disk-only folder is renamed through `folder_cleanup_v1`; a DB-tracked album is **relocated** through Beets (so its DB item paths follow) **only when the DB `mb_releasegroupid` already equals the resolver's**, so a non-resolver MBID is never written; an unresolved folder, a target-path collision, a DB-rgid mismatch, or a multi-album folder is **held for review** with its reason. The MBID written to disk or to the DB template always comes verbatim from the resolver. Reuses the hardened placeholder-apply machinery (write-ahead checkpoint, `batch_limit` counts only mutations, resume dedup, descendant-aware tracked detection, `ok:false` on any error, running-job 409 guard, single-call rollback-by-job from the transaction audit records). The `review` list (unresolved folders, collisions, mismatches) is produced for a later manual-resolution UI; choosing/writing `LIDARR_URL` config is a deploy-time step.

## v0.12.0 - 2026-10-21

### Fixed

- **Recover My Uploads — rollback provenance now survives the plugin field allowlist (independent QA regression).** The recovery import stamps a unique `webmanager_recover_token` (and `source_platform`) into `set_fields`, but the `webmanager` Beets plugin's `validate_fields` allowlist (`beetsplug/webmanager/schemas.py`, `ALLOWED_ITEM_FIELDS`) dropped both fields, so the token never persisted: the post-import token read-back returned nothing and a recovery item that was *actually imported* falsely reported `import_failed` and recorded **no** rollback. Both provenance flexattrs are now in the allowlist, so they round-trip through the real Beets boundary. Plugin `PLUGIN_VERSION` is bumped `1.18.4 → 1.18.5` (`PROTOCOL_VERSION` unchanged at `1.0`) — **the embedded Beets must be restarted/recreated for the new allowlist to take effect** (the rollout script restarts Beets when the plugin version changes). The post-import token lookup (`_find_recovered_item_by_token`) is also made **fail-closed**: a single returned row carrying a *different* non-empty token is rejected (returns `None`) rather than used as a fuzzy single-row fallback, so rollback can never bind to the wrong item's file. Signed-URL scrubbing of the authorization note/evidence (`_scrub_authz_text`) now strips signed/secret `name=value` query params (`X-Amz-Signature`, `sig`, `token`, …) regardless of URL form, including parenthesised URLs the previous URL regex stopped short of and scheme-relative `//host/…` URLs it never matched. The recover-import crash-resume inference is restricted to genuine resume statuses (Running / Recovery Required / Partially Rolled Back): on a **Completed** transaction a missing Beets row is now refused (`recover_rollback_item_missing`) instead of inferred to be a crash and proceeding toward the file unlink (the content-hash guard is unchanged).
- **Recover My Uploads — rollback is now operator-reachable and its provenance/containment hardened (independent review).** The `recover_import_v1` rollback is wired into the existing `POST /api/transactions/<id>/rollback` route, so an authenticated operator can undo a recovery import from the UI (it dispatches to `TransactionStore.rollback`/`rollback_recover_import`, stays an explicit operator action, and is never automatic). Rollback provenance is now captured **authoritatively**: the import stamps a unique per-import token into the created item's `set_fields` and looks the item up by that exact token, so two concurrent imports of the same track can never cross-record and delete the wrong item's file (previously a fuzzy post-import `(artist, title)` re-query). Rollback containment now requires the recorded path to be under the **current** `MUSIC_ROOT` only — a recorded `music_root` that differs (MUSIC_ROOT changed since import) is refused rather than trusted. Crash-resume is corrected so a crash between the Beets-row removal and the progress persist still resumes (the content hash still gates the unlink). The non-duplicate Beets-skip import outcome now carries the `authorization` record like every other outcome. `own_account` URL verification namespaces a `/user/<name>` path segment by host, so an allowlisted `youtube.com/user/alice` can no longer verify a different platform's source whose bare uploader_id is `alice` (`@handle` and `channel_id` behaviour unchanged). Signed-URL query params (`X-Amz-Signature=…` etc.) embedded in the authorization note/evidence are stripped before persistence.
- **Recover My Uploads — a newly-imported recovery item can now be rolled back, safely and audited.** A recovery singleton import (`_recover_import_one` → `beets_adapter.run_import`, `copy=True`) previously created its `transaction_engine` "Import" transaction with `rollback_available=False` and no operations, so `transactions.rollback(tx_id)` raised "Rollback data has not been captured" — there was no way to undo a recovery import. The import now **captures the exact, minimal undo at import time** (through the audited `transaction_engine` store): the single added Beets item id and the single file it created under `MUSIC_ROOT`, with that file's sha256 **content hash + size** and the `MUSIC_ROOT` in force, recorded as the transaction's one `remove_recovered_item` operation with `rollback_available=True` (mutation family `recover_import_v1`). Capture is best-effort — if the copied file is not provably under `MUSIC_ROOT`, rollback stays unavailable rather than failing the import. Rolling back that transaction (`rollback_recover_import`, also dispatched by `TransactionStore.rollback`) removes **only** the recorded Beets item (through `beets_adapter`/the embedded engine — no raw sqlite) and the **single** recorded file, and **only** when **all** guards hold: the path is under `MUSIC_ROOT` and equals the recorded path (never a glob), with no symlinked component; the item id still maps to that path; and the file's current hash + size still match what was recorded at import (proof it is the file *this* import created, not something added/changed later). Any guard failure **refuses** the rollback and leaves the item and file in place; a hash/size mismatch (the recorded file was changed or replaced after import) is refused with `recover_rollback_tampered`. It never deletes via a wildcard, never touches any item or file not recorded by this transaction, never touches the staged original under `DOWNLOADS_ROOT`, and never asks Beets to delete files (the recorded file is deleted under the engine's own `MUSIC_ROOT`-containment guard, re-verified immediately before the unlink). The rollback transitions the transaction to a terminal **Rolled Back** status (or **Recovery Required** on an unexpected mid-rollback error) and logs the item id + path removed, with durable per-step progress so a crash between the DB-row removal and the file deletion resumes cleanly on retry. Authorization gate, DRM refusal, SSRF/host-allowlist guard, staged-root confinement, never-overwrite-on-import, and the identity gate are all unchanged.
- **Recover My Uploads — opus-in-.webm now imports.** A YouTube `bestaudio` upload stages as a `.webm` (opus) container, which passes authorization, integrity, and identity but which Beets/MediaFile adds **no library item** from ("beets import completed without adding item to library") even though the identical audio as `.opus` imports. The recovery import path (`_recover_import_one`) now, **before** calling `beets_adapter.run_import`, detects an unimportable container by its **actual ffprobe container** (not just the extension; `webm`/`matroska`) and produces a **lossless remux** (`ffmpeg -vn -map 0:a:0 -c:a copy`) into the codec's native importable container (opus/vorbis → `.opus`/`.ogg`, aac/alac → `.m4a`); a high-quality transcode is used only as a fallback when a remux cannot yield an importable file (and is recorded as not-lossless). The converted copy is written under `DOWNLOADS_ROOT/recovered` (staged-root-confined, never overwriting, NUL/symlink/hard-link-safe name) and is **verified before import** (ffprobe-decodable, has an audio stream, nonzero size, decoded duration within a tight tolerance of the original); verification failure returns a clean `import_failed` with a sanitized reason and never imports. The **original staged file is preserved untouched** and only the converted copy is imported. The conversion (original container, converted container, lossless-remux vs transcode) is recorded on the import outcome, the provenance sidecar, and the transaction metadata. Already-importable containers (`.opus`, `.m4a`, `.mp3`, `.flac`, `.ogg`) are imported unchanged. DRM refusal, authorization gate, SSRF/host-allowlist guard, identity gate, and never-overwrite rule are unchanged. Optional `AUDIO_FFMPEG_TIMEOUT` (default 180s) bounds the conversion.
- **Recover My Uploads — `RECOVER_OWNED_ACCOUNTS` now reaches the container.** The shipped Compose files (`docker-compose.yml`, `docker-compose.full.yml`) now forward `RECOVER_OWNED_ACCOUNTS` to the `beets-web-manager` service's `environment:` (`${RECOVER_OWNED_ACCOUNTS:-}`, matching the other optional operator vars), so an operator who sets the `own_account` allowlist in the stack `.env` actually has it take effect — previously it was documented and read at runtime but never plumbed into Compose, so `own_account` verification silently stayed inert in the shipped deployment.

### Added

- **Recover My Uploads — authorization + provenance.** Recover fetch and import now require an `authorization` object `{basis, note}` on the request. `basis` must be one of `original_creation`, `own_account`, `licensed_permission`, or `open_license`; a missing or invalid basis is refused with `authorization basis required (...)` (the probe/diagnostics routes stay open). The user's **asserted** claim is recorded separately from **independently verified** evidence on all three of the provenance sidecar, the recovery run record, and the import transaction metadata, as `authorization: {asserted_basis, asserted_note, verified, verified_basis, verified_evidence, platform, recorded_at}`; an asserted claim is never copied into a `verified_*` field. Two bases can be independently verified: `own_account`, when an operator-configured `RECOVER_OWNED_ACCOUNTS` entry matches one of the source's **immutable identifiers** — yt-dlp `channel_id`/`uploader_id` (exact, case-insensitive) or `channel_url`/`uploader_url` (canonical-URL / `@handle` / `/channel/<id>` / `/user/<name>` path-segment match), captured on probe and fetch and persisted on the sidecar; and `open_license`, when the source's own platform `license` metadata (captured from yt-dlp on probe/fetch and persisted as `source_license`) matches an open-license pattern (`creative commons`, `cc-by`, `cc0`, `public domain`, `reuse allowed`) — the verification uses the platform's license, never the user's claim. The **mutable display `uploader`/`channel` name is never evidence**, so a stranger's upload whose display name merely contains an allowlist token does not verify (impersonation-safe); allowlist entries shorter than 3 characters are ignored, and `RECOVER_OWNED_ACCOUNTS` entries are channel/profile IDs or canonical channel/profile URLs, not display names. `original_creation` and `licensed_permission` are asserted-only. The import gate uses this: a verified upload (own_account or open_license) proceeds through the existing AcoustID/MusicBrainz identity gate unchanged, while an asserted-only (unverified) upload is forced to `review_required` and is never auto-imported, so an operator can confirm it. The evaluated `authorization` record is now attached to **every** recovery outcome returned by the fetch and import paths — imported, skipped_existing, import_failed, and both the authorization-gate and identity-gate `review_required`/reject outcomes — so the asserted/verified claim is never dropped, and a retry forwards the originating run's authorization. DRM refusal, staged-root confinement, the never-overwrite-a-library-item rule, the identity gate, and the yt-dlp host-allowlist/SSRF guard are all unchanged. New config `RECOVER_OWNED_ACCOUNTS` (see `docs/CONFIGURATION.md`).
- **Recover My Uploads UI — authorization declaration.** The Recover panel now has a required **Basis for recovery** selector ("I created this recording" / "Uploaded to an account I control" / "Licensed/permitted archival copy" / "Public-domain or openly licensed") and an optional note, sent as `authorization: {basis, note}` on Fetch and Import. Fetch and Import are blocked with an inline message until a basis is chosen, and the backend's `authorization basis required` error surfaces if it comes back. Each run shows its recorded authorization — the asserted-basis label, the user note, and a **Verified** vs **Asserted (pending review)** badge driven only by the backend's `authorization.verified` — with copy stating that an asserted (unverified) claim is routed to review, not auto-imported. No verified state is fabricated client-side.
- **Fix album folders whose template placeholder was never substituted.** A new controlled-mutation apply complements the existing folder-placeholder *scan* (`POST /api/library/folder-placeholders/scan`): `POST /api/library/folder-placeholders/apply` renames album folders literally named like `Hot & Wet (2003){Album MbId}` (or `{Track ArtistMbId}`) to their clean name `Hot & Wet (2003)`. It is a preview-first (`dry_run` defaults true; applying requires `confirmed: true`), cancellable, resumable `job_engine` job that goes entirely through the transaction engine — never an ad-hoc `os.rename`. Disk-only folders (not tracked by Beets) are renamed through the `folder_cleanup_v1` engine (confined to `MUSIC_ROOT`, symlinks and an existing target refused, folder identity re-checked at apply, recorded for rollback). A DB-tracked folder (`db_item_count>0`) is **not** renamed by this flow — its clean destination is whatever Beets' path templates compute, which this flow cannot preview and which could merge into an existing folder via Beets suffixing — so it is held for review (`reason: "tracked_needs_relocation"`); relocate the album(s) directly through the album-relocation flow instead. Every row is re-verified live at apply time (source present, target still absent, still disk-only) to avoid races with the background disk reconcile, and the 52-ish `target_exists` collision cases are never auto-fixed — they are surfaced for manual review. `POST /api/library/folder-placeholders/rollback` (by `job_id` or explicit `operations`) restores every renamed folder from the audit records in one call. Triggering the apply from the existing Cleanup/placeholder-scan UI is a follow-up (frontend); the endpoints are independently callable.
- **Folder-placeholder apply is now safely resumable and recoverable on its error paths.** The apply job's "resumable" and "rollback-able after a partial failure" guarantees now hold end to end: a row that fails after leaving a rollback record keeps its operation id in the batch so one rollback-by-job reaches the partially-applied op; each rename is written ahead to the job checkpoint *before* it runs, and a vanished source on resume is recovered from the transaction store, so a crash between the rename and recording its op is still rollback-able; a persistent skip (e.g. an existing target) no longer consumes the `batch_limit` budget, so a batched run always makes progress across resumes; and an apply with any error now returns a failed result, so a partial or total failure is no longer recorded as a successful job. `resume_from_job` and rollback-by-`job_id` now also refuse a source job that is still running (whose checkpoint would miss its later ops).
- **Folder-placeholder apply: resume/batch/preview-consistency correctness fixes.** No data-safety regression existed; these harden recovery, budgeting, and preview-vs-apply agreement. (1) Resuming a partially-failed apply no longer duplicates the failed row's operation (a recovered op is now deduped by operation id), so rollback-by-job reports success instead of `rollback_not_eligible` after it has actually restored both folders. (2) A DB-tracked album whose tracks live under a **disc subfolder** is now detected descendant-aware at the top gate — matching the engine's own DB-reference refusal — so it is **held** (`reason: "tracked_needs_relocation"`) identically in preview and apply (previously preview said "would rename" while apply refused). (3) A held/`became_tracked` skip no longer consumes the `batch_limit` budget (only rows that actually mutate count), so a leading held album can no longer stall a batched resume. (4) A failed op that mutated nothing (e.g. an unapproved plan) is treated as a rollback no-op rather than a rollback failure. (5) Post-crash op recovery now matches the transaction's structured source instead of a JSON-substring query, so a folder name containing a quote or backslash is no longer silently missed.

## v0.11.0 - 2026-10-21

### Upgrade Notes

- **Playlist auto-sync no longer runs by default.** If you relied on the background playlist scheduler, it is now off after upgrade and manual/explicit syncs are unaffected. To restore automatic syncing, set `PLAYLIST_AUTO_SYNC=1` (accepts `1`/`true`/`yes`/`on`); tune the cadence with `PLAYLIST_AUTO_SYNC_INTERVAL` (default `300` s, minimum `60`). No migration or other operator action is required.

### Changed

- **Playlist auto-sync is now opt-in (default OFF).** `PLAYLIST_AUTO_SYNC` defaults to off; set it to `1`/`true`/`yes`/`on` to enable the background scheduler. Manual/explicit playlist sync is unchanged. When enabled, the scheduler no longer does a ~30 s first wake (the old `min(30, interval)`); it honors `PLAYLIST_AUTO_SYNC_INTERVAL` (default `300`, minimum `60`) from the first run.

### Fixed

- **Library background disk reconcile now reaches an already-open page, and polling is bounded regardless of `library_version` churn (follow-up to the fast-tree work below).** When a cache is served past its TTL the backend returns it with both `reconciled:true` and `reconciling:true` and refreshes it in the background; the Library view derived its "keep polling" state only from `reconciled === false`, so after the TTL it showed stale disk state and the background refresh never reached the open page until the user re-navigated. It now keeps polling while `reconciling === true` as well, and picks up the refreshed tree in place. The poll budget is also no longer reset when `library_version` changes, so a backend that churned the version on every response can no longer poll forever — it terminates at the bounded stall/retry UI.
- **Cold Library fast tree now shows albums and stops the frontend polling forever (follow-up to the fast-tree work below).** The cold-cache fast tree requested the album `year` field, which is not in the plugin's album field allowlist, so the read returned HTTP 400 and every album was silently dropped (only singletons showed); album year is now derived from the item rows instead. The cold fast response also emitted a fresh `library_version` on every call (it fell back to `time.time()` while the cache was cold), which reset the frontend's poll budget every 2 s; it now emits a stable version that only changes once reconciled data is available. The background disk reconcile is now scheduled only after the fast build succeeds, so an offline engine no longer queues a doomed reconcile job behind every failed page load.
- **Library view paints instantly and fills in disk state in the background.** The Library screen now renders from the first (possibly `reconciled:false`) `/api/library` response without a blocking spinner, shows a subtle non-blocking "checking disk" indicator while the backend reconciles disk state, and polls `/api/library` with a bounded backing-off cadence until `reconciled` becomes true (or the `library_version` changes) to pick up disk-only and missing-on-disk items. Polling stops once reconciled or after a bounded budget (a stalled check offers a manual retry instead of spinning). The heavy `include_tracks=1` pull is still fetched lazily (on track search / album expand), not on first paint.
- **Library listing no longer blocks on a synchronous NFS disk walk.** `GET /api/library` (without a `limit`) used to build its artists/albums tree by walking the whole music tree on disk on the request thread whenever the shared cache was cold or stale — and the cache was only kept warm by the opt-in legacy local scan, so on deployments with that scan disabled every page load paid for the full walk (measured ~120s to time out on a 411-album NFS library). The request path now serves the cached disk-reconciled payload when warm, otherwise an engine-only *fast tree* built with no music-tree/NFS disk walk (it still reads the small local artist-image cache, never the music tree), and runs the disk reconciliation (files missing on disk shown red, plus disk-only/not-imported files) as a deduped background `job_engine` job that warms the cache for later loads. The disk-missing/disk-only feature is preserved, just moved off the page-load critical path. The response carries a `reconciled` flag (`true` once the served payload includes disk state). `?refresh=1` now schedules a background rebuild instead of blocking the request. In-process callers that need the full disk-reconciled tree synchronously (the acquisition-queue build job, which already runs in the background) pass `block=True` and keep the previous behavior. Cold-cache loads when the engine is offline return the existing `ENGINE_OFFLINE` 503 instead of a 500.

## v0.10.0 - 2026-10-21

### Added

- **Recover My Uploads — stream diagnostics, safe import, retries & UI (increment 2).** The recovery path now, after fetch+stage, runs read-only stream/manifest/DRM diagnostics on each item (`inspect_stream_diagnostics`: HLS/DASH/SABR/progressive classification, container/codec, and EME/key-system *detection* only), verifies audio integrity and identity (AcoustID + MusicBrainz, with a safe singleton fallback for genuinely-original uploads), and imports into the library through `beets_adapter.run_import` **without ever overwriting an existing library item** (staged-root-confined; paths outside staging are refused). Run state is persisted so failed/unrecovered URLs can be retried, and playlist coverage is refreshed after import. New endpoints `GET /api/recover/runs`, `POST /api/recover/retry`, and `POST /api/recover/import` join the increment-1 probe/enumerate/fetch routes, and a new **Recover My Uploads** panel surfaces per-item recovery states (Available / Authentication required / Unsupported format / DRM-protected / Provider unavailable / Download failed / Recovered / Verified / Imported). A `DRM-protected` state is shown **only** on real EME/DRM evidence, never inferred from a failed download. `sanitize_diagnostic_report` accepts an external/laboratory streaming diagnostic (e.g. from the Streaming Security Lab's opt-in exporter), strips every secret field (content keys, KIDs, PSSH/init-data, licenses, cookies, tokens, headers) by recursive allowlisting, and unconditionally reports `decryption_supported=false` / `widevine_decryptable=false` — a ClearKey laboratory observation never implies commercial Widevine decryptability, and no DRM is circumvented.

## v0.9.0 - 2026-10-21

### Upgrade Notes

- **Breaking: Beets runs only inside Web Manager.** Embedded Beets is the only engine ([ADR 0006](docs/adr/0006-embedded-beets.md), Phase 2 cutover with the Phase 5 removal). The separate `beets` container, the HTTP transport to it, `BEETS_ENGINE` and the plugin provisioning into `/config/beetsplug` are gone. Before you pull this release, follow [Upgrading from two containers](docs/INSTALLATION.md#upgrading-from-two-containers): back up, `docker compose stop beets && docker compose rm -f beets`, delete the `beets` service, mount `/music` read-write in `beets-web-manager`, make sure `plugins:` in `config.yaml` lists `musicbrainz` (nothing adds it any more), remove the legacy settings, and `docker compose up -d --remove-orphans`. Your `config.yaml`, `musiclibrary.blb` and music folder are used as they are, with the same Beets 2.14.1.
- Rollback is the previous image: put back your previous Compose file (with its `beets` service) and pin `ghcr.io/iranman/beets-web-manager:0.8.1` ([Rolling back](docs/INSTALLATION.md#rolling-back)).
- Leftover `BEETS_ENGINE`, `BEETS_WEB_URL`, `BEETS_WEBMANAGER_API_KEY` or `BEETS_WEBMANAGER_API_KEY_FILE` never stop startup: Beets starts and the self-test reports `legacy_settings_ignored` naming them.
- `BEETS_OUTBOUND_ALLOWLIST` now defaults to `127.0.0.1:8337,localhost:8337` (`beets:8337` is gone with the service); `docker-compose.full.yml` forwards `bgutil-provider:4416`.

### Added

- Second-writer detection. A second Web Manager on the same library gets `library_locked` (an exclusive lock next to `musiclibrary.blb`). While the old `beets` service still answers (`separate_beets_running`, at `BEETS_WEB_URL` if set, else `http://beets:8337`) or a mutating `beet` command runs in the container (`beet_command_running`), every library write is refused with `BEETS_EMBEDDED_DEGRADED`; writes resume without a restart once it stops (#459).
- `library_dir_mismatch`: Beets' `directory:` and `MUSIC_ROOT` name different folders; writes are refused.
- The shipped Compose healthcheck fails while Beets' self-test fails (`beets.ok` false), so Docker reports the container unhealthy (#459 D3).
- Recorded-fixture autotag tests: Beets' own importer runs through the embedded engine against a recorded MusicBrainz response for a match, a refused match, MusicBrainz down and not found (`tests/test_beets_embedded_autotag.py`, N4).
- **Recover My Uploads (fetch + stage).** A new authorized acquisition path for your own YouTube/SoundCloud uploads that the search-only album downloader could not reach. `POST /api/recover/probe` and `POST /api/recover/enumerate` read a direct item URL or a channel/user/playlist/set URL (via `extract_flat`) through the unchanged yt-dlp guard (allowlisted hosts + public-IP/SSRF checks). `POST /api/recover/fetch` runs a tracked job that fetches each URL, keeping YouTube audio in its original lossy container unchanged (recorded as not-lossless) and SoundCloud's owner-enabled original file when available, stages it under `DOWNLOADS_ROOT`, gates each file on an ffprobe decode + duration check (rejecting `.part`/zero-byte/truncated files), and writes per-file provenance (platform, source URL, uploader, retrieval time, authorized-access flag, quality) for a later import increment. Owner-only content uses the operator's `YTDLP_COOKIE_FILE` (contents never read/logged); `GET /api/recover/cookie-state` reports `none`/`invalid`/`rejected`/`ok` by signature only. yt-dlp errors are classified (auth-needed / expired-URL / format / HLS-assembly / rate-limited / access-restricted / genuinely-DRM / misconfig) with a recoverable action; a format/HLS/auth failure is never labelled DRM, and no DRM is circumvented. This increment does not import into the library.

### Changed

- A Beets version other than the pin now stops Beets before it reads `config.yaml` or opens the library (`beets_version_mismatch`, #459 N-a).
- `web` and `webmanager` load even when `disabled_plugins:` lists them (#459 N-b).
- Without a `config.yaml`, Beets uses `library: /config/musiclibrary.blb` and `directory:` = `MUSIC_ROOT`, and reports `config_missing`.
- CI: `stock-beets-acceptance` runs the embedded-engine acceptance against the pinned upstream Beets; `compose-verification` and `production-docker-acceptance` check the single-container Compose files and refuse a separate Beets service; `beets-latest-canary` tests the newest Beets from PyPI. Job names are unchanged.

### Removed

- The two-container setup: the `beets` service in every Compose file, `examples/docker-compose.external-beets.yml` and `examples/docker-compose.embedded.yml` (`docker-compose.yml` is now the single-container file), and the stock Beets image pin.
- The HTTP transport and shared-key client to Beets, and the settings `BEETS_ENGINE`, `BEETS_WEB_URL`, `BEETS_WEBMANAGER_API_KEY` and `BEETS_WEBMANAGER_API_KEY_FILE`.
- Plugin provisioning into `/config/beetsplug` and the startup `config.yaml` edits, with the routes `POST /api/plugins/provision`, `POST /api/setup/plugins/provision` and `POST /api/setup/beets-config/include-paths`, and the Setup/System buttons that called them. The opt-in recommended-plugins edit remains.

### Fixed

- Embedded Beets: a fresh install with no `config.yaml` now passes `/health/ready` (HTTP 200) instead of returning 503. A missing config is no longer a readiness blocker (the engine falls back to Beets' defaults and creates an empty library), and required-plugin health is derived from the effective runtime overlay (which always loads `web` and `webmanager`) rather than the on-disk `plugins:` list. A genuinely broken engine or a required plugin that fails to load still fails readiness.
- YouTube and SoundCloud playlist entries now match library tracks that are actually present far more often. These sources routinely put the real "Artist - Title" (or the compact "Title-ARTIST" that SoundCloud/SpotiFLAC use) inside the video title while the uploader field is a VEVO, "- Topic" or aggregator channel that is not the artist (for example uploader "DrakeVEVO" or "TrapNation", title "Travis Scott - SICKO MODE (Official Video)"). Playlist matching can now recover the real artist/title by splitting the entry title and checking it against the library. To avoid mis-mapping a different track, deriving an artist from the title is default-deny: it is attempted only for an entry with no artist at all, or an untrustworthy uploader/channel field paired with the original title. Any established artist — upstream Spotify/YouTube metadata, a pasted "Artist - Title" line, a channel-name promotion, an m3u EXTINF label, or a file's own tags — is trusted, and its title is never re-split to invent a different artist (so the deduplication matcher, which runs on file tags, never does so either). On top of that, the order-reversed reading is tried only for the genuinely order-ambiguous compact "Title-ARTIST" shape (never for the unambiguous spaced "Artist - Title"), and a split half that is only punctuation or a version/format label ("Live", "Remix", "Official Video", "Sped-Up!") is never treated as an artist. It changes nothing about what is downloaded, and uses no AcoustID or MusicBrainz lookup and no network call, so it works even with those providers unavailable.

- Embedded Beets: the engine now re-asserts its own Library onto the process-global stock Beets web app before every in-process request, instead of trusting whatever is in `app.config["lib"]`. Nothing in production overwrites that global, but if another caller in the process ever leaves a closed/foreign Library there, a Beets transaction on the closed connection would strand Beets' per-database lock (its `__exit__` releases the lock only after a `commit()` that then raises), deadlocking every later Beets call. The engine is now immune to that. This fixes the `unit-tests`/`security` CI jobs hanging to the 6-hour ceiling: under the worker pool, test modules that bind the shared web app to their own (then torn-down) Library poisoned the engine for later modules (#466).

### Development

- `scripts/run_tests.py` now fails the run, after dumping every worker's thread stacks, if no test unit finishes within `RUN_TESTS_STALL_TIMEOUT` seconds (default 900), so a wedged worker surfaces in minutes with evidence instead of blocking to GitHub's 6-hour job ceiling (#466).
- `tests/test_plugin_import_autotag.py` now pins Beets' metadata-source lookup to none per test, so its documented "no metadata-source plugin loaded, so no candidates" path stays deterministic and offline even when a sibling in-process test leaves a metadata source (e.g. musicbrainz) in Beets' process-global plugin registry (#466).
- `test_shipped_example_config_loads_every_plugin` is skipped (with reason) when `ffmpeg` is not on PATH, and runs its full assertion otherwise. config.yaml.example enables `replaygain` with the ffmpeg backend, which needs `ffmpeg` to load; it ships in the published image (where the acceptance jobs validate the shipped config) but is absent on the bare CI runner, where that one plugin legitimately cannot load (#466).

### Security

- Embedded Beets: the browser can no longer change `import.set_fields` or `replace` in `config.yaml` (#461). `set_fields` can set any field, including a file's path, before the import moves or copies the file. `replace` holds the rules that keep `paths` and `art_filename` results inside the music folder. Path templates (`paths`, `art_filename`) stay editable while the file uses Beets' default `replace` rules; with its own `replace` or an `include`, they too are edited on the server. Make these edits in `config.yaml` on the server.
- Config editor (every mode): a save or revert whose YAML uses aliases (`*name`, including `<<: *name` merges), has a key that is not text (such as `1:`), or is larger than 1 MiB is refused as invalid before it is parsed into data, so a few lines can no longer make parsing and the embedded guard's comparison run for minutes while holding the config write lock (#461). YAML nested too deeply, or with a value that cannot be read (such as an impossible date), is now a validation error instead of a server error. Write aliased values out in full.
- The documentation no longer says the allowed plugins run nothing external: `chroma` runs the fixed `fpcalc` helper, and `fetchart`'s `cover_art_url` source fetches URLs read from file tags, outside Web Manager's outbound policy.

## v0.8.1 - 2026-10-20

### Highlights

- **Beets now runs embedded inside Web Manager.** One container instead of two: Beets 2.14.1 runs in the Web Manager process against your existing `config.yaml`, `musiclibrary.blb` and music folder, with no Beets container, no HTTP bridge and no plugin to provision. Turn it on with `BEETS_ENGINE=embedded` (single-container Compose: `examples/docker-compose.embedded.yml`); it becomes the default in the next release phase. Setup, moving from two containers and rollback: `docs/INSTALLATION.md`. Self-test codes and fixes: `docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md`. Planned next ([ADR 0006](docs/adr/0006-embedded-beets.md)): embedded by default, explainable matching, first-class partial imports, idempotent staging. Inside the image, `beet` now finds the bundled `webmanager` and `discpath` plugins (`PYTHONPATH=/app`).

### Added

- Beets can run inside Web Manager (`BEETS_ENGINE=embedded`, opt-in; [ADR 0006](docs/adr/0006-embedded-beets.md), Phase 1). Web Manager loads Beets 2.14.1 as a pinned Python library, reads the same `config.yaml` (from `BEETSDIR`), and opens the same `musiclibrary.blb` and music folder through Beets' own Library API. Every Beets call runs in-process through the existing `webmanager` operations, with no HTTP, no plugin provisioning and no plugin version that can drift from Web Manager's. A startup self-test checks the following and reports a stable code and a fix for each failure:
  - the Beets version against the pin;
  - that `config.yaml` parses;
  - that the library opens (a newly created empty library is flagged);
  - that the music and library folders are writable;
  - that every configured Beets plugin loaded;
  - that `fpcalc` and `ffmpeg` are present.

  `/api/health` lists the failing codes, and `/api/health/detail` (authenticated) has the messages. While any error-level check fails, Beets reads keep working but every library change is refused with that check's message (`BEETS_EMBEDDED_DEGRADED`); a Beets version that differs from the pin is an error. The plugins the image ships (`webmanager`, `discpath`) are always loaded from the image; if a copy elsewhere would be used instead, the engine does not start. In embedded mode Web Manager never edits `config.yaml` at startup, and the browser may change only an allowlist of `config.yaml` settings and add only plugins that run no external command or user-written expression; any other change from the Config page (save or revert) or Setup's plugin fixes is refused (`CONFIG_CODE_EXECUTION_EDIT_REFUSED`), and plugin provisioning is refused, because what they run would now run inside Web Manager (see [CONFIGURATION.md](docs/CONFIGURATION.md)); edit those in the file directly. The default stays `http` (the separate Beets container), and the shipped Compose files are unchanged until the Phase 2 cutover. `requirements.txt` now pins `beets==2.14.1` and `pyacoustid==1.3.1`.

### Fixed

- webmanager plugin 1.18.4: a missing-track repair whose files name only some of the Release's credited artists (for example "38 Spesh" for "38 Spesh & Harry Fraud") is no longer skipped for its artist penalty alone. The plugin sets the album-level `artist` penalty aside only when every guard holds: the library album being repaired (`existing_album_id`, now sent by Web Manager and checked by the plugin) is on the pinned Release; chroma is loaded and AcoustID matched every file to the recording of the Release track Beets mapped it to; and every artist name in the source tags, split on `,` `;` `/` `&` `x` `and` `feat.` `ft.` `featuring` `with` and compared after Unicode and case normalization, is one of the Release's credited artists (never a Various Artists release, never a blank artist). The `album` penalty and every track-level penalty stay in, and the rest must still be under `match.strong_rec_thresh`. Such an import is listed in the plugin's `partial_artist_relaxed` response field and logged in the import transaction. Only an AcoustID result from this import counts: a Web Manager import drops chroma's cached results for its files before chroma fingerprints them again. An explicit `match.max_rec.data_source` cap is still honoured. Restart the Beets container to load 1.18.4.
- webmanager plugin 1.18.3: a pinned import (one `search_ids` Release) is no longer skipped because of Beets' `data_source` penalty. With a second metadata source loaded (chroma, deezer), Beets adds that penalty to the album and to every track of any source whose files carry no `data_source`, which is every download. On stock Beets 2.14.1 a complete, fully tagged copy of a Release scored 0.066 against it (strong threshold 0.04), so `beet import -q --search-id` skipped it, and a missing-track repair stayed far over the bar. The plugin now judges the one pinned Release without that penalty, at album level and inside each track's distance, for complete folders and for missing-track repairs (`allow_missing_tracks`, which still also sets `missing_tracks` aside). Everything else keeps Beets' own bar: the pinned Release must be the best candidate, no extra files, no other penalty capped below strong, and the remaining distance under `match.strong_rec_thresh`. Restart the Beets container to load 1.18.3.
- webmanager plugin 1.18.2: the plugin's log messages now include their values. Beets creates the `beets.webmanager` logger as its own `str.format` logger when the plugin loads, so the plugin's `%s`-style messages (for example the warning about a dropped allowed mutation root) were logged as raw templates without their arguments. The plugin now logs through `beets.webmanager.ops`, a standard logger that Beets never replaces; records still reach `beets.webmanager` handlers. Restart the Beets container to load 1.18.2 (the rollout script does this when the plugin version changes).
- Missing-track downloads: a downloaded file is now accepted when AcoustID links its fingerprint to several MusicBrainz recordings and the requested recording has the top score (alone or tied). Before, any other song in the top tier made it "ambiguous" and the file was rejected. A requested recording below another song's higher score, a score under the floor, or no AcoustID result are still not accepted.
- Missing-track downloads: one file that fails fingerprint verification no longer fails the whole batch. Verified tracks are imported, unverified files are removed from the staging folder, and their tracks are logged and returned (`undelivered_tracks`) as still missing. A Soulseek batch with undelivered tracks tries direct sources for them. The job still fails when no file verifies.
- Missing-track downloads no longer leave a second staged copy and a Review row for every failed peer or source (#427). The import now uses a folder that the app already staged under `_beets_missing_import` in place, as long as the folder holds exactly the selected files. Each failed attempt now leaves one staged folder with one Review row that points at it. The acquisition cleanup never deletes a staged folder that has a Review row. If the Review queue cannot be read, the cleanup keeps the folder. Staged audio from earlier attempts is never deleted automatically: it stays until you resolve or dismiss its Review row.

### Development

- The backend test suite now runs in parallel. `scripts/run_tests.py` runs the same tests as `python -m unittest discover` in one worker process per CPU, with the same summary and exit code. The test image and the `unit-tests` workflow use it. A full local run on 12 cores drops from about 21 minutes to about 4. `docs/DEVELOPMENT.md` has the commands and memory sizing.

## v0.8.0 - 2026-10-19

### Security

- Closed CodeQL `py/path-injection` alerts #1411-#1416 and #1422 by stating existing path containment in a form static analysis can verify. The transaction store now also checks that a transaction file path stays inside its directory, in addition to the id format check. The Review Queue folder-delete route confines the path to the cleanup roots before it resolves anything. State JSON writes go through the checked path in its symlink-resolved directory, and a symlinked state file is replaced rather than written through. The temporary file is created exclusively, so a symlink planted at its name cannot redirect the write. Requests that were accepted before are still accepted, and refusals return the same messages.

### Upgrade Notes

- Nothing changes for a running stack: Docker Compose keeps whatever `image:` your own Compose file names. If your `beets` service still says `lscr.io/linuxserver/beets:latest` and you want the same protection, replace that line with the pinned reference under Changed below. If `docker inspect beets --format '{{index .Config.Labels "org.opencontainers.image.version"}}'` already reports `2.14.1-ls356`, it is the image you run now, so Beets keeps its version. If it reports anything else, changing the line is a Beets version change: follow `docs/BEETS_ENGINE_MIGRATION.md` first.
- Restart the Beets container so it loads webmanager plugin 1.18.1. The rollout script does this itself when the plugin version changes. Until Beets restarts, the Library page keeps working through the slower full reads.
- With a wrong or missing Web Manager API key (`BEETS_WEBMANAGER_API_KEY` or the key file) the Library page now fails with a Beets authentication error. Its library read now goes through the authenticated webmanager plugin; before, it used Beets' unauthenticated `/item/` list and loaded anyway. Fix the key as described in docs/TROUBLESHOOTING.md.

### Changed

- The Library page's first load after a restart (and after any 3-minute gap) reads much less from Beets. Web Manager now asks the webmanager plugin for only the dozen item and album fields the page uses (`GET /webmanager/fields/<item|album>`, plugin 1.18.0, capability `library_fields`) instead of Beets' full `/item/` and `/album/` lists, which carry every field and check every file's size on disk. On a synthetic 3,960-track library in the stock Beets image the item read went from 6.7 s and 10.4 MB to 1.1 s and 1.2 MB. With an older plugin Web Manager keeps using the full lists.
- The shipped Compose files no longer float on `lscr.io/linuxserver/beets:latest`. `docker-compose.yml`, `docker-compose.full.yml`, the `docker-compose.dev.yml` default and the install examples pin `lscr.io/linuxserver/beets:2.14.1-ls356@sha256:ae076820ec34ea5e9ec05a22931e98ab555e9c25e784c5fb001c84f411119c73` (Beets 2.14.1): the image CI has been testing, and the one `latest` pointed to on 2026-10-19. Beets upgrades now arrive as reviewed PRs that re-run the stock-Beets acceptance, instead of on the next `docker compose pull`; upstream Beets 2.13 and 2.14 each broke Web Manager that way. CI reads the pin from `docker-compose.yml`, `scripts/validate_compose_security.py` now rejects `:latest` on the `beets` service, and a new weekly, non-blocking `beets-latest-canary` workflow runs the acceptance against `latest` to catch upstream breakage early. See "Beets image version" in `docs/CONFIGURATION.md` and "Upgrading the pinned Beets image" in `docs/BEETS_ENGINE_MIGRATION.md`.

### Fixed

- webmanager plugin 1.18.1: a missing-track repair import (`allow_missing_tracks`) no longer skips Beets' `import_task_before_choice` event. A plugin that answers it, such as badfiles with `import_action_on_error: skip`, can now veto the partial match as it can any stock import; with no such plugin the repair imports exactly as before. `/webmanager/status` still lists `library_fields`, now from the plugin's fixed capability list. Restart the Beets container to load 1.18.1 (the rollout script does this when the plugin version changes).
- Completing an incomplete album (Download All / missing-track repair) always failed with "Beets could not confidently match this source to the selected MusicBrainz release", then downloaded the album again from the next Soulseek peer. The staged folder holds only the missing tracks, and Beets' quiet importer never applies a release with missing tracks (`match.max_rec.missing_tracks: medium`, and each missing track counts in the distance), even with the release pinned. A missing-track repair now asks the webmanager plugin (1.17.0) to accept the pinned release for that folder when the best candidate is that release, every file sits on one of its tracks, no other penalty is capped below strong, and the distance without the missing-track penalty is under `match.strong_rec_thresh`. AcoustID must still confirm every file before it is merged into the existing album; anything less still goes to Review. Restart the `beets` container so it loads plugin 1.17.0.

- A missing-track repair could report success while its tracks were never merged into the existing album. When the engine reconcile (plan or apply) failed or raised, the job checked the newly imported album instead, which carried the right release, so the job succeeded. The existing album stayed incomplete and a second, partial album row was left in the library. The job now fails and sends the folder to Review with `[reason_code=existing_album_merge_failed]`, and the log names the separate imported album row so it can be cleaned up through Review / Transactions; this job never removes it. A reported success is also read back: every imported item for a requested track must now belong to the existing album. Requested tracks the source did not deliver do not fail the job; they are listed in the job log and in its result (`undelivered_tracks`). When Review could not be written, the log says so instead of claiming it. Download All stops at that failure: it does not try other Soulseek peers or direct sources, because each would import again and leave another partial album row (see #428). Format replacement also sets `allow_missing_tracks`, because its source is a single track; AcoustID still gates it. The imported file for the slot being replaced now stays on its own album row for the replacement Preview instead of being planned as a move into the occupied slot; a second imported file for that slot is held for review. If a replacement source also holds tracks for free slots, those tracks are still merged into the existing album, like a missing-track fill. A failed reconcile fails the replacement the same way.

- Import Review: after Delete Folder, the success message read "Deleted undefined audio file(s)", and the item left the list before its message could be seen. The message now gives the real count from the server, split into deleted and quarantined files, and also appears on the page status line. The message says "Delete Folder finished", not "removed", because skipped symlinks or a failed final folder removal can leave the folder on disk. The frontend response type now matches what the endpoint returns (#275).

### Dependencies

- Frontend: next 16.4.0, @mui/material 9.5.0, @tanstack/react-query 5.104.2, postcss 8.5.30 (dependency and override), vitest 5.0.4.
- yt-dlp PO-token provider bgutil-ytdlp-pot-provider 2.0.2, kept in step across the pip plugin (`requirements.txt` and the `YTDLP_BGUTIL_PIP_PACKAGE` runtime default) and the `docker-compose.full.yml` server image (digest-pinned). 2.0.2 refreshes vulnerable server lockfile dependencies; the plugin code is unchanged apart from its version string.

## v0.7.2 - 2026-10-15

### Fixed

- Download All (and format replacement) killed an album download at 40 minutes even while it was still making progress. A slow Soulseek run that had just downloaded every requested track was cancelled before its import, and the files were removed. The wait is now progress-aware: past 40 minutes it keeps going while the download logs progress, and gives up only after 25 minutes with no progress (longer than any silent inner step, such as waiting for the import slot).

## v0.7.1 - 2026-10-14

### Fixed

- A missing-track download into an existing album was refused and sent to Review whenever AcoustID returned no release-level hits for the album, even though every file fingerprinted to a requested recording. The import preflight now accepts per-track proof when all of these hold: the target is the existing album's own release and Release Group; every file's AcoustID lookup confirms a recording on that release (same confidence floor as before); each recording is a requested track; and no recording is held twice. Anything less still goes to Review.
- A refused import now carries a stable reason code (`[reason_code=<code>]` in the job error, `refusal_reason_code` in the review evidence), such as `acoustid_unavailable`, `file_unverified` or `recording_not_on_release`.
- An slskd album download no longer downloads one peer's copy after another when the import is refused for a reason that is not about the files (`missing_track_scope`, `local_scan_unavailable`, `acoustid_unavailable`). It cleans up that one candidate, logs one line naming the reason code, and goes on to the direct-source fallback as before. A refusal about the files themselves, or with no reason code, still tries the next peer.
- Cancelling a failed slskd candidate's transfers always failed ("could not cancel N queued transfer(s)"): the request named the file, but slskd cancels by transfer id. It now looks the ids up first, so the transfers are cancelled and removed from slskd.
- A file that could not be fingerprinted (corrupt, under 5 seconds, or `fpcalc` timed out) now refuses a missing-track import with `file_unfingerprintable`, which is specific to that download, instead of `acoustid_unavailable`. `acoustid_unavailable` now means only that AcoustID itself is not configured, rejected the key, is down or is rate limited, or that `fpcalc` is missing or cannot be run. Both still refuse the import.
- If the downloads folder could not be checked (for example a stale network mount answering `Permission denied`), opening the Review Queue read it as empty and the next queued item overwrote the saved queue. A failed check now keeps every item.
- A refusal reason code is now read only from the fixed import refusal message, so a Soulseek peer cannot stop the peer search by putting `[reason_code=...]` in a file name.
- slskd transfer cancellation never sends a request for a `.` or `..` username or transfer id.
- The Review Queue could be wiped: if Beets was unavailable while an item was being queued, or the queue file could not be read, the saved queue was replaced by just the new item (or by nothing, when one item was removed). A failed load is now never saved over the queue (adding or removing an item answers 503 and changes nothing), a Beets outage during the queue's self-cleanup keeps every item, and queue saves are atomic.
- The Review Queue no longer lists an item whose downloads folder has been deleted (for example a failed download candidate that was cleaned up). The item is auto-resolved.

## v0.7.0 - 2026-10-14

### Upgrade Notes

- The image now ships Deno (`/usr/local/bin/deno`, `DENO_DIR=/tmp/deno`), so YouTube downloads need no extra JavaScript runtime setup. The image is about 96 MB larger uncompressed. Nothing to change if you use the hardened Compose settings (`/tmp` tmpfs).
- `YTDLP_REMOTE_COMPONENTS` is now operator-only: set it in the Compose environment. The Settings page no longer saves it, so a value saved there before is no longer applied.
- The Import page's batch import (`POST /api/ai-batch-import`) is now the deterministic import: Beets imports strong matches, MusicBrainz and AcoustID identify the rest, and AI is never called. It no longer needs an AI provider. Anything it cannot prove goes to the Review Queue.
- The batch import now refuses, and sends to review, a folder holding any symlink or junction (`symlinked_audio`) and a folder it cannot verify: outside every import root, partly unreadable, or with no readable audio (`unverifiable_folder`). Replace such links with real folders to import them.
- A source folder holding any hardlinked file (for example a seeding torrent or a Lidarr hardlink), or a file that cannot be checked, is now copied into the library instead of moved, so its folder stays in Downloads.

- `YTDLP_PO_PROVIDER_URL` is now blank by default, so the bgutil PO token provider is used only when you set it (`docker-compose.full.yml` still sets it for its sidecar).
- SpotiFLAC is no longer in the default slskd fallback chain (now `ytdlp,soundcloud`), and a download request (Find Music, Download All, track replacement) can no longer add or reorder sources: the chain comes only from `SLSKD_FALLBACK_METHODS`. yt-dlp (YouTube) is always in it: a list that leaves `ytdlp` out gets it added before `soundcloud`, or at the end. To keep using SpotiFLAC, list `spotiflac` there and install its CLI. Track replacement now uses a direct-source fallback only when its "try an alternate source" setting is on.
- Playlist downloads no longer try SpotiFLAC by default either: the **Auto** chain (`PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_METHODS`) is now `slskd,ytdlp,soundcloud`. To keep using it, set `PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_METHODS=slskd,spotiflac,ytdlp,soundcloud` (and install its CLI), or pick SpotiFLAC as the method for one download.
- The Review Queue no longer lists library albums and tracks missing MusicBrainz IDs. They moved to `GET /api/library/missing-mb-ids` (the Library "Missing IDs" view). Scripts that call `GET /api/import/review-queue?status=library_no_mb` get an empty list with `"moved_to": "/api/library/missing-mb-ids"` for one minor release, and `counts.library_no_mb` is gone from the review queue response.
- `POST /api/import-review/revalidate` with `all: true`, or with more than 25 `review_item_ids`, now starts a job (`import-review-revalidate`, on the Jobs page) and answers 202 `{"ok": true, "job_id", "mode": "job"}`; the job's result keeps the counts and at most 200 compact rows (`review_item_id`, `path`, `status` of `failed`/`queued`/`eligible`/`revalidated`, `error`) with `items_truncated`, instead of every item's full match and track mapping. A second start while one runs answers 409 `job_already_running` with the running job's id. Up to 25 ids still answer synchronously, as before.
- `POST /api/ai-pending-review` now refuses a `path` outside the Import Review source roots (downloads, playlist downloads, torrent sources), inside the music library, or equal to a root, with 400. From `suggestion` it keeps only the proposed identity (`mb_albumid`, `mb_releasegroupid`, `representative_mb_albumid`, `album`, `albumartist`, `artist`, `year`, `label`, `country`, `reason`; strings capped at 500 characters); `evidence`, `mb_url`, `review_evidence`, `mb_valid`, `confidence`, preflight, track mapping and status fields are dropped, so an item added this way is never ready for auto-import until it is revalidated. `allow_existing` must be `true` exactly; `suggestion.allow_existing_review` is ignored. Origin labels are stored as plain strings of at most 500 characters, and `source_folder` is always the validated folder. A Pending Review entry stored before this check (or edited on disk) no longer authorizes a folder delete or a ready-items auto-import unless its path still resolves under an approved root (a symlink may lead to another download root, never from outside the music library into it). Such a folder delete or file cleanup is now refused at the Pending Review check ("Folder is not in Pending Review, or a Needs MB ID album row match." / "Review item does not match the source folder.") rather than with the cleanup planner's outside-root, root-itself or symlink message.
- A non-integer `limit` on the Import endpoints (`/api/import-log`, `/api/import-skipped`, `/api/ai-match-history`, `/api/ai-review-decisions`, `/api/unmatched-tracks`, `/api/import-review/revalidate`, `/api/import-review/auto-enqueue-ready` and its `/job`) is now 400 `INVALID_PARAM` instead of a 500 (or, for auto-enqueue-ready, a silent 5).
- Import Review "Purge rejected files" (`delete_rejected` / `delete_duplicate` on `POST /api/import/review-files/cleanup`) now quarantines the files instead of deleting them (#380). Whether a file is rejected is the browser's claim, and the server cannot prove it, so the files go to `IMPORT_REVIEW_QUARANTINE_DIR`, where the cleanup can be rolled back. The response says `requested_action`, `action` (what ran) and `downgraded_to_quarantine`.
- `GET /api/acquisition/queue` without `limit` now returns one page of 500 rows (`limit: 500` in the response) instead of the whole list; page with `offset`. Its `refresh=1` argument is ignored: rebuild the list with `POST /api/acquisition/queue/refresh` (#385 F3).

### Known Limitations

- A symlink directly in the batch import scan root (for example a linked album folder in Downloads) is only logged ("symlink not followed, never imported"); it gets no review row.
- Cancelling a batch import while a folder is in its pinned import (Stage 2b) leaves no History row for that folder (N2).
- A crash during a Stage 1 Beets import move leaves no History row for that folder (N3).
- Re-running the batch import over a torrent source that was already copied creates a duplicate Review Queue row (N5).
- Skip pressed between two folders of a batch import is ignored; it applies only while a folder is being processed.
