- A folder whose `cover.jpg` is a symlink is sent to review like any other folder holding a link.
- Library > Missing IDs batch: only one preview and one apply can run at a time; rows cannot be deselected in the preview; each row's child transaction records the system actor rather than the user; the one-time `confirm_token` is visible in the running preview job's result.

### Fixed

- `POST /api/import-review/auto-enqueue-ready`: a row handled by Music Format Preferences now returns the fixed review note instead of the import error's text (CodeQL #1423). The stored review note still lists what was done with each file.
- An album the batch import added without a MusicBrainz Release Group was sent to "Library: missing IDs", but that view listed only albums without a Release ID, so the album appeared nowhere. Library > Missing IDs now also lists albums that have a Release ID but no Release Group (`missing_reason: "missing_release_group"`, `missing_id_type: "Release Group ID"`, `current_mb_albumid`). The Missing IDs batch fills that Release Group only from MusicBrainz's lookup of the album's own Release ID, writes nothing else, and refuses the row otherwise (`release_group_not_resolved`, `embedded_release_group_conflict`, `item_release_id_conflict`, `symlinked_item_path`, `duplicate_release_group`; a MusicBrainz outage is `evidence_unavailable`). A failed fill that already changed the album names its Metadata Update in the row's `transaction_ids`, and so does any Missing IDs row (fill or match) whose write committed but did not verify on re-read (`recovery_required`). These rows are not sent to Batch AI suggest, are left out of `/api/unmatched-tracks`, and cannot be deleted or quarantined from Missing IDs.
- Batch import discovery dropped a folder that held only symlinked audio or a symlinked subfolder without a word. Such a folder is now queued and refused as `symlinked_audio` with a review row, and each link is logged by name. Links are still never followed or imported. The Review Queue no longer drops a `symlinked_audio` or `unverifiable_folder` row as resolved because its folder shows no audio, while that folder still exists.
- Match album (`POST /api/albums/<id>/match`) now rolls the album's new Release ID back when the tag write fails. Before, only a failed track sync or relocation was rolled back.
- Import Review "Purge/Quarantine rejected files" acted on no files (#387 NB1): the page sends each file's absolute path, and cleanup skipped every absolute path. An absolute path is now accepted when it lies under the review folder (compared as text, no decoding), and only that file is quarantined; paths outside the folder, or with `.`/`..` parts, are still skipped. The response now also has `remaining_audio_count` and `pending_review_removed`, which the page already expected.
- The Review Queue's "Clean up stale" message now shows how many entries were removed because their album is already resolved in Beets. Before, it read a `removed_mb_in_library` field that the server does not send, so that count showed as "undefined" (F12).
- Library > Missing IDs: an album that has a Release ID but no Release Group now shows "Needs release group" with its current Release ID beside it, and the album tab and count say "no Release or Release Group ID". In the batch preview such a row reads "Add release group (keeps release)" instead of a full match, and its refusals are named: MusicBrainz did not return a release group for the release, or a track's tags name a different release group or release. Such a row offers no Delete Folder or file cleanup (the server refuses both for it); it points to Fix proven matches instead, and the apply dialog says a Release Group fill writes tags only and moves no files. The page works unchanged when the server does not send these fields.
- An slskd download without `SLSKD_API_KEY` no longer fails deep inside the job. Without a fallback it is refused with `slskd is not configured (SLSKD_API_KEY)`. With a fallback it goes straight to the direct sources, and the job log says why. The acquisition queue now reports `download_sources` (slskd configured, and whether each fallback can run).
- When slskd fails, the job log shows its real reason (rejected API key, rate limiting, timeout, unreachable, unexpected response), and that reason comes first in the final error. These are fixed messages that never include provider bodies or keys.
- Direct-source fallbacks that cannot run here (yt-dlp missing, no JavaScript runtime for YouTube, no SpotiFLAC CLI) are skipped once per job, with the reason in the log, instead of each failing in turn.
- A configured PO token provider that is blocked by the outbound policy or unreachable no longer fails YouTube downloads. They continue without PO tokens, and a warning is logged.
- SoundCloud missing-track downloads now try up to five search results in order. Results whose length is more than 10% off the MusicBrainz length are skipped, as are clips of 45 s or less (previews), and DRM-protected results. Identity verification after download is unchanged.
- yt-dlp per-chunk `[download] x%` progress lines are no longer written to the job log (0% and 100% are kept), so a long download keeps the start of the job in the saved log.
- The Lidarr wanted list is read in pages of 1000 until `totalRecords` is covered, de-duplicated by Lidarr album id, with a 60 s timeout per page and one retry after a timeout or 5xx. Large wanted lists no longer time out after 10 s per 100-album page. A failed page, a page shorter than `totalRecords` promises while `totalRecords` has not changed, or fewer albums in all than the last page's `totalRecords` (the list changed during the read) still means no list, never a partial one.
- The Import page's batch import (`POST /api/ai-batch-import`) works again. Before, every batch failed while it looked for folders, because it called a folder-discovery helper with arguments the helper does not take. The batch now walks the chosen folder itself: each folder that holds audio is one album, and `Disc N` / `CD N` subfolders count as part of their album folder. Hidden folders and symlinks are skipped. Audio files lying directly in the downloads folder are not imported; the job log lists them.
- The batch import no longer needs an AI provider (#288). It matches with Beets, MusicBrainz and AcoustID (see the next entry) and sends everything it cannot prove to the Review Queue. The batch status has `ai_configured` so the page can say whether AI ranking is available in the Review Queue.
- The batch import is now deterministic end to end (#288, phase B). Each folder is first imported by Beets itself (quiet import; only a strong match is imported). A folder Beets skips is identified with MusicBrainz and AcoustID and sent to the Review Queue with the evidence, including why Beets skipped it. It is imported automatically, pinned to the identified Release, only when the identification has complete fingerprint proof, and only if the imported album then carries that Release Group; otherwise it goes to review. AI is never called by the batch, with or without a key. When Beets fingerprinting or an AcoustID key is unavailable, Beets' strong matches are still imported, and the batch status and log show a warning. An imported album without a Release Group is put in review instead of being counted as done. A folder Beets imported only in part gets a Review Queue row listing the skipped paths. A folder holding any symlink or junction (any linked file, whatever its name, or any linked folder, even an empty one) is never handed to Beets; it goes to review as `symlinked_audio`, as does a folder whose path runs through a symlink below the configured import root it lives under. A symlinked import root itself (or a link above it, such as a mount) is allowed. A folder outside every configured import root, a folder (or subfolder) Web Manager cannot read, and a folder with no audio file Web Manager can read go to review as `unverifiable_folder`. Torrent sources are still copied and other sources moved; a folder holding any hardlinked file (another copy shares it, e.g. a seeding torrent or a Lidarr hardlink), or a file that cannot be checked, is also copied, never moved. Every pinned import (batch or not) re-checks this right before planning. A stored folder path that is not absolute, or holds `.`/`..` segments, is refused as `unverifiable_folder`; the review evidence lists the links (`symlinked_paths`) or the folders that could not be listed (`unreadable_paths`). The batch scan root may now be the symlinked spelling of a configured root (a NAS mount link to `DOWNLOADS_ROOT`): it scans the configured root itself instead of failing with "outside the allowed roots"; paths below a root are checked as before. Folders now run one at a time inside the batch job (no extra threads), and a restarted batch re-checks every unfinished folder. New status fields: batch `evidence_capabilities` and `warnings`; per folder `stage`, `beets_skip_reason`, `album_ids`, `missing_release_group_album_ids`, `auto_import`, `warnings`, `pinned_release_id`, and the `importing` status.
- The batch import can now scan the downloads folder itself ("import everything in Downloads"). Before, it refused that folder, although it was the default. The music library folder itself is still refused, because the batch moves files. The batch job is now labelled "Import: <folder>".
- Albums imported by the batch import and by `POST /api/import` now appear in the History tab. Before, only re-imports from disk did. `POST /api/import` records them only with a webmanager plugin that reports the imported album IDs (1.16.0 or later).
- Albums imported from the Review Queue (`POST /api/folders/import-with-id`) now appear in the History tab too, once per album.
- The batch import no longer skips folders it cannot read without saying so. The job log names each one, and the batch status has `folders_unreadable` and adds the count to its summary.
- The batch import now skips playlist download folders and the acquisition flow's missing-track folders that sit inside the folder being scanned, and logs each one. Those workflows import their own folders. Scanning one of those folders directly still works.
- The Review Queue no longer drops its newest rows when it holds more than one page, and its `total` and counts are no longer cut off at the page size. The whole list is sorted newest first and then paged with the new `offset` parameter (F5).
- The Review Queue no longer goes down with Beets, and no longer makes one Beets call per album on every load. It now lists only import decisions (Pending Review and skipped folders) and reads nothing from Beets (F6).
- A folder that is both in Pending Review and in the skipped list is listed once, as the Pending Review item. The skipped list reads only the last 8 MB of `beet.log` instead of the whole file (F18).
- Revalidating the whole Review Queue no longer runs on the request thread (each item makes MusicBrainz and AcoustID calls, so up to 2000 items could hold a server thread for many minutes). It is one tracked, cancellable job, and two cannot run at once. The ready-items auto-import job (`POST /api/import-review/auto-enqueue-ready/job`) is typed `import-review-auto-enqueue-ready` and also refuses a second concurrent start (F10).
- The Import Review search (`q`) again matches year, confidence, the blocked reason and next action, the folder-guessed artist, album and year, and the AcoustID fingerprint-mismatch detail, as the client-side search did before it moved to the server (#395).
- A malformed row in the import-skip ledger (`import_skipped.json`) is ignored, and a failure to update the ledger is logged instead of failing the import that triggered it.
- During revalidation, an item that raises fails on its own with "Could not revalidate this item." instead of stopping the whole run, and an unreadable stored `existing_album_ids` value no longer breaks the item.
- A revalidated item whose import could not be queued now reports "Could not queue this import." instead of the exception text; the detail goes to the server log (F17).
- `GET /api/import-log` reads only the last 8 MB of `beet.log` instead of the whole file; `total` counts the lines in that window. `limit=0` now returns no lines instead of the whole log.
- A folder that imports (or is otherwise resolved out of Pending Review) is dropped from the recorded Beets skip list straight away, so it no longer shows in the Skipped tab while files remain in it. This reads no Beets data (QA #391 N1).

- The Import summary no longer says "Ready" while the Find Music list is still building, was never built, failed without an earlier list, or when a summary count could not be loaded. It names the state instead (for example "Find Music list building" or "Status incomplete").
- Revalidate & Import on more than 25 Review Queue items now follows the background job the server starts. The page says it is running as a background job (or that one was already running and nothing new was started), keeps following it after a reload, and when it ends shows the real reviewed/queued/updated/failed counts and reloads the queue. Before, it reported "Revalidated 0; queued 0" for a job that had only started. Up to 25 items still revalidate at once, as before.
- The Missing MusicBrainz IDs page no longer re-reads the whole Beets library every 60 seconds while it sits open. It reloads only when you change the filter, search, page, or click Refresh, and after a change succeeds. The Review Queue's 60-second refresh is unchanged.
- "Retry failed imports" no longer says "Retry started" when nothing was started. It now says when an import for the batch was already running (and follows it), or when no failed folders were left to retry.
- A Find Music download (a row, or Download All) whose import put the album into the library now drops it from the Find Music list at once. An import that went to Pending Review, repaired an existing album in place, or found the album already complete leaves the row for the next build. The automatic refresh the page sends after such a download reuses that list instead of rebuilding it from the library and every Lidarr wanted page (F2, ARCH-028). `POST /api/acquisition/queue/refresh` with `{"after_download": true}` answers 200 with `reused: true` and `job_id: null` once within 120 s of the download; any other Refresh, including every one the user clicks (no `after_download`), starts or joins a rebuild (202, `reused: false`). A rebuild already running when the download finished leaves the album out too.
- When Lidarr's wanted list changes while it is being read (`totalRecords` moves between pages), it is read once more, so an album shifted across a page boundary is no longer missed (QA #385 N2).
- The folder identification check for symlinked audio stops after 20,000 entries under a symlinked folder, or when one of them cannot be read, and then treats the folder as holding symlinked audio (it goes to review) instead of walking without limit (#381).
- Building the Library page (`GET /api/library` with a cold cache, and the Find Music build's library read) lists each folder once instead of checking every file separately, and matches missing-file albums by artist instead of against every one. On a synthetic 10,000-album, 128,000-track library the build went from 27 s to 9 s with no change to the result (ARCH-031). The two full Beets reads it starts with are unchanged. Cover art named in another letter case (`Cover.JPG`) is still found on case-insensitive filesystems such as SMB shares, and a folder Web Manager cannot read no longer fails the whole build: its tracks show as missing.
- Accessibility: the Review Queue and Missing IDs pages announce the shown range and search results ("Showing 1 to 100 of 250", "No results for …") to screen readers, and the retry dialog's loading bar has a name.
- Find Music: **Retry N failed** now counts and retries every failed row of the last batch that is in the current filter, on any page, not only the rows on the page shown.

### Changed

- Library albums without a Release ID and singleton tracks without a Recording ID have their own endpoint, `GET /api/library/missing-mb-ids` (`kind` all/album/item, `search`, `offset`, `limit` up to 500 with 0 for counts only, `sort` added_desc/added_asc/artist/title). It reads the library once per call, pages the full sorted list, and returns 503 while Beets is down. Albums that a Pending Review item claims are now listed with `in_import_review: true` instead of being hidden. The rows keep the old Needs MB ID shape, so Import Review's decision and folder-delete actions accept them as before.
- Find Music no longer builds its list inside the request (it took about 6.5 minutes on an 11,700-album Lidarr wanted list). A background job (`acquisition-queue-build`, shown on the Jobs page) builds the list from the library and every Lidarr wanted page, and `GET /api/acquisition/queue` serves the last list from that snapshot with `offset`/`limit` paging, `search`, `source`, `album_type` and `sort` filters, and a `cache` block (state, age, last error). A list older than 6 hours is served at once while it is rebuilt in the background. `POST /api/acquisition/queue/refresh` starts a rebuild, or returns the one already running. If the library or Lidarr fails, the previous list is kept and the error and age are reported; a list missing one side is never shown as complete. The snapshot is saved to `WEB_MANAGER_DATA_DIR/acquisition_queue_cache.json`, so a restart serves the last list straight away. The "Show unmonitored" choice now reaches the Lidarr fetch (`include_unmonitored`).
- The Find Music tab now uses that cached list. It loads 100 rows a page, with Previous/Next page buttons, and search, source (All, Missing, Wanted, In both, Unmonitored), album type and sort filters that run on the server. It shows how old the list is ("List updated 12 min ago"). It also says when the list is still being built, when it is stale and updating in the background, and when the last update failed: it gives the error and when it happened, keeps the previous list and labels it as out of date. An empty list counts as "nothing to download" only when the list is actually built. **Refresh list** starts the background rebuild (`POST /api/acquisition/queue/refresh`; the page no longer sends `GET ?refresh=1`). The page then polls until the rebuild finishes and reloads. If Download All is refused because the list is still building, the page says so. "Download next N" works on the rows of the current page, and both download confirmations are unchanged.
- The Find Music fallback label now shows the configured chain the server reports (for example "Fallback: YouTube -> SoundCloud", with unavailable sources marked), not a hardcoded "SpotiFLAC -> YouTube -> SoundCloud". When slskd is not configured, the page says so. Find Music and the Discography panel no longer send `fallback_method: 'spotiflac'`.
- Switching Import tabs now adds a browser history entry, so Back returns to the previous tab. Filter changes still replace the current entry.
- The History tab hides the "AI-matched imports" section whenever no AI provider is configured, including past AI history, and does not request that history while AI is off.
- Library albums and tracks missing MusicBrainz IDs now have their own page, **Library > Missing MusicBrainz IDs** (`/library/missing-ids`), with All / Albums / Tracks filters, server-side search, and 100-row pages. Rows that a folder in the Review Queue already targets show an "In import review" badge. The Library's "Identify in Import" album action is now "Fix missing IDs" and opens this page; the Import summary's "need IDs" chip and old `/import?tab=review&filter=library_no_mb` links go there too. The Review Queue no longer has a "Needs MusicBrainz ID" filter.
- The Review Queue now pages 100 items at a time with Previous/Next page buttons, and sends its type filter (pending AI / skipped), source filter, search and "Evidence only" to the server, so totals and counts cover the whole queue. Search now matches the fields the server searches (title, artist, album, path, folder, reason, status, source and IDs). The state filters (ready, blocked, audio mismatch, ...) still apply to the loaded page, and the page says so.
- Removing rejected files from a review folder now always quarantines them (they can be restored) and never deletes them, matching the server change in #392. The confirmation says so, and the result message gives the quarantined, deleted, skipped and remaining counts, and says when the server quarantined files it was asked to delete.
- **Retry failed imports** on the Import Source tab now retries the failed folders of the last batch import from that tab (`POST /api/ai-batch-import/recover` with `retry_failed`), not the old Import All run. A confirmation lists the folders it will retry; folders at their retry limit are left out. The retry job then shows in the batch panel. **Retry eligible failures** in the batch panel uses the same confirmation.
- The batch import panel shows the new folder states and stages (importing, review created, Beets import / identify / pinned import), why Beets skipped a folder, why a folder was not auto-imported, and per-folder warnings. When Beets fingerprinting or the AcoustID key is unavailable, it warns that albums are imported without fingerprint proof and how many were. Folders sent to review have an **Open in review** button that opens the Review Queue searched to that folder.
- Download All now picks its rows from that snapshot instead of rebuilding the whole list in its POST request. Without a snapshot it returns 409 `queue_not_ready` with the build job's id. Its `?refresh=1` query argument is ignored.
- The direct-source availability checks reported with the Find Music list (`download_sources`, which run `deno --version` and similar) are cached for 5 minutes.
- The Web Manager image now ships Deno 2.9.7 (`/usr/local/bin/deno`, from the digest-pinned `denoland/deno:bin-2.9.7` image), so yt-dlp can solve YouTube's signature challenges without any extra setup. The solver scripts come from the bundled `yt-dlp-ejs` package; nothing is downloaded at runtime (`YTDLP_REMOTE_COMPONENTS` stays empty). Deno's cache is `DENO_DIR=/tmp/deno`, which works with the hardened Compose settings (`read_only`, `/tmp` tmpfs). The image is about 96 MB larger uncompressed (about 42 MB compressed). CI now checks that the app's yt-dlp runtime probe finds Deno and that Deno runs JavaScript as the app user under those settings.
- `YTDLP_REMOTE_COMPONENTS` (lets yt-dlp download and run remote solver code) is now classified operator-only: set it only in the Compose environment; the Settings page cannot save it. The unused `[blocked] Deno auto-install` stubs were removed.
- Deterministic folder identification now gives the identity check each file's AcoustID result, so a fingerprint can prove a track in a folder that has no MusicBrainz tags (I-8). This is opt-in (`fingerprint_evidence=True`); every other preflight caller, including the AI batch gate, behaves as before.

### Added

- Deterministic identification for import folders that Beets skipped (`backend.import_identity.identify_import_folder`). It builds candidates from embedded IDs, AcoustID and MusicBrainz, and ranks them by Release Group without AI. It also decides whether the folder may be re-imported automatically: that needs every file proven by a fingerprint or an embedded Recording ID, exactly one proven Release Group, no conflicts, and a skip reason other than duplicate. Another Release Group blocks it only when it covers at least half of the folder's files, so an album track that is also on a single does not. Every embedded Release ID must be checked, and a symlinked audio file sends the folder to review. Routes and jobs that use it follow in a later change.
- A rule that lists library albums and tracks missing MusicBrainz identity (`backend.matching.library_identity_gaps`). It keys albums on the Release Group, so an album with a Release ID but no Release Group now counts as missing. It also covers album tracks without a Recording ID (I-7). The review queue does not use it yet.
- Web UI: Library > Missing IDs can fix up to 50 selected rows in one batch. "Preview fix" proves each row and writes nothing. It lists proven rows with the exact IDs to be written, and rows that need review with the reason. Apply appears only when something was proven, and runs only after a confirmation that says how many albums and tracks will be written. The page follows the preview and apply jobs, with progress, a Cancel button, and the outcome of each row. A Recovery Required result links to its transaction in Library Changes, which now opens a transaction from `?tx=<id>`. The page keeps following a running job after a reload. If a restart interrupted the preview, Apply stays hidden and the page asks you to preview again. Rows blocked by a release group that another album already has name that album and point to the duplicate checks. Needs the batch endpoints from #399.
- Web UI: Find Music sends `after_download` only on the automatic refresh after a download finishes, never from the Refresh button. When the server answers `reused: true`, the page reloads the list and does not wait for a job.
- Web UI: the batch import names the new folder outcomes. "Contains a linked file or folder" means the import was refused because the folder holds a symlink or junction (even one to an empty folder) or its path runs through one. "Folder could not be checked" means the folder is outside the configured import folders, part of it could not be read, or it holds no readable audio. "Imported in part" means some albums were kept and the skipped paths went to review. Each folder with a review item has an "Open review item" link to its Review Queue row, and that row lists the links, unreadable folders or skipped paths the server named (the first 5, then "and N more"), as plain text. Server codes without a label read as words instead of snake_case.
- Batch fix for library rows missing MusicBrainz IDs, proven matches only. `POST /api/library/missing-mb-ids/batch/preview` (`{"ids": ["album:12", "item:34"]}` or `{"kind", "search", "limit"}`, at most 50 rows; a malformed id is refused with 400) starts a read-only job that proves each row and records a Preview transaction with the exact IDs it would write and returns a one-time `confirm_token` (only its SHA-256 is stored with the transaction). An album is proven only when the folder identification gate says the folder may be re-imported automatically, the folder holds exactly the album's own files (same paths, not just the same number), no track is stored through a symbolic link below the music root, the album has no other Release Group already, no other album (and no other row of the batch) holds the same Release Group (that is a duplicate for the dedup review instead), and the chosen release has the same track count. A singleton track is proven only when AcoustID confirms exactly one recording and that recording is the one trusted candidate that may be attached without review. Everything else is "needs review" and is never written. AI is never consulted. `POST /api/library/missing-mb-ids/batch/apply` (`{"transaction_id", "confirm_token"}`) starts a job that records a snapshot of every target's IDs and the library counts, proves each row again, skips a row whose evidence changed, and writes the rest one by one through the existing Match album and Attach recording ID writers, each with its own rollback-capable transaction. The first failed write stops the batch; cancelling stops after the current row.

## v0.6.0 - 2026-10-13

This is a safety release. Imports and deletes now happen only after an explicit click and confirmation, and a downloads folder is deleted only with proof that its audio is already in the library.

### Upgrade Notes

- After an import, a downloads folder is no longer deleted unless every audio file in it is proven (byte or AcoustID match) to be in the library album. Folders without that proof now stay in downloads, and the import log says why. Clean them up from Import Review.
- Scripts that call `DELETE /api/ai-pending-review` with no body to clear the whole Pending Review queue must now send `{"all": true}`. A request with no `path` and no `all` is refused.
- `POST /api/import/review-folder/delete` ignores `confirmed_wrong_library_folder` and `allow_library_delete`. The folder must be in Pending Review or be a Needs MB ID album's own folder.
- Paths sent to the Import Review delete and file-cleanup endpoints are used exactly as sent and are no longer URL-decoded.

### Security

- A downloads source folder is deleted as "already in library" only with proof: every audio file in it must be a byte match or an AcoustID same-recording match for an item of the library album, and that album's Release Group must be the target one. Only library items under `MUSIC_ROOT` and outside the source folder count as proof, so an album imported in place cannot prove itself. Any file that is neither proven audio nor a known sidecar (artwork, `.cue`, `.log`, `.txt`, `.nfo`, playlists, checksums, `.pdf`) keeps the folder, and so does any change to the folder between the proof and the delete. Matching titles or file names are no longer enough; without proof the folder is kept and the log says why. The import paths no longer pass the "already in library" phrase themselves, and the dead `_delete_if_already_in_library(path, "")` call after `/api/import` is gone (I-4).
- `POST /api/import/review-folder/delete` no longer lets the client's `confirmed_wrong_library_folder` / `allow_library_delete` flag skip the Pending Review check. Every delete must be a Pending Review folder or a matching Needs MB ID album row; the server decides from `MUSIC_ROOT` whether the folder is in the library (library files are quarantined, as before), and refuses a path whose symlinked component moves it into or out of the library. The flags are still accepted and ignored. `GET /api/import/folder-stats` now returns `is_library_path` (REV-04).
- `DELETE /api/ai-pending-review` with no `path` no longer clears the whole Pending Review queue: send a `path`, or `{"all": true}` to clear everything. The queue file is written atomically (F15).
- Import Review folder delete and file cleanup now act on exactly the path that was checked against Pending Review. Before, the path was URL-decoded first, so deleting a review folder named `Mix%20Tape` could permanently delete a different folder, `Mix Tape`, that was never in review. File names in a cleanup request are no longer decoded either: `a%20b.flac` means that literal file (N1).
- A Needs MB ID album row now opens a review-folder delete only for that album's own folder, and only when every audio file in it belongs to that album. Before, sending the album's id with its artist folder quarantined the artist's other albums too (N2).

### Fixed

- Import Review: opening or browsing a review item no longer changes any files or starts an import. Before, showing an item could quarantine or permanently delete up to 4 of its rejected files, and could queue an import for a high-confidence match, without a click. Now **Remove N rejected files…** opens a confirmation that lists the files and says whether they will be quarantined or permanently deleted, and imports start only from the Import button and its confirmation. Backend auto-import jobs are unchanged.
- Import page: every button that starts imports or changes stored data now asks first. **Revalidate** is renamed **Revalidate & Import…** and its confirmation gives the number of items and says eligible items may be imported. **Retry failed imports**, **Retry eligible failures** and **Recover batch** confirm that imports will start. **Clean Stale…** and History's **Clear** confirm that only review or history metadata changes. Cancel sends nothing.
- Find Music: a per-album download now asks first, because the finished download is imported into the library. The **Download next N** confirmation now says the downloads are imported. Import Review's reconciliation **Keep existing** and **Keep imported** now ask first, because they quarantine or replace a library file; **Keep both** changes nothing and does not ask.
- Import Review keyboard shortcuts (`[`, `]`, `i`) no longer fire while another Import tab is open or while Ctrl, Alt or Meta is held. Shift and AltGr are allowed, so the shortcuts work on layouts that need them to type `[` or `]` (for example German). The candidate Left/Right arrow keys now work only while focus is inside the candidate carousel, not anywhere on the page.

## v0.5.0 - 2026-10-12

### Upgrade Notes

- Legacy playlist import no longer reads a `MUSIC_ROOT/playlists` folder that is a symlink, a file larger than 5 MB, or more than 500 files in one folder. If your `playlists` folder is a symlink, replace it with a real folder (or move the files into Web Manager) before upgrading. Web Manager's own playlist folder is not affected.
- After the upgrade, the first startup moves transactions that were left Running by an earlier crash or restart, and that no recovery step covers, to Recovery Required. They then show up in Library Changes for you to review; nothing in the library is changed.
- Cancelling a job that is waiting on a child job (imports, downloads, Clean All artwork/genre steps) can now take up to 5 minutes, so the job reports the child's real outcome instead of abandoning it.

### Security

- Outbound-request log lines and provider HTTP errors no longer include the URL path, which can carry a webhook token or URL signature. They show the scheme, host, port and a short hash of the path (#350).
- A URL password that contains a raw `/` (`http://user:pa/ss@host`) is now hidden in the config editor, the plugin preview diff, outbound-request logs and stored job records. Before, only the part before the `/` was hidden (#239 F-3).
- Stored job logs and results now also hide a token-only URL login (`https://TOKEN@host`) and the whole of a password that contains `@` (#351 N2). Result fields named `fingerprint` or `client` are no longer blanked when a job is stored; a `client=` query parameter is still hidden.
- The request fingerprint stored with each job is now an HMAC keyed with this install's secret key (`BEETS_WEB_SECRET_KEY` or `.flask_secret_key` in the data folder), not a plain SHA-256. The job files alone, without that key, can no longer be used to check guesses of a secret sent in a request. An uploaded file is hashed before the job store is locked, so a large upload no longer delays other job starts (#351 N1).
- Two settings saves at the same moment no longer fail with a 500. Each save writes its own uniquely named, 0600 temporary file instead of a shared `.env.tmp` (#183).
- `GET/POST /api/setup/env` returns the settings file name (`.env`), not its path inside the container (#183).
- The Discogs token is read from Beets `config.yaml` only when `BEETS_CONFIG` is inside the Beets config folder (`BEETSDIR`), the same rule as every other reader of that file (#183, #239).
- A `config.yaml` that Web Manager creates on a fresh install is now mode 0600 instead of 0644, because it holds Beets' secrets once they are set. Beets runs as the same `PUID`, so it can still read the file. Only the newest 10 timestamped config editor backups (`config.yaml.bak.<time>`) are kept (#324 N-2). Saving a CRLF `config.yaml` in the editor keeps its CRLF line endings (#324 N-3).

### Fixed

- TrueNAS rollout script: the online library identity digest now hashes item paths relative to the library directory Beets reports in `/webmanager/status`. Before, an engine restart that moved from plugin 1.15.0 to 1.16.0, which serves Beets 2.14 item paths as absolute paths, failed with "library identity digest changed across the engine restart" even though the library had not changed. A real path change still changes the digest. This applies to the pre/post-deploy, engine-restart and offline stop/start comparisons.
- Beets adapter (#351 N5): a relative item or art path from Beets that contains a `..` segment is no longer collapsed into a path under the library directory. Beets only stores paths inside its library directory as relative, so such a row is treated as corrupt and its `path`/`artpath` becomes empty. An absolute path with a `..` segment is treated the same way. A `library_directory` that is not absolute is ignored instead of being joined to relative paths. Normal Beets 2.14 relative paths still become absolute.
- Beets adapter (#351 N6): stock Beets turns every `\` in a query term into `/`, and no URL encoding survives that, so a file name that contains a backslash cannot be queried exactly. Looking up a library item by such a path no longer accepts the item Beets matched when `web.include_paths` is off, because that item can be a different file. With `include_paths` on, the exact path comparison already rejected it. Other queries that contain a backslash still match `/`; this is a stock Beets limit.
- Playlist sync (#363): a track a playlist deliberately repeats is no longer re-sent to Plex on every sync. Plex keeps one entry per track and drops a repeat it already holds, so the sync logged "+1 added" and counted a Plex write that changed nothing. A repeat is now satisfied by the one Plex entry, an unchanged playlist makes 0 Plex writes, and the "added" count (log and `tracks_added`) is what Plex holds after the write. The repeat stays in the Web Manager list and its `.m3u`.
- Legacy playlist import (#351 N3): each `MUSIC_ROOT/playlists` file is opened first (no symlink follow, non-blocking) and then checked on the open file (regular file, at most 5 MB), so a file swapped for a symlink or FIFO after listing is not read. A symlinked `playlists` folder is skipped, and at most 500 files per folder are imported. The folder is opened once without following symlinks, and both its listing and each file open go through that handle, so swapping the folder for a symlink after the check does not redirect the import (#364 F1).
- Playlist suggestions (#356): `GET /api/playlists/<name>/suggestions` and `POST /api/playlists/<name>/apply-safe-suggestions` return a response-level `acoustid_status` (`not_attempted`: suggestions for missing tracks have no audio to fingerprint) beside `musicbrainz_unavailable`.
- `GET /api/items/<id>/mb-candidates` now reports a MusicBrainz outage as `200` with `ok: true`, `candidates: []` and `musicbrainz_unavailable: true`, like the other candidate endpoints, so the UI can show the outage notice. Before, it returned a `503` (#356). A successful search sets `musicbrainz_unavailable: false`. The album route is unchanged.
- The MusicBrainz track sync no longer sends an `acoustid_verify` flag that nothing read. The unused `backend/track_align.py` was removed: since #249 nothing called its AcoustID promotion, and `mbsync` never assigns Recording IDs from alignment (#261 N-7). #261 F-5 and the "explicit"/"operator-selected" summary were fixed in v0.4.0; their technical-debt entries are closed.
- Jobs that wait on a child job (imports, downloads, Clean All's artwork and genre steps, replacements): a cancel or timeout no longer abandons a child that may be in the middle of a Beets operation. The parent asks the child to stop and waits up to 5 minutes for its real outcome (#317). The parent's transaction is Cancelled only when the child stopped before reporting a library change; Failed (partially applied) when the child finished or reported a change first; Recovery Required when the child was still running. A timed-out child that then finishes is used as a success. Read-only children (scans) are still stopped at once.
- A Running transaction of a mutation family this version does not know (a removed family, or one cut off before it recorded evidence) is moved to Recovery Required by the startup sweep instead of staying Running for good (#266).
- The album move rollback log names the folders the tracks are back in as absolute paths, also for moves planned before 0.4.0 (#328 F9).
- Staging moves re-check the source's identity right before the directory rename and before the file's old name is unlinked, so a source swapped in between is refused, not moved or deleted (#351 N4).
- Jobs whose label contains "Artist" (or "repair", "maintenance") are no longer recorded as Artwork Update or AI Suggestion transactions (#325 F5).
- The Music Format Preferences rejection message no longer says files were "handled according to settings" when they were left in place, and names why they were kept (#325 F3).
- Troubleshooting §12 no longer says a failed tag write (`WRITE_FAILED`) cannot be rolled back; since v0.4.0 the Failed item or album edit can be rolled back once the files are writable (#318).

### Added

- `GET /api/transactions/<id>` returns `cancel: {allowed, mode, code, reason}`, from the same rule as `POST /api/transactions/<id>/cancel` (#356).
- Library Changes uses the server's `cancel` field: Cancel is enabled only when the server allows it ("Cancel Sync" for a library sync Beets is running), and the server's reason is shown when it is not. The web UI no longer guesses eligibility from the status and mutation family (#356).
- Playlists: the playlist sync status links its last sync job, and `/jobs?job=<id>` opens that job on the Jobs page. Playlist suggestions pass `acoustid_status` to the outage notice; `not_attempted` shows no AcoustID warning (#356).

### Tests

- The stale opt-in Docker smoke test (`RUN_DOCKER_SMOKE`), which built the Web Manager image and ran Beets in it, now runs against stock `lscr.io/linuxserver/beets`: it loads every plugin in `config.yaml.example` with the bundled plugins in `/config/beetsplug`, imports a generated track and runs fetchart. It runs wherever Docker is available, including CI, so it is no longer always skipped (#355).
- CI's `unit-tests` job runs the root-only PUID/PGID ownership tests as root, the `setup.ps1` (pwsh) test and the stock-Beets smoke test, and fails if any of them skips (#355).
- The slskd containment tests patch only `backend.slskd_service`'s `time`, not the process-wide `time.time`/`time.sleep`, so threads left by other tests cannot consume their fake clock (#266).
- No backend test leaves `job_engine` threads running after it ends (#234 item 2). Before, 22 tests left job threads or a `job-heartbeat` thread running into later tests. These threads are the suspected cause of the intermittent exit-139 crashes in CI. The tests now close their `JobStore` or join the shared store's jobs, and `tests/test_job_thread_guard.py` fails any test whose job threads are still alive 10 seconds after it ends. The suite runs the guard on every test. Production code is unchanged.

## v0.4.0 - 2026-10-12

### Upgrade Notes

- Restart the Beets container so it loads webmanager plugin 1.16.0. The rollout script does this itself when the plugin version changes. Until Beets restarts, item audio/file downloads stay broken on Beets 2.14.
- Playlists: the Web Manager list is now the master copy. Sync updates Plex playlists in place, so each one keeps its ratingKey. A track is removed from Plex or the `.m3u` only after you remove it in Web Manager; tracks added in Plex or the `.m3u` are merged in. Playlists that exist only in Plex are no longer adopted.
- On first load, playlists in `<MUSIC_ROOT>/playlists` (`.m3u` and `.playlist.json`) and hand-placed `.m3u` files in the Web Manager playlist folder are imported once, read-only, when no Web Manager list has that name. Web Manager never writes to `<MUSIC_ROOT>/playlists`.
- `PLAYLIST_AUTO_SYNC` now runs as a tracked job, one sync at a time. `PLEX_INDEX_TIMEOUT` is now the total time for the whole Plex index fetch. Every other Plex call now treats its time limit (usually `PLEX_API_TIMEOUT`) as a total for reading the response, not a limit per network read, so a very slow Plex can fail a call it used to finish slowly. Raise the timeout if that happens.
- "Import as-is" no longer imports folders Beets cannot match. They stay in place and go to the Review Queue (`asis_refused`). Albums with a blank artist or album are refused.
- Without `ACOUSTID_API_KEY`, a missing-track fill is no longer merged on text or duration evidence; it waits in Review.
- Out-of-range transaction settings return 400 instead of being clamped. The cover upload floor is 1000 bytes. The quarantine root must be inside the configured root.
- `SPOTIFLAC_SERVICES` accepts only known service names or `ext:<id>`; other values are refused on save and before every run.
- A Beets config path outside `BEETSDIR` is never read or verified.
- Config editor revisions are keyed per process, so an edit opened before a restart gets a revision conflict when saved after it. Reload the editor and save again.
- `BEETS_WEB_MANAGER_VERSION` is no longer used to report the running version; the image's `/app/BUILD_INFO.json` is. With a `:latest` or pinned `image:` the rollout script no longer writes the variable and warns if a stale one is set. A compose `image:` that interpolates the variable is deprecated.
- Fingerprint results cached as "no match" by earlier versions are ignored and re-checked against AcoustID the next time each file is looked up. The old cache files under `/config/.cache/metadata/acoustid` are left in place; delete them to reclaim space.

### Fixed

- AcoustID lookups run again in the Web Manager image (QA 0.4.0 B2). Its fpcalc 1.5.1 exits with an error on every file while still printing a valid fingerprint, and Web Manager discarded that fingerprint and cached the file as "no match". Fingerprints are now judged by fpcalc's output, not its exit code. A file that really cannot be fingerprinted is reported as not checked and is never cached as "no match".
- Item audio streaming and download (`/api/items/<id>/audio`, `/file`) no longer fail with 500 on Beets 2.14, and artwork placement after import finds the album folder again (#302). The plugin binds the library directory for every Beets web request, and the adapter turns relative item and cover paths into absolute ones.
- Path lookups against stock Beets work (`/` is sent as the query path separator). The stale-row cleanup before a re-import removes only rows whose files are provably missing.
- Clean All can no longer run twice at once; racing starts return the running job (D1). Cancelled runs are recorded as cancelled (D15). Imports of the same source folder are deduplicated (D2).
- After a restart interrupts Clean All, resuming it works at once instead of failing with "already running" for about two minutes. The restart releases only the interrupted job's workflow lock; a lock held by a live process still blocks. A start refused because Clean All runs in another process returns 409 `workflow_busy` and no longer records a Failed job and transaction.
- Every transaction records who started it. Composite workflows such as removing an album cover, and jobs an operator started, record the signed-in user; scheduled and unattended actions record `system` instead of `operator`.
- Job starts are protected against retried requests: a request fingerprint and an optional `Idempotency-Key` header return 409 `job_already_running` / `job_already_started` instead of starting a second job. Jobs interrupted by a restart become Recovery Required. Job logs and results are redacted before they are stored (#272, #229, #266).
- Transaction logs are appended, not replaced, so recovery and audit history is kept. Interrupted cleanups become Recovery Required after a restart instead of staying Running. One-shot mutations that could not start are Cancelled with "nothing was changed". Partially Rolled Back operations can be retried (#298, #328, #317, #318, #308, #245).
- Staging moves never overwrite an existing target; cross-filesystem moves fsync and verify the copy before removing the source; cleanup refuses to cross mounts (#230).
- The torrent-source protection applies to every modify, and album artpath is validated (#335, #327, #336). Clean All's Release Group merge no longer loops on blocked empty folders.
- Cover delete is truthful and reversible (`/album-art/remove`).
- Identity: a missing-track merge needs AcoustID proof and fails closed to Review; a mixed AcoustID top tier goes to Review; the resolver never sets identity from text alone; Release Group input is checked against its Releases and fails closed (#244, #261, #264, #303).
- Provider outages are reported as "not checked", never "no match": AcoustID during matching, ai-suggest and playlist placement, and MusicBrainz during ai-suggest (#252, #297, D11).
- Playlists: a sync with no changes makes no Plex writes; duplicates and order are kept; unmatched tracks show as pending with a reason; a sync whose Plex step fails is reported as failed; creating a playlist with no matched tracks returns `no_tracks` instead of 502; albums imported outside Web Manager are seen within about 15 seconds; hand-placed `.m3u` files are listed.
- Plex: the index fetch respects `PLEX_INDEX_TIMEOUT` as a total (#339), even when Plex sends the response slowly a few bytes at a time. A Plex time-out is reported as a time-out, not as a path-mapping problem. A URL blocked by the outbound policy is reported as such. The per-track title searches that follow share one `PLEX_API_TIMEOUT` total; tracks not searched in time stay pending with that reason. A track removed in Web Manager now leaves the Plex playlist even when Plex shows a different title for the same file, and a Plex-side track that only reads alike is kept. Imported `.m3u` entries take the library's artist and title and show correct counts.
- slskd: a cancelled playlist track cancels and cleans its transfer (#280); partial-file cleanup is contained (#281); download-folder scans are bounded (#263); a 401 names `SLSKD_API_KEY`.
- Lidarr's wanted list says why it failed. A refused qBittorrent login shows in provider health and no longer echoes qBittorrent's response (#252).
- Setup no longer labels a saved key as "Docker environment overrides saved key" (D12). Duplicate-resolver and MusicBrainz-completeness errors return fixed reasons instead of exception text.
- `/health/live` and `/api/setup/status` report the running `version` and `revision`.

### Security

- `SPOTIFLAC_SERVICES` can no longer inject command-line flags (#340). SpotiFLAC output is confined to the download folder and symlinks a run creates there are removed (#274).
- The config editor redacts secrets in every YAML form and refuses to show the file if it cannot (`config_redaction_failed`). Revisions are HMAC-keyed. Saves, reverts and plugin edits are serialized (#324).
- Config editor backups are private (0600) and never follow symlinks (#183). Plugin provisioning refuses a symlinked `beetsplug` folder and plugin preview diffs mask secrets (#239).
- Rollout script: Ctrl-C seals the backup and restores state, a backup directory is never reused, the token is read in isolated Python, and the running version and revision are verified after deploy (#253).
- 0.4.0 security review: the transaction-settings and config-redaction errors return fixed text instead of exception text, playlist M3U and state-file paths pass a normalized containment check, and a line break in a track's tag or file name can no longer add an entry to an exported M3U.

### Changed

- Dependencies: eslint 10.12.0, jsdom 30.1.2, react-router 8.4.0, @tanstack/react-query 5.104.1, postcss 8.5.29.
- Web UI follows the 0.4.0 API: job starts send an `Idempotency-Key` and follow an already-running job instead of erroring; interrupted jobs show "Recovery required"; imports point as-is refusals to the Review Queue; Playlists gets Sync All to Plex with failed, busy and pending (with reasons) results; AcoustID/MusicBrainz outages show "not checked", never "no match"; Library Changes offers Retry Rollback, shows restored/adopted counts, cancels orphaned library syncs and reports rejected settings; System shows the build revision; Torrent Repair shows qBittorrent login failures.
- CI pulls stock Beets authenticated from GHCR with retries (#234), runs Python tests with faulthandler, and runs the frontend Vitest suite. The ARCH-002 caller audit no longer needs git; `Dockerfile.test` is the supported full-suite environment.
## v0.3.0 - 2026-10-11

### Upgrade Notes
- **Some settings are now operator-only and a saved value for them is dropped.** `BEETS_OUTBOUND_ALLOWLIST`, `BEETS_WEB_AUTH_DISABLED`, `BEETS_TRUSTED_PROXIES`, `BEETS_WEB_SESSION_COOKIE_SECURE`, `BEETS_AUTH_RATE_LIMIT`, `BEETS_AUTH_RATE_WINDOW`, `BEETS_AUTH_ACCOUNT_RATE_LIMIT`, `BEETS_AUTH_ACCOUNT_RATE_WINDOW`, `SPOTIFLAC_CMD`, `YTDLP_JS_RUNTIMES`, `YTDLP_COOKIE_FILE`, `YTDLP_NETRC_FILE`, `YTDLP_ALLOW_BROWSER_COOKIES`, `SLSKD_API_KEY_FILE`, `IMPORT_REVIEW_QUARANTINE_DIR` and `MUSIC_FORMAT_QUARANTINE_DIR` can no longer be saved from the System page. On first start the settings migration removes any of them from `/web-manager-data/.env` (backup `.env.bak-migration-<timestamp>`; only key names are logged). If you had set one of them on the System page, set it again in the Web Manager service's `environment:` block (or the Compose `.env` where the Compose file forwards it) and recreate the container. In the shipped Compose files a web-saved `BEETS_OUTBOUND_ALLOWLIST` never took effect anyway, because Compose always sets it.
- You no longer need allowlist entries for Plex, Lidarr, slskd, qBittorrent, the yt-dlp PO provider or a LAN AI endpoint when their URL is set to a private LAN, CGNAT or IPv6 ULA address; existing entries keep working. An entry whose host now resolves to cloud-metadata, link-local, unspecified, multicast or reserved space, and a catch-all CIDR such as `0.0.0.0/0`, no longer admit anything.
- **`HTTP_PROXY`/`HTTPS_PROXY` are no longer used for any outbound request.** Every provider call -- public APIs such as MusicBrainz, AcoustID, Discogs, ListenBrainz and a hosted AI endpoint included, not only LAN services -- now connects directly to the address the policy checked. A deployment that reaches the internet only through an HTTP proxy loses those providers after upgrading. There is no setting to turn the proxy back on: give the Web Manager container direct outbound access to the providers it uses (a route or NAT rule on its Docker network), or use a transparent, network-level proxy that needs no `HTTP_PROXY` variable.

### Security
- **Outbound requests to operator-configured services are pinned to the checked address.** The operator path used to check a host and then let urllib resolve it again to connect, so a DNS answer that changed in between (DNS rebinding) could reach an address that was never checked. Each hop is now resolved once, every answer checked, and the socket connected to a checked address (TLS still verifies the certificate for the hostname); each redirect hop is checked and pinned again.
- **Allowlist entries can no longer admit metadata or link-local addresses.** Host, IP and CIDR entries skipped the address checks, and `169.254.169.254` could be allowlisted. Every entry now requires every resolved address to be private, CGNAT, IPv6 ULA or loopback; cloud-metadata addresses (also IPv4-mapped and `fd00:ec2::254`, `100.100.100.200`) are refused on every path, and CIDR entries wider than those ranges are ignored with a startup log line.
- **`BEETS_OUTBOUND_ALLOWLIST`, `SPOTIFLAC_CMD` and the other security, command and file-path settings are operator-only** (see Upgrade Notes): a web session can no longer edit the allowlist or any operator-only key, so it cannot add allowlist entries or set a command line that is executed. It can still point the web-editable service URLs (`PLEX_URL`, `LIDARR_URL`, `SLSKD_URL`, `QBITTORRENT_URL`, `YTDLP_PO_PROVIDER_URL`, `AI_BASE_URL`) at a private-LAN, CGNAT or ULA host, which that service's calls then reach; loopback and cloud-metadata addresses stay refused.
- **A setup connectivity test never gives a typed-in URL the configured service's trust.** The public first-run Plex/AI tests send a caller-supplied URL without the implicit trust of `PLEX_URL`/`AI_BASE_URL`.

### Changed
- **Each operator-hosted service trusts its own configured URL.** Plex (`PLEX_URL` or the Beets `config.yaml` `plex:` stanza), Lidarr, slskd, qBittorrent, the yt-dlp PO provider and the AI provider (`AI_BASE_URL`) may reach exactly the scheme, host and port of their own configured URL, for their own calls only, when it resolves to a private LAN, CGNAT or IPv6 ULA address. A Docker service name such as the default `SLSKD_URL=http://slskd:5030` works without an allowlist entry. Loopback and metadata addresses are never trusted this way. The trust applies only to a call that starts on that origin, every redirect hop of it must stay there, and a redirect from elsewhere into the origin is refused.
- **"Blocked by outbound URL policy" now says what to add.** The error and the provider status name the exact entry, for example `add 192.168.1.20:32400 to BEETS_OUTBOUND_ALLOWLIST or set PLEX_URL to it` (host and port only; no credentials, path or query). `provider_boundary.allowlist_hint(url, provider)` returns the same sentence for UI messages.
- **The Settings API refuses to save a key the Docker/Compose environment set at startup.** Such a save applied until the next restart and was then silently reverted by the Compose value.
- The operator path no longer uses `HTTP_PROXY`/`HTTPS_PROXY` (see Upgrade Notes): a proxy would connect to an address the policy never checked.
- Saving `PLEX_URL` also refuses a value that cannot be parsed (for example look-alike userinfo characters), any `;` and an out-of-range port, with the same fixed message (the value is never echoed). IPv4-compatible IPv6 addresses (`::a.b.c.d`) are treated as non-public.
- The System page reports `PLEX_URL` and `PLEX_TOKEN` as applying live (they always did; they were labelled restart-required).
- The System page describes `BEETS_OUTBOUND_ALLOWLIST` correctly (exact `host:port` entries for non-public services) and shows it read-only.

### Fixed
- **Plex, Lidarr, slskd, qBittorrent and AI errors now say when the outbound policy blocked them.** Before, a policy refusal read "Could not reach Plex." or "Lidarr returned an unexpected response", which looks like a network fault. The Plex status, the Plex and AI connection tests, Lidarr and slskd errors, the qBittorrent hardlink dry run and the provider health row (`last_reason`) now return `reason: "outbound_not_allowlisted"`, a fixed message and the `host:port` hint; the System page badge shows "Blocked by outbound policy". Other Plex failures get fixed messages with reasons `unreachable`, `timeout`, `auth_failed`, `http_error` or `plex_error`.
- **`/api/plex/status` and the qBittorrent status no longer echo URL credentials.** User name, password, query (such as `?X-Plex-Token=`) and fragment are removed. Saving a `PLEX_URL` with any of them, or without `http(s)://`, is refused.
- **The Plex connection test and the "configured" flag use the same settings as the Plex workflows** (`PLEX_URL`/`PLEX_TOKEN`, then the Beets `config.yaml` `plex:` stanza). Playlist sync to Plex now needs both a URL and a token, and stores a fixed message and reason instead of the raw exception text.
- The System page's `BEETS_OUTBOUND_MAX_RESPONSE_BYTES` default now shows the real 20 MiB.
- **TrueNAS rollout script: Web Manager data folder.** `scripts/deploy_truenas_web_manager.sh` now uses the mount at the container's own `WEB_MANAGER_DATA_DIR`. Before, any mount at `/data` won, so a stray anonymous `/data` volume made the script back up an empty folder and then fail after the recreate. If `WEB_MANAGER_DATA_DIR` is set and nothing is mounted at exactly that path, the dry run and the deploy stop with `webmgr_data_dir_unmounted`. If the data folder holds no auth token while the app requires authentication, they stop with `webmgr_data_source_without_token` before anything changes. Each backup now records the folder its Web Manager state came from, and `--rollback` stops with `backup_data_source_mismatch` instead of restoring into another folder, which would move that folder's live state aside. Script-only; no new image.

## v0.2.0 - 2026-10-09

### Upgrade Notes
- **Restart the `beets` container once after upgrading so it loads `webmanager` plugin 1.15.0, and keep `mbsync` and `musicbrainz` in `plugins:`.** v0.1.49 shipped plugin 1.5.0. Until the restart, the features below fail or degrade as each item says. One restart covers all of them; the TrueNAS rollout script does it for you.
  - **Plugin 1.7.0: Clean All folder operations.** Clean All's folder renames, merges and empty-folder removal now run inside Beets (`POST /webmanager/folder-op`). Until the restart they fail with a message naming plugin 1.7.0, and the folder is left unchanged.
  - **Plugin 1.8.1: Beets autotagger imports. Check that `musicbrainz` is in `plugins:`.** Imports now run Beets' own autotagger (`beet import -q`, with `--search-id` for a confirmed release). Startup adds `musicbrainz` to an existing `plugins:` entry and keeps every plugin already listed. If it cannot prove the edit safe, it leaves `config.yaml` unchanged and setup shows the warning `beets_config_plugins_not_added`; then add `musicbrainz` to `plugins:` by hand and restart the `beets` container. Since Beets 2.4 the importer finds no MusicBrainz candidates without it, so every album would be skipped as not matched.
  - **Plugin 1.9.0: Clean All folder steps.** It refuses a Clean All folder step with `BUSY` while an import runs instead of queueing it, and saves folder-step outcomes in `webmanager_operations.json` next to the Beets library database (the Beets config folder in the shipped Compose files).
  - **Plugin 1.10.0: import skip reasons.** Its import reply says why Beets skipped each folder. With an older plugin, skipped folders still reach the Review Queue, with the reason `not_matched`.
  - **Plugin 1.11.0: genre and tag writes.** On Beets 2.13 and later, genre edits now reach Beets' `genres` field and the file tags. `/webmanager/modify` now refuses a field this Beets has no column for (`UNSUPPORTED_FIELDS`), and fails with `WRITE_FAILED` when it cannot write the file tags. Album updates with tag writing on now also write their tracks' tags, so they rewrite every track file of the album and hold the Beets lock longer on large albums. `WRITE_FAILED` arrives after Beets has saved the change: the transaction is marked Failed, the library database already holds the new value, and the file keeps its old tag. Such a transaction cannot be rolled back yet. Make the music files writable by `PUID`:`PGID`, then apply the edit again (or edit the field back) to bring the file in line; see Troubleshooting §12.
  - **Plugin 1.12.0: album artwork.** Album artwork upload and "replace from URL" run inside Beets (`POST /webmanager/album-art`). Until the restart they fail with "Restart the beets container so it loads webmanager plugin 1.12.0; nothing was changed."
  - **Plugin 1.13.0: MBSync All. Keep `mbsync` in `plugins:`.** MBSync All (Library Cleanup, "Sync metadata") runs Beets' own mbsync inside Beets (`POST /webmanager/mbsync/library`). Until the restart it fails with "Restart the beets container so it loads webmanager plugin 1.13.0; nothing was changed."; without the `mbsync` plugin it fails with a message saying to enable it. It writes tags only if Beets' `import.write` says so, never moves files (whatever `import.move` says), and cannot be rolled back.
  - **Plugin 1.14.0: album rename and "move to library".** Album rename and "move to library" now run through `POST /webmanager/album-relocation`. Until the restart they fail with "Restart the beets container so it loads webmanager plugin 1.14.0; nothing was changed."
  - **Plugin 1.15.0: seeding torrents are linked, not moved (#332).** "Move to library" and album rename of an album in a preserved torrent source link the files (or copy them when a link is impossible) instead of moving them. Until the restart such a move is refused with "Restart the beets container so it loads webmanager plugin 1.15.0, which links a seeding torrent's files instead of moving them; nothing was changed." Albums outside a torrent source move as before.
- **No built-in `/data/...` library paths (#251).** Plex and playlist path matching no longer assume `/data/music` or `/data/media/music`, and `PLAYLIST_PATH_ROOT_ALIASES` now defaults to `MUSIC_ROOT` (it was `/data/music,/music`). With the documented default `MUSIC_ROOT=/music` nothing else changes. If `MUSIC_ROOT` is not `/music`, playlist entries whose paths start with `/music` no longer resolve, because `/music` is no longer a default alias. If Plex or your playlists use another library path, set `PLEX_MUSIC_ROOTS` (the path Plex sees the library at) and/or `PLAYLIST_PATH_ROOT_ALIASES` (for example `/music` plus the old path) under the Web Manager service's `environment:` in Compose and recreate the container. These are not application settings: the System page cannot save them, `/web-manager-data/.env` does not load them, and the shipped Compose files do not pass them through. `PLEX_MUSIC_ROOTS` can instead be set as `plex_music_roots` in the `plex:` section of the Beets `config.yaml`.
- **The shipped Compose files now deploy `image: ghcr.io/iranman/beets-web-manager:latest`.** v0.1.49's `docker-compose.yml` hard-coded `ghcr.io/iranman/beets-web-manager:stable`; unreleased work had switched it to `${BEETS_WEB_MANAGER_VERSION:-stable}`, which is reverted. `BEETS_WEB_MANAGER_VERSION` is no longer read from `.env` by the shipped files; to pin or roll back, replace `latest` in the `image:` line with an exact version such as `0.1.49`. The TrueNAS rollout script works with the literal `:latest` line without editing the Compose file or `.env`: it pulls `:latest`, deploys it only if its version label is `VERSION` (otherwise it stops with `latest_image_not_requested_version` before changing anything), and `--rollback` re-tags `:latest` locally to the recorded previous image, pulling it back by registry digest if it was pruned (also when the old container was created from a pinned tag and the Compose file now names `:latest`). A refused deploy or a dry run points the local `:latest` back at the image it named before the pull. The recreate runs `up --pull never`; if the container still lands on another image it is stopped (`recreated_image_unverified`). The rollout script (deploy, dry run and `--rollback`) therefore needs Docker Compose v2.22 or later; on an older Compose it stops with `compose_too_old` before changing anything. Compose files that pin `:<VERSION>` or use the variable keep working.
- **Restores now require a checksum manifest (#178).** `scripts/restore.sh` refuses an archive without `MANIFEST.txt` (made by an older `backup.sh`), and `deploy_truenas_web_manager.sh --rollback` refuses a backup folder without a full checksum list in `state-manifest.txt` (made by an older version of the script), both with reason code `backup_manifest_missing` and before anything is changed. To restore such a backup anyway, add `--allow-legacy-backup`; it prints an `UNVERIFIED BACKUP` warning.
- `scripts/backup.sh` and `scripts/restore.sh` changed. They now default to the stack folders `./beets` and `./web-manager` (or `--beets-config`/`--web-manager-data`/`--out`) instead of the container path `/config`, and the archive layout has `beets/` and `web-manager-data/` folders. `restore.sh` still reads archives from the old script, but only with `--allow-legacy-backup`, because they have no `MANIFEST.txt` (see the note above).
- The TrueNAS rollout script (`scripts/deploy_truenas_web_manager.sh`) now restarts the `beets` service when a release changes the webmanager plugin version, and fails a deploy that adds a new setup blocking reason. Its backups now hold Web Manager state and the Beets config; their diagnostic copies of the container environment are redacted.
- On first start, Web Manager removes host-side Compose values (`MUSIC_PATH`, `DOWNLOADS_PATH`, `BEETS_CONFIG_PATH`, `WEB_MANAGER_DATA_PATH`), Compose-pinned values (`PUID`, `PGID`, `TZ`, `WEBCONTROL_PORT`), container paths and retired keys (`PLAYLIST_DIR`, `BEETS_SQLITE_TIMEOUT`, `WEB_MANAGER_PATH`) from the saved settings file `/web-manager-data/.env`. A backup `.env.bak-migration-<timestamp>` is written next to it first. The System page and `/api/setup/status` (`settings_migration`) report how many keys were removed. Only key names are logged.
- The System page can no longer save `PUID`, `PGID`, `TZ`, `WEBCONTROL_PORT`, container paths (`BEETS_CONFIG`, `BEETS_LOG`, `MUSIC_ROOT`, `DOWNLOADS_ROOT`) or host paths. Every shipped Compose file sets these, so a saved value never took effect. Set them in Compose and recreate the container.
- Startup now adds `include_paths: yes` to the Beets `web:` block when the key is missing, so Beets returns file paths. An explicit `include_paths: no` is left alone and reported by setup (see Added). The plugin restart above covers this (it needed plugin 1.6.1).
- Startup no longer adds the recommended feature plugins to an existing `config.yaml`. Plugins added by earlier versions stay. To add the rest, use the recommended-plugins preview (see Added).
- If you saved edits from the Config page on v0.1.49 or earlier, those edits never reached `/config/config.yaml` (see Fixed). Apply them again.
- `docker-compose.yml` now forwards `BEETS_WEB_URL` and `BEETS_OUTBOUND_ALLOWLIST` from the Compose `.env` (defaults unchanged). It also sets `MUSIC_ROOT`, `DOWNLOADS_ROOT` and `BEETS_CONFIG` explicitly, and accepts the older host-path names `MUSIC_LIBRARY_PATH`, `DOWNLOAD_PATH` and `BEETS_WEB_MANAGER_DATA_PATH` as fallbacks.
- **AcoustID needs your own key.** The built-in fallback client key is gone. Set `ACOUSTID_API_KEY` (or the legacy `ACOUSTID_KEY`) to your own application key from https://acoustid.org/new-application. Without it, fingerprint lookups report `not_configured`: the evidence is unavailable, which is not the same as "no match", so ambiguous identities go to review. Setup shows "AcoustID not configured".
- **Outbound allowlist entries are now required for LAN, Tailscale and other non-public addresses.** An operator-configured service (Beets, Plex, Lidarr, slskd, qBittorrent, the PO provider, the AI provider) whose host resolves to a private, loopback, CGNAT/Tailscale (100.64.0.0/10), benchmarking or documentation address must be covered by a `BEETS_OUTBOUND_ALLOWLIST` entry. The documented `CIDR:port` form (for example `192.168.1.0/24:32400`) and `[IPv6]:port` / `[IPv6-CIDR]:port` entries now actually parse. A malformed entry is logged at startup and fails closed.
- **Sign-in rate limits now apply before the password is checked.** While a client IP, or the new account-wide bucket, is limited, even a correct password gets HTTP 429 until the window passes. The account bucket is set with `BEETS_AUTH_ACCOUNT_RATE_LIMIT` / `BEETS_AUTH_ACCOUNT_RATE_WINDOW` (default 100 failed attempts per 300 s across all IPs). Signed-in sessions and bearer-token clients are not affected.
- **`X-Forwarded-For` is read right to left** from a trusted proxy, skipping trusted hops. A reverse proxy that appends the client address (nginx `$proxy_add_x_forwarded_for`, Traefik, Caddy) works unchanged. A proxy that overwrites the header with a client-supplied value is no longer trusted for the leftmost entry.
- **Setup probes no longer reuse stored keys for a URL you type in.** `/api/setup/test/ai` and `/api/setup/test/plex` use the stored `OPENAI_API_KEY` / `PLEX_TOKEN` only for a signed-in caller testing the configured endpoint. To test a different URL, send the key with it, as the setup wizard already does.
- **Playlist URL import accepts only supported media hosts.** `POST /api/playlist/parse` with `source=url` now rejects URLs that are not on yt-dlp's allowlisted public media hosts (YouTube, SoundCloud and the other listed hosts), with a clear message. `ytsearch:` / `scsearch:` queries are unchanged.
- **Public URL fetches have a total deadline.** `BEETS_OUTBOUND_TOTAL_TIMEOUT_SECONDS` (default 60) bounds a whole reference-URL or artwork fetch, including redirects and the body read. A TLS certificate failure is now final and is no longer retried.
- **The setup env API shows a fixed `********` for configured secrets** instead of the first and last characters and the length. Use the password-confirmed reveal to see the value.
- **No-audio folder cleanup no longer scans `/tmp`, `/data/downloads` or `/download`.** It covers the music root, `DOWNLOADS_ROOT` and the `DOWNLOAD_PATH` mount.
- **Container image:**
  - `PUID` or `PGID` set to `0`, or to a non-numeric value, is refused at start with exit code 64.
  - The image no longer contains `git`, `pip` or `tests/`. `docker exec ... pip install` no longer works; build a derived image if you need extra packages.
  - In the hardened compose files (`docker-compose.full.yml` and the external-Beets example), `cap_drop: [ALL]` now adds back `CHOWN`, `SETUID` and `SETGID`. Without these, both files crash-looped at start. After the user switch, the app runs non-root with no effective capabilities, and `no-new-privileges` stays on.
  - On the read-only root filesystem, a custom `PUID`/`PGID` runs as that numeric uid:gid instead of remapping the built-in user. `/config` is chowned only when it is a mount, so the external-Beets example now starts.
- Web Manager no longer deletes audio files inside the music library except through an album cleanup the operator explicitly confirms with `DELETE ALBUM FILES` (see Security). Track removals and proven duplicates go to a restorable quarantine instead. Deletion outside the library is limited to the staging/download roots (no-audio folders, rejected preview-length downloads). Several endpoints now need an explicit confirmation or a preview's ids; requests without them are refused with a `code` (`confirmation_required`, `planned_ids_required`, `empty_selection`, `not_preview`, `requires_review`, `music_root_not_allowed`). Frontend flows that called these endpoints without those fields will show the refusal until they are updated.
- `POST /api/transactions/<id>/approve` now refuses an album-cleanup plan that deletes files unless the body has `confirm_delete_files: "DELETE ALBUM FILES"` (400 `confirmation_required`). In Library Changes, Approve on such a plan opens a dialog that stays disabled until that phrase is typed exactly; refusals and lost state races (409 `not_preview`) show what failed and what to do next. Row-only plans approve as before.
- Clean All's folder-safe renames run again, now through the transaction engine (`folder_cleanup_v1`, one audited transaction per folder, confined to `MUSIC_ROOT`). The import template pre-rename and the import Step 0b orphan pre-cleanup are no longer performed; they log `not_supported` and the import continues without them.
- The Web Manager data directory (`WEB_MANAGER_DATA_DIR`), `transactions.db`, any `*.db` file and the backup directories can no longer be deleted or moved by the staging-file helpers, even when they sit under a staging root.
- Internal playlist media cleanup (after a playlist import or library-wide removal) now removes Beets rows only and keeps the files; it needs an Approved transaction and runs once.
- **Download paths follow `DOWNLOADS_ROOT`.** The app used a fixed `/data/torrents/music` as its downloads root, while setup checked `DOWNLOADS_ROOT` (default `/downloads`). Now both use `DOWNLOADS_ROOT`, and the defaults derived from it changed: `TORRENT_SOURCE_ROOTS` and `QBIT_REPAIR_ALLOWED_ROOTS` default to `DOWNLOADS_ROOT`, `PLAYLIST_DOWNLOAD_ROOT` to `DOWNLOADS_ROOT/music/Playlist Downloads`, and `QBIT_PATH_ALIASES` to empty. The import-review path allowlist was `/data/torrents`, `/data/downloads` and `/tmp`; it is now `DOWNLOADS_ROOT` only. A `DOWNLOADS_ROOT`, `TORRENT_SOURCE_ROOTS` or `QBIT_REPAIR_ALLOWED_ROOTS` entry that is `/` or overlaps the music library (equal to `MUSIC_ROOT`, inside it, or containing it) is ignored with an error in the log, and such a `DOWNLOADS_ROOT` blocks setup. The slskd completed-download search now looks only under `DOWNLOADS_ROOT` and `TORRENT_SOURCE_ROOTS`; it no longer scans the parent of `DOWNLOADS_ROOT`, `/data/downloads`, `/download` or `/tmp`, and ignores transfer paths outside those roots. The no-audio folder sweep takes its downloads root from `DOWNLOADS_ROOT` (alias `DOWNLOAD_PATH`). A deployment that mounts downloads at `/data/torrents/music` must set `DOWNLOADS_ROOT` (and these variables, if it relied on the old defaults). Two consequences of `TORRENT_SOURCE_ROOTS` now defaulting to `DOWNLOADS_ROOT`:
  - A folder under `DOWNLOADS_ROOT` that the app did not create is treated as a preserved torrent source. `POST /api/import` with `move: true` on it returns 400, and imports from it copy, unless `ALLOW_TORRENT_SOURCE_MOVE=1`.
  - Download folders the app manages under `DOWNLOADS_ROOT` become eligible for the "already in library" source cleanup after an import.
- **Move All is refused.** `POST /api/library/move-all` still returns a `job_id`, but the job fails at once with `not_supported` and nothing in the library changes; its transaction is recorded Failed. Relocate albums one at a time instead. The duplicate-start guard (HTTP 409 `job_already_running`) applies only to jobs with an explicit `dedupe_key`; only Move All and MBSync All set one. For Move All it refuses a second start only in the moment before the first one fails. MBSync All works again (see Fixed).

### Fixed
- **"Move to library" no longer moves a seeding torrent out of its folder (#332; webmanager plugin 1.15.0).** Album rename and "move to library" (and every job that relocates an album) moved the files of an album in a preserved torrent source into `/music`, leaving the torrent folder empty and breaking seeding. They now apply the same rule as imports and rejected downloads (`TORRENT_SOURCE_ROOTS`, `ALLOW_TORRENT_SOURCE_MOVE`; a file whose folder, or any folder up to the torrent root, is preserved) to each track and the cover when the move is planned, and record `move` or `link` for each. Beets hard links a `link` track into the library with its own `Item.move()` (`HARDLINK`), or copies it (`COPY`) when a hard link is impossible (`/downloads` and `/music` on different filesystems or Docker volumes, or no permission to link); it never moves it. The torrent file keeps its path, bytes and inode, the Beets row points at the library file, and the transaction log says how many tracks were hard linked or copied. Rollback points each row back at its original once its inode, size and modification time prove it unchanged, then removes only the library file this move made (the original's inode for a link, or the size, time and inode recorded for it, kept up to date after each tag write) when no track uses it. Beets takes all of this from its own durable record of the move, never from the rollback request; a library file is only ever removed from inside the Beets library folder, re-checked by inode right before the unlink. When that record has expired (after 7 days, or sooner once 1000 newer plugin operations are on record), or the album was already rolled back, a linked or copied album cannot be rolled back automatically: the rollback refuses with `relocation_record_missing` and both the library files and the torrent originals stay exactly as they are. A replayed rollback removes nothing. It refuses and changes nothing when the original changed or is gone (`source_changed`) or the library file is not the one it made (`library_file_changed`).
